# Changelog

All notable changes to this project are documented in this file.

The format is based on [Keep a Changelog](https://keepachangelog.com/en/1.1.0/),
and this project adheres to [Semantic Versioning](https://semver.org/spec/v2.0.0.html).
Release notes on GitHub are generated from the section for each version.

## [Unreleased]

### Added

- **Workflow effect journal: exactly-once effects for irreversible nodes.** New tenant table
  `workflow_effect_journal` (V680) and `WorkflowEffectJournal`: `bulk_email`, `tool`, `crm_lead`,
  `device_control`, `media`, `output`, `workflow` and non-GET `http` nodes record `STARTED` before and
  `COMPLETED` (with the result) after their side effect, keyed `(run, node, iteration)`. A retry, crash
  re-run or approval resume of a completed key replays the recorded output with a trace line instead of
  repeating the effect; a `STARTED` row with no `COMPLETED` is "effect uncertain" and fails the node for a
  human decision (`app.workflow.effects.on-uncertain=fail|rerun`, default `fail`). The HTTP node sends the
  stable `runId:nodeId:iteration` key as `Idempotency-Key` on POST/PUT/PATCH unless the node sets one.
  `WorkflowRunRecoveryJob` now states in the failed run's message whether the crash left an uncertain effect
  or the run is resumable after its last completed node, and purges journal rows past
  `app.workflow.effects.journal.retention-days`. Off switch: `app.workflow.effects.journal.enabled=false`.
### Security

- **Taint now survives across the turns of a conversation.** A turn that became tainted (web page, email, recalled memory, inbound channel) leaves a small marker with the chat session, and every later turn of the same session starts tainted from it, including after the history is summarised. `GET`/`DELETE /api/agents/{id}/chat/sessions/{sid}/taint` show and clear it (audited). `app.agents.taint.session-persistence`, default on whenever the taint layer is.
- **Memory and knowledge writes are screened.** `remember_fact`, `remember_shared_fact` and graph-memory writes refuse injection phrases and credentials (one shared `ContentWriteScreen`); ingestion by upload, URL, connector or job refuses credentials and records a `screen_flags` marker on chunks that match an injection phrase, which `search_knowledge_base` results now show the model.
- **`app.agents.taint.inbound-channel-sources` (`off` | `all` | `non-owner`, default `off`)** and `InboundChannelTaintPolicy`: the seam for marking inbound channel turns, without choosing the policy.

### Fixed

- **Workflow step timeout no longer breaks slow nodes.** The workflow-level `stepTimeoutSeconds` (default 30 in every existing row, never enforced before) now bounds only fast node types (`http`, `condition`); agent, supervisor, sub-workflow, tool, code and other long-running nodes get a 600 s floor (`-Dvegaduta.workflow.longRunningStepTimeoutSeconds`, 0 = no limit). A node's own `timeoutSeconds` (1..3600) overrides both. A timed-out attempt is never retried, because its worker may still be running.
- **Workflow engine: duplicate side effects on retry, unenforced step timeout,
  approvals inside loop bodies.** (1) The default retry path no longer retries
  irreversible nodes (`bulk_email`, `tool`, `crm_lead`, `device_control`,
  `media`, sub-`workflow`, non-GET `http`) unless the node sets
  `retryUnsafe=true`, matching the per-node error strategy. (2) Saving a graph
  whose loop, fan-out or sub-flow body is an `approval` / `human_input` node or
  has `requiresApproval=true` is now rejected (a paused run cannot resume at the
  right iteration); the "needs exactly one Trigger node" message now says "at
  least one" (several triggers were always allowed). (3) `stepTimeoutSeconds` is
  now enforced per node attempt by the in-process engine (the attempt is
  interrupted and fails), parallel branches are cancelled when a sibling fails,
  and the parallel join is bounded.
- **Untrusted-content taint now covers auto-injected memory recall and the workflow `knowledge` node.** A non-empty recalled-facts block injected into the prompt marks the turn tainted (source `memory_recall`; sinks need approval in enforce mode, recorded in observe mode) via `TurnTaintSeeds`, bound to the turn's taint key when tools are resolved. The `knowledge` workflow node wraps retrieved chunks in the untrusted-data spotlight with numbered source citations, skips with a clear result on an invalid `collectionId`, and clamps `topK` to 1..20.
- **Tenant isolation hardening.** A JWT or API-key tenant claim naming the `public` (or a reserved) schema is rejected with 403 instead of running tenant queries in the legacy schema (webhook paths that set `public` deliberately are unchanged); the tenant status guard now fails closed on a schema-check SQL error (`app.tenant.status-fail-open-on-schema-check-error=true` restores the old behavior) and rejects claims the schema-name sanitizer would rewrite; tenants whose startup schema migration failed get 403 until a background retry (`app.tenancy.migrate-retry-interval`) migrates them; a non-Keycloak JWT issuer with neither an azp allowlist nor a required audience now fails startup (`app.security.jwt.allow-unrestricted-audience=true` opts out).
- **Workflow editor hint** now says a workflow needs "at least one" Trigger node (it said "exactly
  one"; the validator allows several). Control-UI translation keys were back-filled with English
  fallbacks in every locale file.
- **Agent scratchpad: discoverability, lifecycle and an operator view.** The
  four `scratchpad_*` tools are deferred (behind `search_tools`), so a model
  that never searched for tools never learned the notepad existed. A new
  standing directive (`directive.scratchpad`, kill-switch
  `app.chat.directives.scratchpad.enabled`) now tells the turn "you have a
  private scratchpad - persist plans and large extractions there" on exactly
  the turns where the pad is reachable: on the tool list outright, or offered
  and deferred behind `search_tools`. `scratchpad_list` results are
  size-aware - key, scope, `byte_size`, a bounded preview and `total_bytes`,
  bodies still fetched via `scratchpad_read` - and `scratchpad_write` to a
  full pad now evicts the least-recently-updated note instead of failing
  (`app.tools.scratchpad.evict-oldest-on-cap`, default on; the tool result
  names the dropped key). Session-pad notes age out lazily on writes
  (`app.tools.scratchpad.session-note-ttl`, default `30d`; the agent
  long-term pad never expires unless `agent-note-ttl` is set). Operators get a
  read-only REST window, `GET /api/agents/{agentId}/scratchpad[?session=...]`
  and `GET .../scratchpad/{key}` (VIEWER access; `session=agent` or omitted
  reads the long-term pad).

### Fixed

- **Auto-injected retrieval and inbound channel turns now propagate taint to tool gates.**
  Previously, only explicit `UNTRUSTED_EXTERNAL` tool calls (such as `fetch_web_page` or
  `search_knowledge_base`) marked a turn as tainted. Auto-injected context bypassed the taint gate:
  - `ChatPromptAssembler` now marks the turn tainted with `recall_related_facts` whenever
    long-term memory recall injects stored (private or tenant-shared) facts into the prompt,
    while owner-authored static blocks (persona/profile/notes) remain clean.
  - The workflow `knowledge` node now wraps retrieved context with `Spotlight.wrap` delimiters
    and per-chunk source citations rather than raw string concatenation. Downstream agent nodes
    detecting spotlight-delimited input are marked tainted with `untrusted_input`.
  - Inbound non-owner entry points across omnichannel adapters (WhatsApp, Telegram, Twilio,
    Signal, Matrix, Email, Discord, Slack, Teams, X, MCP, and Studio App) now mark `TurnContext.taint()`
    with `inbound:<channel>`.
  - `TurnContext` now carries its own `TurnTaint` record, and `AgentToolProvider` binds it to
    the turn's step counter during tool resolution, ensuring tool guards and taint gates evaluate
    sink permissions against auto-injected and inbound taint.
- **Workflow retries no longer duplicate irreversible side effects.** The
  legacy retry path only excluded `output` nodes, so `bulk_email`, `tool`,
  `crm_lead`, `device_control`, `media`, sub-workflow and non-GET `http`
  nodes were each attempted up to three times by default — a retry could
  double a send, lead or device action when the first attempt had already
  reached the wire. `RetryPolicy` now shares `NodeErrorStrategy`'s
  irreversible-type list (including on the Temporal engine, which dispatches
  those nodes on a single-attempt activity stub), GET/HEAD/OPTIONS calls
  stay retryable, and a node can still opt back in with
  `"retryUnsafe": true`. Tool node calls are additionally claimed through
  the output dedup guard keyed on (run, node, loop index + resolved input),
  so a replayed attempt is suppressed instead of re-invoking the tool.

- **Python Agent SDK: audit-chain ordering, truncation anchors and approval
  defaults.** `evidence_bundle` (and `verify(sort_by_seq=True)`) verify in
  `chain.seq` order, so a concurrently written log stored out of order is no
  longer reported broken (Java parity). `verify`/`evidence_bundle` take optional
  `expected_first_seq`/`expected_first_prev`/`expected_last_seq`/
  `expected_head_hash` anchors to detect prefix/suffix deletion, and the bundle
  exposes `head_hash`/`last_seq`. `ApprovalGate` now defaults to
  `FourEyesAuthorizer` (opt out with `authorizer=allow_any`); resumption
  failures store the exception type and a bounded, redacted message instead of
  raw text; `ApprovalStore.update` documents the compare-and-set requirement for
  multi-process use.
- **`tenant_id=public` claims can no longer route into the legacy shared
  schema.** A JWT or API key asserting the `public` tenant used to pass the
  schema-exists fallback (Postgres always has `public`) and land tenant
  queries in the shared schema. `TenantSchemaResolver` now refuses it like
  any reserved name, `TenantStatusGuardImpl` rejects it explicitly, and its
  SQL-error path fails closed instead of open. Deployments on a non-Keycloak
  OIDC issuer must also configure `app.keycloak.allowed-azp` or
  `app.security.jwt.required-audience`, or the boot refuses to start rather
  than accept tokens with no client-bound check.

- **Python Agent SDK 0.2.0 (breaking for 0.1.x): approval decisions are
  compare-and-set.** `ApprovalStore.update(request, expected_status)` returns
  whether the write applied; `SqliteApprovalStore` runs it as `UPDATE ... WHERE
  id=? AND status=?` and `ApprovalGate` routes approve, reject, expiry and the
  result write through it, so two gates, connections or processes sharing a
  database can no longer both approve a request and run its resumption twice
  (the loser gets `ApprovalAlreadyDecidedException`). A custom store whose
  `update` lacks `expected_status` still works but makes the gate warn. With
  the four-eyes default and the failure-result redaction above, this is why the
  package moves from `0.1.0` to `0.2.0`: code that relied on self-approval must
  pass `authorizer=allow_any`. `Mandate.is_expired` is now true at the expiry
  instant (Java parity), mandates are validated when decoded, and
  `verify_chain` checks each hop was issued by its parent's subject. `Mandate.encode()` now emits the Java wire text exactly (`exp` URL-encoded, no trailing `;`). See
  "Release notes" in `sdk/python-agent/README.md`. Not tagged or published.
- **Java Agent SDK: `JdbcApprovalStore` approvals were not compare-and-set.**
  It inherited `ApprovalStore.saveIfStatus`'s non-atomic find-then-save default,
  so two gates on one database could both read `PENDING`, both approve, and
  execute the action twice. It now overrides it with one `UPDATE ... WHERE id=?
  AND status=?` and checks the row count. The gate's expiry-on-read uses the
  same compare-and-set, so a decision that lands between the read and the
  write is no longer overwritten by `EXPIRED`, and a failed execution stores
  and audits the exception type plus a bounded, redacted message (matching the
  Python SDK) instead of `e.toString()`. `ApprovalStore` documents the
  atomicity requirement; `ApprovalDecisionAuthorizer.allowAny()` is the
  explicit opt-out of the four-eyes default (`permitAll()` stays as an alias).
  The Java default itself does not change (four-eyes was already on), so the
  Java version stays `0.1.1`.
- **`add_to_knowledge_base` could be relevance-trimmed from a turn while its
  search siblings stayed.** The write tool was missing from both standing-
  capability lists (`ALWAYS_RELEVANT_TOOL_NAMES` and the tool-search always-on
  default) that exempt `search_knowledge_base` and `list_knowledge_collections`
  from trimming, so an agent with knowledge-base write enabled could see only
  the read tools on a crowded or small-model turn and report "I can only search
  collections". The write/read pairing is now asserted in
  `AgentToolProviderCapTest`.
- **Workflow approval gates are refused inside loop, fan-out, parallel and
  sub-flow bodies.** A human-decision halt (approval node, human-input node,
  or `requiresApproval` on a body node) parks the run with a resume envelope
  carrying the node id and input but no iteration cursor, so a resumed loop
  would run only the halted item and drop the rest, and sub-flow body nodes
  are not in the main graph's node table at all. `WorkflowApprovalPlacement`
  now rejects these placements at save time (the same rule
  `WorkflowWaitPlacement` already applied to Wait nodes) until the resume
  payload grows an iteration cursor.
- **Parallel branches fail fast.** `ParallelBranchRunner` joined branches in
  index order, so a failure in the last branch surfaced only after every
  slower earlier branch had finished. It now joins in completion order and
  returns on the first failure.
- **Execution-engine copy is honest about the legacy engine.** The platform
  pages and configuration docs previously claimed both engines read the same
  per-workflow value. Retry counts and the step ceiling do apply on both, but
  `stepTimeoutSeconds` is enforced only by the durable Temporal engine; the
  default in-process engine is now labelled best-effort (no per-step timeout,
  crashed runs are reaped `FAILED` rather than resumed).
- **Release workflow gates the tag before spending a build matrix.** `prepare`
  now refuses tags on commits that are not ancestors of `main` and versions
  with no `CHANGELOG.md` section (the release notes generate from it), instead
  of discovering both late. `verify` waits up to 150 minutes for the tagged
  commit's `CI OK`/`CodeQL OK` - main-push CI serializes on a concurrency
  group, so a release tagged right after a merge can sit behind a full earlier
  run - and fails fast after 10 attempts when the commit has no workflow run
  at all instead of polling an hour for a check that will never exist. New
  pre-flight for humans: `scripts/ci/tag-ready.sh <sha>` reports whether a
  commit is release-ready before tagging (`docs/maintainers/releasing.md`).
- **A tenant-less sign-in no longer pops "sign out and back in" on every API
  call.** Core's 403 "account not linked to an organization" is a normal
  pre-onboarding state (OnboardingGate owns that UI), but it reached the
  global error toast - and its advice loops forever for an account whose
  organization was never created. `toJson` now classifies it as a guard state
  (`tenant_missing`) that throws for callers without raising the popup. Also
  documented that non-localhost browser access requires HTTPS: the realm
  marks session cookies `Secure`, so plain-`http://` LAN/hostname installs
  silently drop them and the sign-in form loops.

### Added

- **Java Agent SDK: audit-chain truncation anchors, cross-SDK fixtures.**
  `HashChainedAuditSink.verify(events, verifier, ChainAnchors)` and the
  `EvidenceBundle` builder (`expectedFirstSeq`/`expectedFirstPrev`/
  `expectedLastSeq`/`expectedHeadHash`) pin either end of a chain to values
  stored outside the log, so a deleted prefix or suffix (invisible to the log
  alone) fails verification with a message naming the anchor. The bundle
  exposes `firstSeq()`, `firstPrev()`, `lastSeq()`, `headHash()` over the whole
  chain, and the EU AI Act JSON Lines/Markdown export carries `firstSeq`,
  `firstPrev`, `lastSeq`, `headHash` under the same names as the Python SDK
  (`headHash` was the last event of the reporting period, now the head of the
  chain). The canonical form is unchanged. `sdk/testdata/cross-sdk` holds a
  signed chain and a mandate chain written by each SDK; both test suites verify
  both, including after anchoring, and the Java test asserts it reproduces
  Python's hashes and HMAC signatures byte for byte.

- **Piper: a local, keyless TTS provider for the voice agent.** Until now
  every voice reply needed a paid hosted key (ElevenLabs preferred, Sarvam
  fallback); a deployment with neither fell back to browser-side speech.
  `VOICE_AGENT_TTS_PROVIDER=piper` runs `piper-tts` in the worker via
  `app/tts_piper.py` - no API key, no egress. The image bakes the
  `en_US-lessac-medium` voice (sha256-verified at build); `PIPER_VOICE` /
  `PIPER_MODEL_DIR` / `PIPER_SPEAKER_ID` / `PIPER_SAMPLE_RATE` tune it, and
  `services/voice-agent/piper_voices_download.py` fetches additional voices.
- **Docling-first document conversion for knowledge ingestion.** The new
  `services/docling` FastAPI adapter wraps IBM's Docling parser behind the
  sidecar's exact `/extract` contract (`X-Internal-Token` auth, `DOCLING_*`
  env). Set `DOCLING_BASE_URL` (Compose: `--profile docling`) and supported
  uploads convert there first - layout-aware PDF, tables, plus formats the
  sidecar never handled (`.pptx`/`.xlsx`/`.csv`/`.tiff`/`.bmp`, which also
  widen the upload allowlist while configured). A Docling transport/5xx
  failure falls back to the sidecar extractor unless
  `app.knowledge.docling.fallback-to-sidecar=false`; a 4xx still means the
  file was the problem and surfaces unchanged. Empty `DOCLING_BASE_URL` =
  byte-for-byte the old behaviour.
- **Composio hosted MCP connectors in the catalog.** `composio-connect`
  (`https://connect.composio.dev/mcp`, `x-consumer-api-key` header) and
  `rube` (`https://rube.app/mcp`, `Authorization: Bearer`) join the MCP
  gallery as remote entries - 1000+ SaaS apps (Gmail, Slack, GitHub,
  Notion, Linear, HubSpot...) through one per-tenant registration, no
  local subprocess.
- **n8n bridge examples and docs.** `examples/integrations/n8n/` ships an
  importable VegaDūta workflow template (HTTP node -> n8n webhook), an
  importable n8n workflow (HTTP Request -> platform agent), and a README
  covering both directions.
- **Academy: scriptable how-to video pipeline.** `academy/` holds lessons
  authored as machine-readable scripts (narration text + Playwright
  actions per scene); `academy/tools/` narrates them with edge-tts and
  assembles captioned mp4s with ffmpeg, and `tests/e2e/video/` records the
  console footage. A UI change means re-running a script, not re-shooting
  a video. Curriculum index: `academy/lessons/INDEX.md`.
- **Knowledge upload UI offers the Docling-extended formats.** The console's
  upload picker now lists `.pptx`/`.xlsx`/`.csv`/`.tiff`/`.tif`/`.bmp`
  alongside the existing types (PPT/XLS badges); a deployment without the
  `docling` profile still rejects them server-side with a clear error.

## [0.1.12] - 2026-10-26

### Fixed

- **Release images no longer depend on a live upstream version feed.** The
  Chrome-for-Testing install inside `core` (full variant) and
  `mcp-stdio-runner-browser` resolved `chrome@stable` through
  `googlechromelabs.github.io/.../last-known-good-versions.json` at build time;
  that endpoint went down mid-release (HTTP 200, empty body) and failed every
  affected image leg. The version is now pinned literally
  (`CHROME_FOR_TESTING_VERSION`, `156.0.8078.47`), so builds skip the
  resolution fetch entirely; the pin is bumped deliberately with each release.
- **Personal/lite installs never enabled the tool-sandbox client.** `scripts/vegaduta`
  started the tool-sandbox container with the `tools` choice but exported
  `TOOL_SANDBOX_ENABLED` only from the profile list - which `tools` never joins on
  single-box tiers - so core kept `TOOL_SANDBOX_ENABLED=false` and `create_custom_tool`
  (agents authoring their own tools) never resolved on any personal or lite install.
  The launcher now exports it whenever the sandbox container ships.
- **Approvals that park a task now actually resume it.** An approval request recorded
  without the chat turn's origin (a caller with no conversation subject key, or one recorded
  before the origin fields existed) used to show "The agent was not restarted automatically
  after this decision" and then answer a typed "carry on" with no memory of the task. The
  continuation and the outcome note now derive the conversation from the fields every request
  does carry - agent, session, tenant, subject - verify the derived session really holds the
  conversation, and restate its last request when the goal is missing. A wrong derivation
  (a caller whose subject key is not its tenant subject) is still refused.

### Added

- **Desktop watch pops out of the console tab.** The live-screen dock now has a "Pop out"
  button (browsers with the document Picture-in-Picture API) that moves the feed into a real
  always-on-top OS window, so the console tab can be minimized and the desktop left free for
  the agent to work in while the person watches from the floating window; frames keep polling
  while the tab is hidden in that mode.
- **Personal installs turn on deferred tool search.** A one-person agent resolves far more
  tools than a small local model should see at once; with `app.tools.search.enabled` off a
  relevance-trimmed tool was unreachable for the turn and the model reported "limited tools"
  for capabilities it actually has. The personal profile now enables `search_tools` /
  `call_tool` (env: `TOOL_SEARCH_ENABLED`) so every tool stays reachable and the model is told
  to search instead of claiming a capability is missing.
- **Desktop runs are journaled per step and proposed as recipes.** Each replayed recipe step
  writes its own `tool_call_log` row under the calling turn (the sidecar path and non-secret
  fields only - resolved `{{secret.*}}` values are never journaled), so the run journal, the
  Issues portal and the learning loops see which step did what instead of one opaque
  `run_desktop_recipe` row. New `app.learning.desktop-recipes.enabled` (on in the `personal`
  profile): a finished turn that ran two or more `local_desktop_*` actions files a
  "Save this desktop run as a recipe?" proposal; approving it materializes the recipe through
  the same save-from-run path the button uses.

## [0.1.11] - 2026-10-26

- Shared-agent voice works without server speech services: the public share page's call panel now offers one-tap downloads of the on-device models (whisper speech-to-text ~45 MB + kokoro voice ~90 MB) on the visitor's device. With whisper installed, hold-to-talk transcribes locally and only the text crosses the wire (the shared chat endpoint), so talking works even when the platform's STT, TTS and LiveKit legs are all down; any miss falls back to the server voice turn as before. Loading indicators on the share page were strengthened (larger spinner + clearer thinking bubble).
### Added

- **`docs/api/openapi.json` now shows the real security of the local-auth routes.** The generator only mirrored the main security chain, so every `/api/auth/local/*` route claimed `bearerAuth` - including the anonymous `login`, `setup`, `refresh`, `config` and `unlock` sign-in routes. The spec now matches `LocalAuthSecurityConfig`: only `change-password` and the unlock-management routes require a session; the rest are public (and no longer claim an `Idempotency-Key` that anonymous calls never replay-protected).
- **Password reset for a locked-out personal install.** A new "Forgot password?" link on the local sign-in screen asks core (`POST /api/auth/local/reset-request`) to mint a one-time reset link and write it, owner-readable only, to `password-reset.txt` in the auth data directory - file access on the machine is the proof of ownership, same model as the first-run setup link, so the endpoint never returns the link over the wire. `scripts/vegaduta reset-password [EMAIL]` (and `.\scripts\vegaduta.ps1 reset-password` on Windows) prints the link; opening it runs the existing `/login?setup=` flow, which sets a new password and signs in. The request is throttled per client address, and a blank e-mail resolves to the install's only account.
- **Code Intelligence analysis suite** (`services/code-intel` + `core/codeintel`):
  twelve new deterministic, read-only endpoints and matching agent tools, on top
  of richer extraction - symbols now carry signature/doc/test flags and the graph
  gains value/type `references` edges and an HTTP route table.
  New endpoints (`/repos/{id}/...`): `map` (PageRank-ranked skeleton of the repo
  under a char budget - the agent's first call to learn a codebase's shape),
  `symbols` (name-path search), `symbols/{qname}` (exact source body),
  `outline`, `references/{symbol}` (all inbound mentions, not just calls),
  `graph/implementations/{symbol}` (recursive subclass tree),
  `graph/path` (BFS call chain between two symbols), `dead-code` (zero-inbound-edge
  review candidates), `tests/{symbol}` (test-side linkage), `api-surface`
  (FastAPI/Flask/Express/Spring/Go/Rust route extraction), `hotspots` (git churn
  x size) and `import-cycles` (file-level SCCs). All surfaced to agents as
  `code_map`, `find_symbols`, `symbol_detail`, `code_outline`, `code_references`,
  `code_implementations`, `code_path`, `code_dead_code`, `code_tests`,
  `code_api_surface`, `code_hotspots`, `code_import_cycles` - per-agent
  toggleable in the workbench's Tools grid like every other tool, and scoped by
  the same `allowedCodeIntelRepoIds` grant (empty = unrestricted for read tools).
  The MCP bridge (`mcp_bridge.py`) exposes the same twelve to MCP clients in
  read-only mode. Route extraction covers FastAPI/Starlette/Flask/Django,
  Express/Koa/Hono/Nest, Spring MVC/JAX-RS, net/http/Gin/Echo/chi, axum and
  ASP.NET attributes.
- Workbench: the Code Intelligence section's per-agent repository access editor
  now shows whenever code-intel is reachable, not only for workspace-enabled
  agents - a read-only agent's `allowedCodeIntelRepoIds` was previously
  unreachable in the UI. Hint copy now states the two empty-set semantics
  honestly (read tools treat it as unrestricted; workspace tools as deny-all).
- **On-device media generation, out of the box.** The edge tool surface gains
  `local_generate_image` (Stable Diffusion Turbo via onnxruntime-web/WebGPU, ~2.4 GB
  model) and `local_generate_audio` (MusicGen-small via transformers.js, ~1.8 GB
  model) - an agent running on an account-less laptop can produce images and short
  music/sound clips with no provider key and no data leaving the browser. Both are
  capability-gated (image needs WebGPU; audio needs WebGPU or an 8GB+ device),
  consent-gated (model downloads start only from the Edge console's download rows;
  a tool call on a device without the model refuses with a pointer, never a silent
  download), and report into a session media gallery in the Edge console where the
  person can preview and download artifacts.
- **Windows support in `desktop-agent`** (`services/desktop-agent`): `win32`
  hosts previously fell through to the Linux path and every action answered
  501. Input now goes through user32 `SendInput`/`mouse_event` via an inline C#
  interop class, screen capture and monitor frames through
  `System.Drawing.CopyFromScreen`, and the clipboard through
  `Get/Set-Clipboard` - all via the bundled Windows PowerShell, no external
  installs. `/ui` accessibility tree remains macOS-only (honest 501 elsewhere).
  README gains a Task Scheduler autostart recipe.
- First-token latency: the three pre-turn reads (session history, tool resolution, memory recall) now share a `app.chat.pre-turn-max-wait` deadline (default 3500ms). A stalled MCP connect or unresponsive sidecar used to hold the model call open indefinitely - the console showed silence for minutes; now a timed-out read is dropped and the turn proceeds with a reduced prompt. Set `APP_CHAT_PRE_TURN_MAX_WAIT=0` to restore unbounded waits.
- **Local-model context self-heal.** Some Ollama runners (notably MLX on Apple Silicon) ignore the per-request `num_ctx` and load the model's 4k default, silently truncating every agent prompt - the model then invented tool names and fabricated work because it never saw its schemas. On each model warm the platform now compares the loaded window (`/api/ps`) against `app.llm.ollama.num-ctx`; when it is smaller, a `model-ctxNk` variant is derived via `/api/create` (weights reused, no download), the serving row is repointed, and the variant warmed. Set `APP_LLM_OLLAMA_AUTO_CONTEXT_FIX=false` to keep warn-only behaviour.
- Fixed live voice transcription: sidecar pinned PyAV 19, which removed the `metadata_errors` kwarg `faster-whisper` passes to `av.open`, so every `/transcribe` call 400'd and calls hung at "Transcribing". Pinned `av==15.1.0` (newest release that keeps the kwarg).
- Approval cards for `local_desktop_*` and `run_desktop_recipe` calls now show readable labels ("Open an app, file, or link") instead of raw wire names.
- "The task it was working on" on approval cards now shows the user's actual message: the console's `<app_context>` preamble is stripped when core records the goal and when the web renders already-parked rows.
- Playground call controls: the screen-share button is always visible while connected (explains itself when on-device sight is not configured), and "Call + camera" renders disabled with a reason instead of disappearing for text-only models.

- Fixed voice calls on single-box installs: LiveKit now advertises the loopback/node IP (`LIVEKIT_NODE_IP`, default 127.0.0.1) instead of the WAN address discovered by STUN, which browsers could not reach - the media path died at "Media connection blocked".

- Voice calling (livekit + voice-agent) is now available on the personal and lite tiers: `personal --profile voice` / `up --lite --profile voice` start both services and imply `rag` for Whisper STT (previously only the full stack declared them).

- Personal install footprint: default memory ceilings tightened (tool-sandbox 1g to 512m, sidecar 2g to 1536m, browser-runner 2g to 1g; all stay env-overridable) and the sidecar build gained `SIDECAR_BAKE_EXTRA_MODELS=false` to slim a local image by ~550MB.


### Added

- **Desktop tools are always offered when enabled**: `local_desktop_*` (and `run_desktop_recipe`) join the always-relevant tool set and the small-model intent table gets a desktop family — a request like "play minesweeper" or "open Notes" shares no word with `local_desktop_click`, and a relevance-trimmed turn hid the whole capability (the model then correctly answered "I have no desktop tools"). Only applies where the desktop sidecar is already opted in.
- **Desktop recipes (personal tier, opt-in)**: a finished desktop run can be saved as a `desktop_recipe` — a placeholder-only replay of the `local_desktop_*` sequence (`open`/`activate`/`press`/`click`/`type`/`key`/`wait`), recorded from the run's tool journal with the same "no secrets or user values stored" rules as browser recipes (`{{param}}`/`{{secret.NAME}}`, literal typed text refused unless explicitly allowed). `run_desktop_recipe` replays it deterministically with per-step verification and a pause-before-irreversible gate; it sits behind `app.assistant.desktop.recipes.enabled` (`DESKTOP_RECIPES_ENABLED`, default off) plus the existing `app.assistant.desktop.approval-required` pin.
- **Monitor-or-auto replay**: each recipe carries a `default_mode` (`AUTO`/`MONITOR`); MONITOR paces the replay (`monitor-step-delay-ms`, default 800 ms) so a person can follow it live on the console's desktop monitor (`GET /api/desktop/monitor/frame` → the sidecar's new `GET /frame`, `app.assistant.desktop.monitor.enabled`, default off). Browser recipes gain the matching "Watch" affordance — the console polls the run's tool-call journal (`watchSessionId` in the `run_browser_recipe` result) — which also works on lite/full tiers.

### Added

- **Agents can download web documents into the knowledge base.** `add_to_knowledge_base`
  gained an optional `url` argument: the platform downloads the page/document server-side
  (SSRF-guarded, redirects re-validated, 5 MB cap) and runs it through the exact upload
  pipeline - a PDF/DOCX/HTML/Markdown file gets a document row, dedup and text extraction,
  plain text lands as a fetched note. `POST /api/knowledge/ingest-url` is the same flow for
  the UI/API. Knowledge write is now configurable per agent: `PATCH
  /api/agents/{id}/knowledge-write` opts in, `GET`/`PATCH /knowledge-write-access` manages
  the deny-by-default writable-collection grant list, and the agent workbench shows both
  under Knowledge collections.
- **Enabling a built-in MCP server that can't be served connects a per-tenant copy.** A
  catalog "Activate" for a stateful static server (puppeteer, playwright-vision, memory,
  sequential-thinking) used to silently do nothing when the deployment had no shared static
  connection (no `mcp` profile, e.g. lite) or the server was hidden by the cross-tenant
  exposure policy. The registration now upgrades itself to the catalog's own launch spec and
  connects through `mcp-stdio-runner` - each tenant gets an isolated server process. New
  env: `MCP_STATIC_STATELESS_SERVERS`, `MCP_STATIC_SHARED_STATEFUL_ENABLED`.

- **SDK publishing ready.** The Java Agent SDK moves to `0.1.1` (the 0.1.0 coordinate is
  retired: `sdk-v0.1.0`'s Central Portal upload was rejected because central-publishing-maven-plugin
  0.7.0 produced a bundle where no artifact directory held a `.pom`; the now-pinned 0.12.0 stages
  `io/github/cd-lc/<artifact>/<version>/<artifact>-<version>.pom` correctly - verified by a local
  dry-run bundle). The parent pom gains the `<organization>` block, both Python packages ship the
  `NOTICE` file (Apache-2.0 §4) via `license-files`, and `docs/maintainers/releasing-sdk.md` gains a
  credential-free bundle dry-run plus the incident write-up. Publish paths: tag `sdk-v0.1.1` →
  SDK release workflow → Central Portal "Publish"; tag `pysdk-v0.1.0` → Python SDK release → PyPI
  (`SDK_PUBLISH_PYPI` is already `true`; the `pypi` environment/trusted publisher are one-time PyPI
  setup); `restsdk-v*` + `SDK_PUBLISH_NPM` for the generated REST clients once the npm name and
  `NPM_TOKEN` exist.
- **Each agent gets a persistent scratchpad.** Four deferred built-in tools -
  `scratchpad_write`, `scratchpad_read`, `scratchpad_list` and `scratchpad_delete` - give an agent an
  agent-authored notepad: session-scoped working notes plus an agent-long-term pad. Caps:
  `app.tools.scratchpad.max-notes` (per agent+session, default 200) and
  `app.tools.scratchpad.max-note-bytes` (per note, default 65536); the platform switch is
  `app.tools.scratchpad.enabled` and the per-agent toggle `tools.scratchpad.enabled` in the behaviour
  settings catalog. New env: `APP_TOOLS_SCRATCHPAD_ENABLED`, `APP_TOOLS_SCRATCHPAD_MAX_NOTES`,
  `APP_TOOLS_SCRATCHPAD_MAX_NOTE_BYTES`.

### Fixed

- **Fresh installs and upgrades no longer resolve the wrong release.** Publishing `sdk-v0.1.1` made it GitHub's `/releases/latest`, so `install.sh`, `scripts/vegaduta`, `vegaduta.ps1` and the `/downloads` page all pointed at the SDK release - which has no installer assets, no `vX.Y.Z` tag and no `VegaDuta-Setup-*` downloads. All four now read the releases list and pick the newest platform `vX.Y.Z` tag, skipping component releases (`sdk-*`, `pysdk-*`, `restsdk-*`).
- **Mid-loop tool-call narration no longer leaks into streamed replies** when a provider reports `stop` (or no reason) on a round that actually ended in tool calls - common on Ollama and other OpenAI-compatible local servers. `TerminalRoundStreamAdvisor` now classifies the round by the chunk's own `hasToolCalls()`, the authoritative signal, not the provider's `finish_reason` metadata.
- **Turn start no longer waits on catalog screening.** When deferral is active
  (`app.tools.search.*`), each deferred tool's name/description/schema was judged by the
  guardrail model BEFORE the turn's first model call - ~90 sequential calls on a 59-tool catalog,
  over two minutes of "Gathering context" on a local model. Two changes: platform-authored tool
  text skips the semantic judge (it is repo-authored; the judge is for MCP/custom descriptions)
  and the remaining screen runs on a background virtual thread - only `search_tools`/`call_tool`
  join it, so a turn that never searches pays nothing.
- **`browser_navigate` now tells the model the sandboxed browser is invisible and inaudible to
  the person.** The reply is directed to describe it as the agent's own browser (never "your
  Chrome"), to say when `local_desktop_*` control is unavailable instead of silently substituting
  the sandbox, and to finish visual tasks with `browser_screenshot` so the person sees proof of
  what actually happened.
- **A turn parked on a human approval no longer asks the model to write its closing reply.** The
  tool loop now ends the round the marker lands in (`returnDirect`), and the person reads the
  platform's fixed pause text (new prompt key `message.approval.parked`) - a structured pause,
  not model prose - while the pending approval stays in the inbox and the journal. Queued
  post-park calls are answered with the `approval_parked` marker result.
- **A streamed (FAST) turn emits only the tool loop's terminal round.** Mid-loop narration -
  the assistant text of rounds that ended in `tool_calls` - is held and dropped; the final
  synthesis round arrives as one burst once its finish reason proves it terminal. New:
  `app.chat.parity.fast-terminal-rounds-only` (default `true`; `false` restores per-round
  streaming).
- **A streamed (FAST) turn now has a total wall-clock ceiling.** The idle watchdog
  (`stream-idle-timeout`) only bounds model silence and re-arms on every tool round, so a turn
  that kept calling tools (each call answering quickly) could run for hours. One streamed turn
  is now capped at `app.llm.http.fast-turn-timeout` (default 600 s, tenant/agent
  `turnTimeoutSeconds` replaces it, an agent-job deadline still ends it sooner, `0` disables):
  past the ceiling the stream is cut, the partial answer stays stored as interrupted, and the
  same "steps that ran" notice a buffered timeout emits is appended. New env:
  `LLM_HTTP_FAST_TURN_TIMEOUT`.
- **A turn's tool-round budget now counts all of its passes together.** Each continuation
  and tool-call-retry pass used to start a fresh tool loop with the round cap refilled, so a
  turn could spend its budget several times over; every pass's transcript now carries the
  rounds earlier passes already ran (`ToolLoopTranscript.roundsSpentBefore`).
- **`call_tool` refusals are bounded even without an agent identity.** The consecutive
  refusal ceiling only engaged for instances built with an `agentId`; the no-identity path
  could refuse forever. After the ceiling it now returns the same marked budget-exhausted
  result, which ends the loop the same way.
- **Tool results keep their platform marker through a `{"result": ...}` wrap.** The JSON
  wrap minted a new String, losing the `ToolResultMarker` identity (a WeakHashMap), so a
  wrapped platform answer could be mislabelled in the journal or re-screened as untrusted
  tool output. The marker is re-applied across the wrap.
- **`search_tools` results can't be wholesale withheld by one hostile description.** Deferred
  tool descriptions are screened once when the catalog is built; a screened catalog marks the
  search result as the platform's own answer instead of re-screening MCP-supplied text on
  every call.
- **The file-size ratchet actually gates again.** An empty `file-size-baseline.txt` made
  awk's `FNR==NR` match every scanned file, silently adopting all oversized files as
  "baseline"; the check now compares by filename and the baseline is reseeded.
- **`search_tools` activations now survive restarts and follow the session across replicas.**
  The carry-over of already-searched deferred tools was kept in per-replica memory, so a turn
  landing on another replica (or any restart) silently re-deferred every tool. Behind a new
  `ToolActivationStore` seam, `app.tools.search.activation-store=auto` (default) keeps them in
  Redis when a `StringRedisTemplate` bean exists and per-replica memory otherwise;
  `memory`/`redis` force one. New env: `TOOL_SEARCH_ACTIVATION_STORE`,
  `TOOL_SEARCH_ACTIVATION_TTL`.
- **Platform tool-dispatch diagnostics no longer trip the output guardrail.** A
  `call_tool` refusal (e.g. "the tool has not been loaded yet - call `search_tools`
  first") or a `search_tools` input error is the platform's own answer, not tool
  output; the content-safety screen used to read it as a prompt-injection attempt
  and withhold it, looping the model on instructions it could not see. Results a
  producer registers with `ToolResultMarker` now bypass the untrusted-content
  screen; real tool output is still screened exactly as before.
- **`search_tools` no-match answers now name what is searchable.** When keyword search over
  deferred tools finds nothing, the response lists every deferred tool name (up to 60; larger
  catalogs get tool-name groups plus a few generic tools such as web/fetch/file helpers) and the
  `select:name1,name2` escape hatch. A model that searched task words - which rarely overlap tool
  names - used to get only a source count and could loop without ever discovering the deferred
  catalog.
- **A turn parked on human approval now actually ends instead of looping.** After the first
  gated call parked, post-park tool calls were answered by the guard without consuming the step
  budget - a model that kept calling tools could burn the whole round allowance (41 rounds;
  more on raised ceilings) on "queued behind approval" answers, each resending the full
  conversation. Parked and queued results now carry an `approval_parked` marker the tool loop
  reads: the next round gets the budget-exhausted soft stop, and a model that still calls tools
  is ended with the hard stop. Parked results also survive tool-result clearing so the marker
  cannot be garbage-collected mid-turn.
- **Dispatch plumbing no longer files phantom approvals when queued behind a park.**
  `search_tools`, `call_tool` and `restore_tool_result` calls made after a turn parked used to
  each mint an `mcp_tool.*` approval request - but approval replay resolves tools by exact name
  without deferral, so an approved `call_tool` could only ever replay to a refusal. They are now
  refused without a record (`app.guardrails.approval-park.not-queued-message`).
- **The tool-loop round backstop now follows the turn's own ceiling.** It used to be the larger
  of the platform's derived cap and the turn's - so a SUPERVISED turn (ceiling 7) got the
  platform's 41-round allowance, and a personal install's raised ceiling gave 311. The cap is
  now derived from the ceiling this turn actually enforces plus its grace; an explicit
  `app.chat.tools.max-rounds-per-invocation` still wins.


- **`web_search` no longer vanishes silently when no provider is configured.** The tool was
  omitted from the payload unless a search provider was set up, so a misconfigured deployment
  made the model correctly conclude it had no web access and answer research requests from stale
  memory instead of failing fast. When nothing in `app.web-search.provider`/`fallback` is usable,
  `web_search` is now offered as a stub whose call returns an operator-facing explanation ("web
  search is not configured on this deployment..."), and a boot WARN names the gap
  (`WEB_SEARCH_PROVIDER`/`WEB_SEARCH_SEARXNG_BASE_URL` or a provider key). Per-agent deny lists
  still filter it; `deep_research`/`verify_citations` still require a configured provider.
- **A run's tool loop now has a cumulative prompt-token budget**
  (`app.chat.tools.max-prompt-tokens-per-turn`, tenant/agent override
  `chat.toolLoop.maxPromptTokensPerTurn`, `0` = uncapped). The per-turn call ceiling bounds rounds,
  not size: it missed the runaway shape where every loop round resends a near-full context window.
  Over the cap, calls are answered with the `tool_budget_exhausted` soft stop and the model writes
  its closing answer, so a runaway cannot resend an unbounded context.
- **Context compaction now reserves output headroom.** `app.context.compaction.reserve-output-tokens`
  (`APP_CONTEXT_COMPACTION_RESERVE_OUTPUT_TOKENS`, default 8,192) is subtracted from the model's
  context window before the clear/compact fractions apply, so a run at the compaction trigger still
  leaves room for the answer instead of riding at ~98% of the real window.

## [0.1.10] - 2026-10-24

### Added

- **Agents can author their own tools.** The new built-in `create_custom_tool` lets an
  agent write a small program when no existing tool fits - a Java `Tool.run(Map)`, a Python
  `run(input)`, or a JavaScript/TypeScript `run(input)` (new sandbox languages). The program is
  compiled and statically verified by the tool-sandbox service and stored as a DISABLED draft; a
  `custom_code_tool.enable` approval request (default-gated, so no approval-policy or autonomy
  tier can bypass it) is what enables it, for every agent in the workspace at once. Rejected
  programs stay on disk as drafts with the verifier's diagnostics. Personal and lite installs get
  tool-sandbox automatically with the `tools` profile (so `--no-tools` declines it); `--native-core`
  keeps it off since a host JVM cannot reach the container-only sandbox network.
- **Custom code tools now support JavaScript and TypeScript** in the console editor, the
  AI-generate flow and the sandbox. JS/TS tools run under Node 22 with `--experimental-permission`
  (fs, child_process, worker, addons and wasi calls throw) plus an identifier policy that rejects
  `import`/`require`, `process`, `globalThis`, the eval family, the network globals and
  `.constructor` access; vault-secret grants are currently Java/Python only.
- `continue_in_background` agent tool: when a task needs more tool calls than a
  single chat reply's budget, the agent hands it to a multi-turn background job
  instead of stopping with a partial answer. The job re-runs the same agent under
  the normal per-turn ceiling and approval gates; `AgentJobStartRequest.maxTurns`
  (capped by `APP_AGENT_JOBS_MAX_TURNS_CAP`, default 8) controls how many turns it
  may use. Opt-in per agent via the tool allow-list.


### Changed

- Fresh personal, lite and full installs now start the out-of-box tool surface by
  default: private web search (SearXNG), the MCP connector runner (built-in STDIO
  servers and tenant MCP connectors) and the Assistant's browser
  (`browser-runner`, giving agents `browser_navigate`, `browser_screenshot`,
  `browser_read_page_text` and friends plus the takeover view). Decline per
  install with `--no-search` / `--no-tools` / `--no-browser` or the matching
  `VEGADUTA_*=0` env var; `--minimal` still drops all of them. A remembered
  choice and installs that predate the defaults are left unchanged.


### Security

- **The console moves to Next.js 16.4.2.** Next.js 16.0.0 through 16.4.0 are affected by two critical advisories, unauthenticated remote code execution in the Image Optimization API when SVG files are used ([GHSA-2464-fhr4-w9xw](https://github.com/advisories/GHSA-2464-fhr4-w9xw)) and in `'use cache'` functions that return typed arrays ([GHSA-673q-mjm6-r5xr](https://github.com/advisories/GHSA-673q-mjm6-r5xr)), and by a high-severity denial of service in the App Router with Server Actions ([GHSA-p3m5-59mp-jv5r](https://github.com/advisories/GHSA-p3m5-59mp-jv5r), CVE-2026-94482); the first patched release is 16.4.1. The console, published up to 0.1.9, shipped 16.3.6. `web/package.json` now asks for `^16.4.2` and the lockfile resolves `next` and its `@next/*` packages to 16.4.2; no application code changed.
- **Tenant, project, workspace and git-ref ids no longer accept a trailing newline.** The allowlist checks in app-builder, code-intel and rl-trainer used `re.match` with a `$` anchor, which also matches before a final newline, so `abc\n` passed as a valid id; they now use `re.fullmatch`. The same fix covers the voice agent's LiveKit room-name patterns.
- **A monorepo `subdir` can no longer inject a second shell command.** app-builder's repo import interpolates `subdir` into `cd <subdir> && ...` (and into the generated GitHub Actions workflow), and its allowlist accepted a trailing newline (`a/b\n/` became `cd a/b\n && npm install`). It is now matched in full, refuses control characters and a leading `-`, and is shell-quoted where it is interpolated.
- **Two tenants with the same project id can no longer share an app-builder preview route.** With the legacy local preview on (`APP_BUILDER_LOCAL_PREVIEW_ENABLED`, off by default), the Caddy route was keyed by the project id alone, and project ids are unique only per tenant, so tenant B starting `shop` replaced tenant A's `shop` route. Routes, preview paths and preview hosts now carry a per-tenant prefix: the URL is `<base>/preview/<tenant key>/<project id>/` (or `https://<tenant key>-<project id>.<preview domain>`). A preview started before the upgrade keeps running and is removed by `preview/stop`.
- **app-builder no longer returns database error text to the caller.** `backend/provision`, `backend/schema` and `backend/tables` put the Postgres driver's message (role, schema and connection details) in the response `reason`; they now log it and return a generic reason with the SQLSTATE code, for example `schema apply failed (SQLSTATE 42601); see the app-builder service log`.
- **Caller-controlled values are logged without CR/LF** in app-builder's preview ingress and tool-sandbox's execute and error logs (`toolName` and the request path could forge a second log line).
- **The voice-agent image ships aiohttp 3.14.5 and the sidecar image pypdf 6.21.2.** aiohttp 3.14.3 had eight open advisories (three High; fixed in 3.14.4) and pypdf 6.19.0 five (fixed through 6.21.1). Both `requirements.lock` files were regenerated with `services/lock-requirements.sh`; only those two packages moved.
- **Core generates and reads DOCX/XLSX/PPTX with Apache POI 5.5.1 (was 5.3.0).** 5.4.0 fixed the advisories listed for `poi-ooxml` (CVE-2025-31672 among them). The version is one property, `poi.version` in `core/pom.xml`, for `core/documentgen` and `core/omnichannel`; the `commons-io` pin rose from 2.17.0 to 2.21.0 because POI calls into it. `PoiVersionAndRoundTripTest` guards the floor and writes then reads back an XLSX and a DOCX.
- **App Builder starters scaffold patched build tools.** `node-react` and `capacitor-app` use `vite` ^7.3.7 (was ^5.4.0) and `astro-site` uses `astro` ~7.3.8 (was ^4.16.0, one Critical advisory and seventeen more). Astro stays on 7.3 because 7.4.2 and later need Node 22.23.4, newer than the workspace runner's; each starter was built with `npm install && npm run build` on Node 22.23.3. The React 19, Capacitor 8 and TypeScript 7 majors in Dependabot pull request #43 are not part of this.
- **The console's lockfile takes the fixed vitest, vite, js-yaml, browserslist, brace-expansion, source-map-js and baseline-browser-mapping releases** (vitest 4.1.12, vite 7.3.7, js-yaml 4.3.2, browserslist 4.29.4, brace-expansion 1.1.21, source-map-js 1.2.2, baseline-browser-mapping 2.11.30). Everything except the last two is a dev dependency; `package.json` changes only the two vitest ranges, and `next` and `sharp` are left to the pull request that patches them.
- **Pathological text can no longer tie up a request thread in a regular expression** (CodeQL `java/redos` and `java/polynomial-redos`). The consent keyword reader (`ConsentText.normalize`, fed by inbound channel text) took about 8 s on 50,000 characters of punctuation; it now reads at most 512 characters and treats anything longer as "not a keyword" (never as consent). The pasted GitHub skill-folder link (`SkillRegistryClient`) was matched with a nested optional-slash group that backtracks exponentially (22 s at 50,000 characters); it is now a linear pattern and links over 400 characters are refused before matching. The email patterns in `PiiGuardrail`, `LlmGatewayPiiRedactor`, `SensitiveDataMasker`, `SkillDraftScrubber`, `HookRedactor` and `TurnRedaction` (13-19 s at 50,000 characters; `PolicyPackPatternMatcher`, which only detects, keeps a 64-character-bounded pattern that already matches any address) and the PEM private-key patterns in the step-event redactor, `PatternBasedGuardrailService`, `SensitiveDataMasker` and `SkillDraftScrubber` (quadratic on text full of `BEGIN` lines) are replaced by one linear scanner, `TextRedaction` in `core/security`: a regular expression bounded to 64 characters before the `@` finds the address and the match is then widened in code over the rest of the local part, so an address with a 65, 70 or 300-character local part is still redacted whole; a private-key block is found with `indexOf` and spans the first `BEGIN` to the first matching `END`, as before.
- **`WorkspaceFiles.saveUpload` checks the file name itself.** It relied on the controller to strip directories; it now refuses a name containing `/`, `\` or NUL, or equal to `.` or `..`, so no caller can write outside the chosen folder.
- **The browser-runner image clears the high-severity image scan.** It no longer carries the base image's npm, whose bundled `tar` 7.5.19, `undici` 6.27.0, `ip-address` 10.2.0 and `brace-expansion` 5.0.7 had high advisories with fixes (GHSA-r292-9mhp-454m, GHSA-rfgv-xxqx-mfg5, GHSA-mwp4-54f8-5fhr, GHSA-rgw5-rvv9-x895 and three more); the server starts with `node`, not `npx`. Playwright 1.49.0 (GHSA-7mvr-c777-76hp) is replaced by 1.63.0 (see Fixed), and the image upgrades the base image's openssl (CVE-2026-84782).
- **The web console image no longer ships known High vulnerabilities with fixes available.** `next` 16.3.6 -> 16.4.2 (GHSA-cjq9-62q9-8jv4, SSRF in the image optimiser, already limited to the hosts in `images.remotePatterns`; and, published 2026-10-22, the Critical GHSA-2464-fhr4-w9xw and GHSA-673q-mjm6-r5xr (unauthenticated remote code execution) and the High GHSA-p3m5-59mp-jv5r, all fixed in 16.4.1) and `sharp` 0.35.4 -> 0.35.5 (GHSA-wq5f-xc86-pv6w). The runtime stage also deletes the node base image's npm, corepack and yarn, which the console never runs and whose bundled brace-expansion, ip-address, picomatch, pacote and sigstore carried 10 more High advisories; grype `--only-fixed` now reports nothing for the image. `scripts/ci/web-image-deps.sh` keeps both fixes from regressing.
- **The core images ship the fixed releases of six Java dependencies.** The jar scan of the published 0.1.8/0.1.9 images listed High advisories with a fix available, which the critical-only release gate does not stop: Jackson 2.21.4 to 2.21.7 (jackson-core GHSA-p6pp-m3f8-5c89, GHSA-7hhh-6rmp-j9qf; jackson-databind GHSA-q4xh-88c3-wmh7, GHSA-cxp5-3px4-pw24, GHSA-wv8q-qhhj-9h54), httpcore5 and httpcore5-h2 5.3.6 to 5.4.3 (GHSA-hf6x-8p5f-cgmf, GHSA-v3jc-474w-2wm6), the PostgreSQL JDBC driver 42.7.11 to 42.7.12 (GHSA-j92g-9f8w-j867) and lz4-java 1.10.1 to 1.11.4 (GHSA-mcr4-qmvw-px4g, via kafka-clients). `core/pom.xml` overrides the Spring Boot BOM properties (or pins the artifact) and `DependencyAdvisoryFloorTest` fails the build if a later change brings one below its fixed release. The Java Agent SDK's build resolved Jackson 3.1.5 (`tools.jackson`, via spring-ai-client-chat) with the same advisories; `sdk/java/pom.xml` now imports the Jackson 3.1.7 BOM first and carries the same guard test. The SDK's published POMs are unchanged. One High stays in the core images by decision (2026-10-22): the MCP Java SDK (mcp-core 0.18.3, GHSA-8jxr-pr72-r468, a DNS-rebinding gap in MCP servers that do not validate the Origin header). Its only fixed release, 1.0.0, removes `SseMessageEndpointValidator` and `io.modelcontextprotocol.json.jackson`, which the remote-MCP origin pinning (SEC-026) and the stdio sidecar routing build on, and needs Spring AI 2.x with Spring Boot 4. Until that migration it is a dated, scoped exception in `.grype.yaml`; core's own `McpOriginValidationFilter` already answers 403 to a present, unlisted Origin on `/mcp` (enforce by default, `app.mcpserver.origin.mode`).
- **The Node runner images no longer ship High-severity npm advisories.** The npm CLI inside `node:22.23.3-trixie-slim` bundles brace-expansion 2.0.2 (five advisories), ip-address 10.1.0, picomatch 4.0.3, pacote 19.0.2/20.0.1 and sigstore 3.1.0, none fixed in any `node:22` tag yet. `workspace-runner`, `mcp-stdio-runner` (both variants) and `core-full` run npm/npx, so each now replaces it with the pinned npm 11.21.1 (`NPM_VERSION`, same value in all three Dockerfiles, checked by `services/mcp-stdio-runner/dockerfile-npm.test.js`). `mcp-stdio-runner-browser` also deletes its build-time npx cache, which nothing reads (every connection has its own cache) and which carried `@modelcontextprotocol/sdk` 1.0.1 (GHSA-w48q-cv73-mx4w) and basic-ftp 5.3.1 (GHSA-c475-qrg2-pj4r) from the deprecated `server-puppeteer`; `core-full` keeps that cache, because core runs `npx` against it, and pins both packages to fixed releases (1.32.1, 6.2.1) there. `workspace-runner`'s JDK base is now pinned by digest like every other base image (the same Temurin 21.0.12.1 as before).
- **A tenant's IMAP mailbox host is now checked by the outbound request policy.** Connecting an email mailbox (and every five-minute poll of it) dialled whatever host and port the tenant typed, so a tenant could probe loopback, internal services and the cloud metadata address, and the connection error text came back to the caller as a port oracle. The host now goes through the SSRF guard (host-name rules, every resolved address, the egress list and `app.security.outbound.allowed-private-hosts`), the client connects only to the addresses that passed, the port must be 1 to 65535, and a failed connection returns a fixed message with the detail kept in the server log. **A mailbox on a private address (a LAN mail server, a local Bridge) must now be listed in `app.security.outbound.allowed-private-hosts`, and a stored mailbox that is not listed stops being polled (a WARN names it).**
- **The Telegram webhook secret is stored as its SHA-256 digest.** The token Telegram echoes in `X-Telegram-Bot-Api-Secret-Token` is the only authentication on `/webhook/telegram/{botUserId}` and sat in the database in clear, so a database copy could forge inbound messages as an already-paired chat. Migration `V662` rewrites existing rows in place; Telegram keeps the raw token, so registered webhooks keep working. A core replica still on the previous version refuses Telegram calls (403, Telegram retries) until it is upgraded.
- **AG-UI run rejoin is scoped to the agent and the user that started the run.** The event buffer was keyed on the tenant and the client-chosen `runId` only, so anyone with RUNNER on any agent in the tenant who knew a `runId` could replay another agent's or another user's run, and two callers choosing the same `runId` wrote into one buffer. The buffer is now keyed by agent, caller and `runId`, and a `runId` must be 1 to 128 letters, digits, `-` or `_`; a rejoin by someone else gets the same empty stream as an unknown run.
- **A voice relay admission ticket can be used once.** `VoiceRelayTicketService` called its ticket "single-call" but only checked its five-minute expiry, so a ticket captured from a log or proxy could open further sockets. The relay now claims each ticket through the shared Redis claim (fail-open on a Redis outage, like the webhook dedup).
- **A Slack workspace belongs to one tenant.** Connecting a Slack app silently re-pointed the workspace's `team_id` link, so a second tenant connecting its own app in the same workspace took the first tenant's inbound events (which then failed the signature check and were dropped). The second connection is now refused with "already connected to another workspace"; the same tenant can still reconnect.
- **Remote MCP connections can no longer be redirected to an internal address by DNS.** The MCP SDK built its own `HttpClient` and resolved a registered server's host a second time at connect, so a name that was public when `McpRemoteUrlGuard` checked it could answer with a private or metadata address a moment later (and again on every SSE reconnect). Both transports now use the address-pinned client (`GuardedHttpClientBuilder`): each connect resolves through the SSRF guard and goes to the address it validated. A connection that is idle for 15 minutes (no event, no keep-alive) is now treated as dead.
- **Notifications can only be marked read by someone who can see them.** `POST /api/notifications/{id}/read` marked any notification in the tenant read; it now acts only on the caller's own notifications (or a broadcast, for a tenant admin) and is a quiet no-op for any other id.
- **Deciding an ambient-mode `agent.proposal` approval needs run access to the agent it proposes for.** Any member except the requester could approve or reject another team's agent's proposal. Holders of `PERM_MANAGE_APPROVALS` are unchanged. Channel-send approvals (`channel.*.send`) can still be decided by any other member and stay listed as a known gap.
- **`/actuator/prometheus` can be locked to a scrape token.** It stays open by default (the Full stack's Prometheus scrapes it without one) and is still hidden from anything that came through a proxy. A core published straight onto a network (Lite or Personal with `BIND_ADDR=0.0.0.0`) can now set `APP_ACTUATOR_SCRAPE_TOKEN` and `APP_ACTUATOR_SCRAPE_TOKEN_REQUIRED=true`; the scrape token is also hidden from the JWT decoder, so a scraper that sends it is no longer answered with 401.
- **`core/.env.example` no longer ships guessable secrets.** `DISCORD_GATEWAY_SHARED_SECRET` and `META_WEBHOOK_VERIFY_TOKEN` were `CHANGE_ME_...` values that core accepted as real; both are blank now, and core refuses the Discord callbacks and the Meta verification handshake while they are.

### Changed

- **Studio's cloud turns no longer look frozen while the model writes.** The cloud assist endpoints answer in one non-streamed reply, so the live-output box used to sit on "Waiting for the first words…" for the whole turn; it now phases an honest line on elapsed time ("Sending your request…", "the model is writing — cloud replies arrive all at once", "still writing — bigger changes take a couple of minutes"), and a 5-minute ceiling ends a stalled turn with a clear message instead of an endless wait. On-device engines still stream their tokens live.
- **The local-model surfaces open on any install that actually runs a local model.** /troubleshoot and /providers/local-model were personal-install only, so a lite or full install deployed with `--local-ai` offered no console path to diagnose or manage its Ollama pool - "No platform LLM is configured" pointed at a page that refused to help. Both pages now ask core whether this install manages a local model (a reachable model server, or a platform-pool row serving it) and open for it; a hosted tenant without one still sees the operator-managed notice. The provider catalogue's guided-setup link and first-run card follow the same check.
- **The App Builder's "change my app" turn explains itself while it runs.** The hosted generate turn streams its stages over Server-Sent Events (`POST /projects/{id}/generate/stream`), so /build shows live progress ("Reading your app…", "Writing your change…", "Checking it builds…", "Publishing the change…") instead of one opaque spinner, and the applied file set arrives as a `files` event the sandbox preview renders immediately, before the build/test check finishes. The model now also returns a plain-English `summary` of the change (core's generate-files contract, echoed back by the on-device path's `/generate/apply`), shown as the headline of each chat result alongside per-file +/− counts; on a failed turn the preview reverts to the pre-change files. The Studio mobile-app surface asks its model for the same one-line `NOTE:` and shows it instead of the bare "N changes applied". Retrieval for a change fetches the relevant files in parallel instead of one-by-one, shaving the context step off every turn.
- **Waiting tool calls can be decided together, and read in plain words.** When several calls wait in a chat, the approval panel offers "Approve all N" and "Reject all": the calls are decided one after another, oldest first, with progress shown, stopping at the first problem; a call whose arguments are too long to show is never approved that way. Approval cards and the learned-preference note name tools the way a person reads them ("Search the web", "create_issue (github)") with the exact name still under "Technical name", and a queued call no longer shows `[queued behind: ...]` in its name. Each reasoning-depth and autonomy option in an agent's settings now says what it costs in one sentence.
- **The Assistant shows its work while it works, and reads calmer.** A turn shows what it is doing from its first moment ("Searching the web for “x”…", "Writing the answer…") with each tool call as a chip that opens its arguments and result, and says when the model has been silent for 20 s (heartbeats count as life). After a tool approval, the "carrying on" line shows elapsed time, which stage the stored conversation proves (decision recorded, agent restarted, working on it), a still-working note after 30 s, and Retry if the continuation did not start, timed out or broke. Web answers list their sources as chips; Copy/Listen/Regenerate appear on hover or focus; transcript and composer share a centred 760px column. The welcome modal, tour and catch-up toast no longer open over the Assistant for someone who already has conversations, and the top bar's health and browser-model chips show as quiet icons there.
- **Platform trace filters are validated.** `GET /api/platform/traces` now answers 400 when `userId` or `name` contains a character outside letters, digits and `. _ : -` (plus `@ +` for `userId` and a space for `name`) or is longer than 128 characters; before, the value was URL-encoded and forwarded to Langfuse unchanged. Ordinary user ids, e-mail addresses and trace names such as `agent.turn` are unaffected.
- **A personal install no longer carries the operator and business features one person does not use.** core now runs as one edition, `app.edition.tier` = `personal` | `lite` | `full`, and the personal profile sets `personal` (`APP_EDITION_TIER`). Whole feature groups (Spring beans, controllers, schedulers and the tools built on them) are simply not registered on `personal`; with no tier set, or `lite`/`full`, every one stays on, so Lite, Full and the worker and jobs shapes behave as before. Tables, entities and migrations are unchanged. What a personal install leaves out (each can be switched back on, see the next entry):
  - **Billing and payments** (`billing-payments`): payment gateways (Razorpay, Stripe, PhonePe), checkout, invoices, subscriptions and plan upgrades, budget top-ups, the agent wallet, payment mandates and the commerce product feed (`/api/commerce/feed`). The agent tools `create_payment_link`, `request_upi_collect` and `check_payment_status` are no longer offered. The x402 seller endpoints answer "payments disabled" (404) instead of failing startup when no wallet ledger is registered. This is the existing `app.edition.billing-enabled` switch, now one of the features.
  - **Slack, Discord, Microsoft Teams and X** (`social-channels`): adapters, webhooks and credential endpoints; the Channels grid lists only the channels that are running. Telegram, Signal, WhatsApp, iMessage, Matrix, Twilio and email are unchanged.
  - **Agent-to-agent and the agent directory** (`a2a-directory`): the A2A JSON-RPC and REST endpoints, agent cards and the public agent directory. `app.a2a.enabled` is `false` on personal, and scheduled-task results can no longer be delivered to `slack` or `a2a`.
  - **The platform as an MCP server and the developer API** (`mcp-server-exposure`): `/api/dev/v1`, `/api/mcpserver/exposure`. The OpenAI-compatible API stays available when `app.openai-compat.enabled=true`, and the grants endpoint that mints its keys stays registered for that case.
  - **The platform operator console** (`operator-console`): the tenant, user, plan, spend, abuse, moderation, fleet, impersonation, audit, ops and reliability endpoints, and the demo, newsletter and landing surfaces. The platform LLM pool, tool credentials and platform settings stay, so a personal install can still add its model keys. SCIM (`/scim/v2`) is also no longer registered whenever `app.identity.provider=local`, because nothing mints a token for it there.
  - **Cloud speech** (`cloud-speech`): the Sarvam and ElevenLabs beans. Without them WhatsApp voice notes and call transcription have no speech backend and end as "not available", and music and voice cloning are unavailable.
  - **Realtime voice on public share links** (`voice-realtime-share`): anonymous realtime voice sessions, call recording and the LiveKit egress. The call-token endpoint stays.
  - **Video and demo media** (`video-demo-media`): Vertex Veo video generation, its job runner and the demo image and video controllers.
  - The hourly resolution sweep (`resolution.sweep.enabled`) is off on personal; the REST surface stays.
- **The console follows the edition.** `NEXT_PUBLIC_EDITION` (`personal` | `lite` | `full`) is read synchronously; without it the console derives the tier as before (local sign-in means personal). On a personal install one route table drives the rail, deep links and a real 404 from the proxy for pages the install does not have: SSO, shared-with-me, agent trust, API keys, team access and users, plans, ROI, payments, commerce, directory, studio, leads, branding, MCP-server exposure, view-as, sign-up, try-it, the template-submission page and the public agent card; `/robots.txt`, the sitemap and the A2A discovery files are inert. Cards and buttons that would call a feature core no longer has (budget top-up, teammate sharing, share-as-template, Sarvam and ElevenLabs, voice cloning, generated-audio listings) are hidden, and `/platform-admin` shows only LLM & Providers, Voice & Tool Credentials and Platform Settings. Team access hides `/admin/access`, `/admin/users` and the Users tab of `/admin/maintenance` (assumption: one person, no team). `/usage` stays, without the top-up card and the `/roi` link. The console warns, and shows the platform status banner, when core reports a different tier than the build was given.
- **The OpenAPI breaking-change gate catches what it let through.** `scripts/ci/openapi-breaking.mjs` now flags an operation that starts requiring credentials or a role/permission (`security-tightened`, `access-tightened`), a removed parameter or request property, a tightened input constraint (`minLength`/`maxLength`, `minimum`/`maximum`, `minItems`/`maxItems`, `pattern`, `format`), a narrowed `oneOf`/`anyOf`, and on responses a required property made optional, a loosened constraint or a widened `oneOf`/`anyOf`. `allOf` members are compared as one schema. A change that is deliberate goes in `scripts/ci/openapi-breaking-allowlist.txt` with a reason, as before. `docs/api/DEPRECATION.md` lists what counts as breaking, and says that a success status moving within 2xx (200 to 201) is not gated.
- **Code scanning reports on what ships.** CodeQL now reads `.github/codeql/codeql-config.yml`, which leaves test, tooling and sample code (`tests/`, `web/e2e/`, `**/src/test/`, `*.test.ts(x)`, `web/scripts/`, `examples/`, bundled doc assets) out of the alerts; the query suite is still `security-extended` and the check still runs on every pull request. Trivy reads `trivy.yaml`, which skips `deploy/helm/examples` and `tests/load` (64 example and load-test manifest findings); the Terraform reference modules stay in the scan. See `docs/security/scanning.md`.
- **Discord is described as send-only on this distribution.** Receiving Discord messages needs a Gateway bridge service that this repository does not contain (the README, `core/.env.example`, the console hint and `docs/channels/discord.md` said or implied it ships with the platform). `docs/channels/discord.md` now states what works with just a bot token, and the contract (`DISCORD_GATEWAY_SERVICE_URL`, `DISCORD_GATEWAY_SHARED_SECRET`, the `/bots/{id}/start` call and the `/webhook/discord` callback) for anyone who runs a bridge. `core/.env.example` no longer points `DISCORD_GATEWAY_SERVICE_URL` at a `localhost:4002` that nothing starts.
- **The email connect guide tells you what the poller does.** The console guide and `docs/channels/email.md` say to use a mailbox only the agent reads: it is opened read-write, every unread message is marked read after one attempt, a message that failed is not retried, and a mailbox on a private address needs an operator allow-list entry.
- **The security model states the limits it had left implicit.** `docs/security-model.md` now says that email contacts are keyed on the unauthenticated `From` header (no SPF, DKIM or DMARC check), documents what replay protection each inbound channel has (only Slack has a time window; the others rely on a 60-minute, fail-open provider-id de-duplication), and `docs/engineering/SECURITY-HARDENING.md` lists the OAuth browser-binding gap for Google, X, Atlassian and the two MCP flows as a known limitation.

- **Evals replay through the full production pipeline by default, in a sandbox; the tool-less replay is an explicit "quick" mode (package D2, owner decision 2026-10-23).** An eval used to withhold the agent's tools unless its dataset opted in, so a green eval did not measure what users get. A run now has a mode, `FULL` or `QUICK`, chosen in this order: the `mode` named when the run is triggered (`POST .../runs`, and the run options in the console), the mode its dataset pins, the workspace setting `quality.eval.defaultMode` (non-security; an admin can switch the default back to `quick`), the platform property `app.quality.eval.default-mode` (`full`, replacing `app.quality.eval.default-tool-execution`, which only ever existed in the unreleased 5d change). A `FULL` run is the same turn a person's message takes (reasoning depth, tool selection, guards, approval gate, step ceilings) and consumes its budget cap, with four additions that make it safe: (1) a tool runs for real only if its classification says it only reads (`EvalToolEffectPolicy`; `http_request` only for GET, HEAD and OPTIONS); a write, a send, shell, code, scheduling, a payment, a delegation to another agent, an unclassified tool and a tool whose classification cannot be read are answered with a simulated result and never reached (`toolresult.eval_sandbox.simulated`), recorded as `SIMULATED` in the tool ledger, the case's tool calls and the turn journal (`simulated`), and listed in the run detail (`simulatedTools`); `app.quality.eval.sandbox.read-only-tools` lets the operator vouch for an unclassified reader and can never override a declared class; (2) a call that needs a person is held as in production but records no approval request, so nobody is notified; (3) approvals auto-resolve only through the existing sandbox auto-approval (`autoApproveTools`, non-production tenants), are journaled as `decided:eval-approved`, and an approved write is still simulated; (4) a replay with tools refuses to start when the effect policy is unavailable. The run row records its mode (`eval_mode`, V670, backfilled from the dataset's old flag), which the run list, the run detail, the export bundle and the JSONL export show. A dataset's `tool_execution_enabled` is now a nullable pin (`mode`: `FULL`, `QUICK`, or `DEFAULT` to drop it): V670 turns every stored `FALSE` into "no pin", so existing datasets follow the new default; the dataset content hash is unchanged, so no run or gate binding goes stale. The golden-set seed and the App Builder envoy datasets no longer pin text-only. A rolling deploy needs the migration applied before old nodes read a dataset again. See "Evals, the Agent Lab, the quality judge and RL exploration on the real pipeline" in `docs/configuration.md`.
- **A supervised agent no longer asks before a read-only call in the `personal` profile, and still asks before every call elsewhere (package D1, owner decision 2026-10-23).** The `SUPERVISED` autonomy tier gated every tool call, a search or a lookup included, and every read-only prompt was the main reason a task felt stalled for one person who is also the approver. New setting `app.guardrails.supervised.gate-read-only` (`GuardrailTuningProperties.Supervised`, `APP_GUARDRAILS_SUPERVISED_GATE_READ_ONLY`): `true` in the base `application.yml` (unchanged: nothing weakened), `false` in `application-personal.yml`. With `false`, a call runs without approval only when its tool has an explicit classification row that says read-only (a stored row or a compiled-in declaration; never an unclassified tool and never an MCP server's own read-only annotation), the agent is positively judged not to hold the lethal trifecta, and nothing else asks for approval (taint, a procedure, a hook, the confirmation gate). Argument policies, hooks, budget and step limits and the circuit breaker still run on it. Each such call is audited (`tool.supervised.auto_allowed`, outcome `AUTO_APPROVED`), counted (`agent.tool.supervised.auto_allowed`) and journaled as `decision=auto-allowed reason=read-only-under-SUPERVISED`, which the turn inspector shows as information. It is a SECURITY key in the settings catalog (`guardrails.supervised.readOnlyCalls`, `auto-allow` or `gate`): a workspace administrator or an agent can only choose `gate`, and a stored or unknown value can never loosen a platform `true`. `remember_fact` is classified as a write, so it is still asked about. Code: new `ApprovalGateMode.GATE_UNLESS_READ_ONLY` and `ToolDispatchGate.Verdict#readOnly`; `TrifectaEnforcementService#definitelyNotLethal`. Tests: `SupervisedReadOnlyE2ETest` (real turn, real classification, real trifecta analysis, real settings catalog), `GuardedToolCallbackReadOnlyAutoAllowTest`, `GuardrailSettingsSupervisedTest`, `AgentToolGovernanceReadOnlyTest`, `JournalingToolCallbackTest`, `PersonalProfileConfigTest`. See [settings](docs/settings.md), [configuration](docs/configuration.md) and the defaults review (note 3).
- **Six agent-runtime defaults flipped on, from the defaults review (package D3, owner decision 2026-10-23).** In the base `application.yml` (inherited by lite, full and team deployments): `app.agent-hooks.enabled` `true`, `app.tool-arg-policies.enabled` `true` (both inert until a hook or policy is saved; a policy lookup failure follows `app.guardrails.fail-mode`), `app.agents.taint.mode` `observe` (was `off`: counts and audits what would need approval, changes nothing a user sees), `app.skills.supply-chain.enforce` `true` (a changed or unapproved high-risk custom skill is withheld until an admin re-approves it; built-in skills are never withheld), `app.context.compaction.enabled` `true` (a prompt inside the model's window is untouched) and `app.agent-jobs.durability.step-journal-enabled` `true` (job-turn journal, observability only).
