The `personal` profile keeps hooks, argument policies and supply-chain enforcement off, keeps taint on `enforce`, and has compaction and the job step journal on. Resume, rerun, job ownership, the turn journal (write cost unmeasured), moderation, pinning, trifecta, unclassified-tool action, escalation, the swarm verifier, reasoning depth and injection screening are unchanged. Each is still an environment override (`AGENT_HOOKS_ENABLED=false` and so on). `AgentRuntimeDefaultsConfigTest` loads the effective base, lite and personal configuration and the real components; `SkillSupplyChainEnforceDefaultTest` and `ContextCompactionDefaultOnHarnessTest` carry the conditions the last two flips were made on. See `docs/configuration.md` and `docs/engineering/agent-runtime-defaults-review.md`.

### Fixed

- **"Search the repository" reaches the code index, not the knowledge base.** `search_code` was missing from the always-relevant tool list while `search_knowledge_base` was always offered, and their descriptions overlapped enough that a "search the repository" request could land on the uploaded-documents index and report empty. Both descriptions now state their scope and point at each other (uploaded documents vs indexed source code vs workspace files), and `search_code` joins the always-relevant and tool-search always-on lists so lexical relevance can never trim it away when code-intel offers it.
- **Agent playground: the continuation reply after a tool approval is now clearly the final answer, and the status artifacts around it collapse instead of lingering.** A tool-call decision used to leave the receipt card and a "completed" status line sitting under the agent's real reply for several seconds, the transcript re-animated its scroll on every streamed chunk, and the reply was indistinguishable from intermediate output. The reply now carries an "after approval" badge, the continuation status line is dismissed the moment the reply lands, a fresh assistant turn closes leftover "Done" receipts at once, the stage label reads "Agent resumed", and scrolling follows instantly while a turn streams (smooth only for discrete new turns).
- **An approved action's raw tool output no longer appears as an assistant message.** After a person approved (or denied) a parked tool call, the outcome note (`[approved action 'web_search' completed: ...]`, with the tool's untrusted output) was stored as assistant text, so a reloaded Assistant thread showed it as a reply. It is now stored as a platform tool record on an assistant turn with no text: the model still reads it on every later turn (always, whatever the tool-record replay settings), while the transcript, the export and the resumed-reply reader leave it out, and the console never renders an empty assistant entry. New settings `app.approvals.continuation.max-note-arguments-chars` and `note-approval`.
- **Assistant threads are titled with what the person typed, not `<app_context>`.** The block the console puts ahead of each message (what is connected, the attached folder) became every thread's title. The server's thread list and the console's thread index now leave it out, and threads already stored with that title are re-titled when they are read.
- **Replies stream again on agents that offer a generation or MCP tool.** Such a turn was held whole until the model finished, so the person saw nothing and then the whole answer at once; an agent that always offers `generate_document` or any MCP tool (the time tools, for one) was held on every turn, whatever its provider. The reply now streams as it is written and only the part from the first link on is held, checked by the invented-link guard and then sent or replaced (`app.chat.parity.fast-buffering-mode`: `FROM_FIRST_LINK`, or `WHOLE_REPLY` for the old behaviour; `fast-hold-markers`). A reply held for output guardrails is still held whole.
- **A local model answers later turns from its prompt cache.** Ollama puts tool definitions ahead of the system prompt and keeps one prompt cache, and the per-message tool selection changed that prompt start on most turns, so a 9B model re-read 9,000 tokens (about 15 s on a 24 GB M5) before every reply. A model on the person's own machine (the install's Ollama pool row, or a bring-your-own `OLLAMA` / `LOCAL_OPENAI` provider) now gets the agent's tools unchanged and in a fixed order (`app.chat.local-model.stable-tools`, `app.chat.local-model.providers`); a cached turn starts in under 1.5 s, and a tool the message did not name, such as web search, is no longer dropped. Models in small-model mode keep their short six-tool list.
- **The small-model size limit is a setting.** `app.providers.small-model-max-billions` (default 8.0) replaces the constant; a row's own "Small-model mode" control still wins. [local-models.md](docs/local-models.md#response-speed) has a speed and hardware guide (GPU, CPU-only, low memory).

- **Public repository hygiene.** `scripts/dev/repo-identity.env` no longer carries a maintainer's personal mailbox as the Code of Conduct contact (it is the project address `CODE_OF_CONDUCT.md` already uses), which also lets `scripts/dev/set-repo-identity.sh` find that address again; when the security and conduct contacts are the same address, `--conduct-email` now rewrites only `CODE_OF_CONDUCT*.md` and `--security-email` every other file. The internal stage-test work list `docs/operations/stage-e2e-improvements.md` (local paths, private working-copy name, agent prompts) is removed, the desktop-agent `launchd` example no longer names a private checkout path, `AGENTS.md` no longer names the old repository, and the `[Unreleased]` and `[0.1.3]` link references at the bottom of this file point at tags and anchors that exist.
- **`scripts/vegaduta personal --minimal` (and `--no-valkey`) no longer exits in silence.** The Valkey step ended with a test that returns failure when Valkey is off, and under `set -e` that stopped the whole run before it printed its plan or started anything. `tests/install` now covers it.
- **SDK artifacts now ship the license they declare.** Every `io.github.cd-lc:*` jar carried the repository's BSL-1.1 `LICENSE`/`NOTICE` in `META-INF` while its POM declares Apache-2.0; the `sdk/` subtree now has its own Apache-2.0 `LICENSE` and `NOTICE` (also copied into the Python and TypeScript package directories), and the jars bundle those.
- **A first personal start no longer gives up while core is still booting.** On an empty database core runs about 640 migrations and creates the owner's schema before it reports ready; on a busy laptop that took 5 minutes, longer than core's health budget (120 s plus 10 checks 15 s apart), so Compose stopped with "dependency failed to start" and the console was never created. Core now has a 10-minute start period (failed checks do not count inside it), and the console starts as soon as core's container is up instead of after core is healthy; `up --wait` still waits for core. The personal stack's healthchecks (Postgres, core, console, MCP runner, search) also probe every 1-2 seconds while starting, so each service reports healthy within seconds of being ready instead of on the next 5-30 s tick.
- **Faster personal start.** The core JVM starts with a 25% initial heap instead of about 24 MB, which avoids dozens of stop-the-world grow-and-collect cycles during boot. The personal profile now moves best-effort work off the readiness path: the agent version-snapshot backfill runs after readiness (`APP_STARTUP_BACKGROUND_WARMUP_ENABLED`), a restart checks the personal schema with one query when its migration history is already complete (`TENANCY_MIGRATE_SKIP_UP_TO_DATE`), and the MCP registry is no longer fetched during boot (`MCP_REGISTRY_WARM_ON_STARTUP`). Each can be set back from the environment.
- **A restart no longer re-runs the local model's tool-call test.** `scripts/vegaduta` sent a real chat request to the model on every start (a cold model load, up to 240 s). It now measures once, records the result in `.env` as `LOCAL_MODEL_GRADE`, and reuses it until the model changes; `VEGADUTA_REPROBE=1` measures again.
- **`ApprovalNotificationBlockedException` is a `GovernanceException` again.** It extended `RuntimeException`, so `catch (GovernanceException)` - the documented one-stop handler - missed it.
- **Preparing an install no longer stops another install's containers.** When the launcher found the data of an earlier personal install in Docker, `scripts/vegaduta secrets`, a dry run and the install tests all ran `docker stop` on every container of the default `vegaduta-personal` project. The name of the new project is still chosen then, but containers are stopped only on a real start, and only one that publishes a host port the new install needs.
- **The personal install test no longer overwrites your real kept secrets.** `tests/install/vegaduta-personal.test.sh` did not set `VEGADUTA_SECRETS_KEEP`, so each run replaced `~/.config/vegaduta/secrets.env` with throwaway secrets. It now keeps its own copy, and a fake `docker` checks that file-only runs never stop or remove a container.
- **REST clients: streams, retries and errors are publish-grade.** The Python client's 60 s request timeout also applied to SSE reads, so a stream idle for a minute died; `stream()` now bounds connect+headers only (`http.client`, blocking reads). Both clients retry idempotent GET/HEAD on 429/5xx/network failures honouring `Retry-After`, re-resolve a callable token once on 401, wrap transport failures in a `NetworkError`, expose `code`, `headers` and `retry_after`/`retryAfterMs` on `ApiError`, and send an SDK `User-Agent`. The TypeScript client gains `timeoutMs` (client and per-call), `maxRetries` and `userAgent` options.
- **The core image is about 340 MB smaller.** The application jar was copied as root and then rewritten by a recursive `chown`, so it was stored in two layers. It is now copied with its owner set.
- **SDK metadata is publish-ready.** `vegaduta-agent` uses PEP 639 license metadata and carries `LICENSE`, coverage and mypy config and the missing `Security`/`OS Independent`/`Typed` classifiers; `vegaduta-sdk` (Python) gains full PyPI metadata; `vegaduta-sdk` (TypeScript) gains `exports`, `repository`/`bugs`/`keywords`, a `prepack` typecheck+build, a pinned `typescript` devDependency and a package `README.md`.
- **The installer test no longer depends on the machine it runs on.** `tests/install/vegaduta-install.test.sh` failed 4 checks on a Mac with Ollama running (the plan picked host mode instead of the container) and on any macOS (BSD `grep -r` does not follow the symlinked compose directory of its skeleton install), and 3 more under macOS's stock bash 3.2 (an empty `COMPOSE_ARGS` array under `set -u`). A curl shim now hides any Ollama on 127.0.0.1:11434 (with a case that provides one), the skeleton gets a real copy of the compose files, and the test runs clean under bash 3.2 and 5. No product change.
- **The governance and contributing pages no longer claim a branch ruleset that does not exist.** `GOVERNANCE.md`, `CONTRIBUTING.md` and `docs/maintainers/branch-protection.md` now say that no ruleset is configured on `main` yet (2026-10-22), what the repository owner must create (the `maintainers` team first, then the ruleset) and how to check it. `docs/maintainers/repo-setup.md` lists the GitHub settings that are still off (Dependabot security updates, secret-scanning push protection, Discussions) and the open code-scanning alerts. The maintainer placeholder comments in `MAINTAINERS.md`, `CODE_OF_CONDUCT.md` and `.github/CODEOWNERS` are removed, and `scripts/dev/repo-identity.env` no longer holds a personal address for the conduct contact. `docs-link-check.sh` now also fails on a leftover placeholder.
- **Handbook links to Markdown pages work on the published site.** Six links on two HTML handbook pages pointed at `.md` files, which are a 404 on the site; they now use the site URLs, and `docs-link-check.sh` fails on any `.md` href inside `docs/**/*.html`.
- **Service documentation matches the repository.** The service READMEs, Dockerfile and code comments, and the Python base-image notes name `deploy/compose/docker-compose.yml` and `deploy/compose/.env` (not `deploy/docker-compose.yml`), `rl-trainer` is started by the compose profile `rl` (there is no `RL_TRAINER_ENABLED`), `voice-agent` is described as wired into Compose and Helm, and `code-intel` and `app-builder` are started through their profiles instead of the superseded overlay files. `sidecar` and `workspace-runner` have READMEs.
- **The `mcp-stdio-runner` seccomp profile is documented as Compose-only.** The README, the chart README and the OWASP mapping now say the custom profile is applied by the Compose files, not by a plain `docker run` and not by the Helm chart (pods get `RuntimeDefault` plus the required VM-isolating RuntimeClass), and how to load it on Kubernetes nodes.
- **Setup on Windows downloads the model packs it reports.** It ran `vegaduta.ps1 models pull`, a command that script does not have (it prints its help and exits 0), so the model never arrived yet Setup said it was installed and remembered the pack. On Windows Setup now pulls through the install's Ollama (the container's `ollama pull` or the one on this computer), a pull counts only when Ollama says it finished, and on every OS a failed pull is reported on the done page and is not remembered as installed, so the next run offers it again.
- **Re-running Setup on Windows keeps the tools runner.** `vegaduta.ps1` records the `tools` profile in `profiles=` of its state file; Setup looked for `tools=1` (the bash launcher's key), saw it as off, and rewrote the state without it.
- **Setup no longer calls an existing install "latest".** A tree already in the install folder is reused as it is, so the plan (`--dry-run`, wizard) now shows the version that is installed, and says when a newer release exists or `--version` differs, with the exact `upgrade` command to run.
- **Setup finds the latest release without the GitHub API.** It reads the `releases/latest` redirect as `install.sh` does (the unauthenticated API allows 60 requests an hour per address, and every failure was reported as "no published release"), ignores releases that are not `vX.Y.Z` tags (the SDK release `sdk-v0.1.0` was once GitHub's Latest) and pre-releases, and names the real cause (rate limiting, nothing published, no network) in the error. `--version` accepts pre-releases such as `1.0.0-rc.1`, as `release.yml` and `install.sh` do, and is validated at start-up, in `--dry-run` and in the plan instead of at install time.
- **Setup honours `ALL_PROXY` and `NO_PROXY` and explains proxies.** Besides `HTTPS_PROXY`/`HTTP_PROXY` it reads `ALL_PROXY`; a download that cannot connect says to set them (it does not read the Windows/macOS system proxy settings).
- **The busy-port message names the ports you configured.** It said "3000 or 8080" even after `WEB_HOST_PORT`/`CORE_HOST_PORT` were moved, and recommended `net stop winnat` without saying it drops every Hyper-V and WSL connection. It now lists your ports and the busy ones, points to `netsh ... excludedportrange` and moving the port first, and warns about winnat. The preflight port check also reads those keys from the install's `.env`.
- **Setup no longer restarts Docker and WSL without asking (Windows).** Giving Docker more memory ran `wsl --shutdown`, closing every WSL window. It now needs the wizard's "Let Setup restart Docker" box (or `--allow-docker-restart`), and keeps your `.wslconfig` as `.wslconfig.vegaduta.bak` the first time it rewrites it.
- **The `.dmg`'s "READ ME FIRST" gives the steps macOS actually accepts.** It said right-click > Open, which macOS 15 removed; it now says System Settings > Privacy & Security > Open Anyway (or `xattr -dr com.apple.quarantine`). The app is still only ad-hoc signed and not notarized.
- **Windows: `scripts\vegaduta.ps1 upgrade -Version X` now brings the compose files, realm and scripts to release X.** It only re-pinned the image tags, so a Windows upgrade ran new images on old compose files. It now downloads `vegaduta-X.tar.gz` and `SHA256SUMS` (and checks the Sigstore bundle when `cosign` is installed), verifies the checksum, unpacks it over the install folder and finishes the upgrade with the new script, as the bash launcher does; any failed check stops before anything is changed. In a git checkout it warns and leaves the files to `git checkout vX.Y.Z`.
- **Windows: core sizes local models from the computer, not the WSL2 VM.** `scripts\vegaduta.ps1 personal` never wrote `HOST_RAM_GB` / `HOST_ACCELERATOR`, so the hardware panel and the memory budget saw Docker's VM (often 4-8 GB). It now writes both to `.env` like the bash launcher (RAM from the computer, `nvidia:NAME:GB` from `nvidia-smi`).
- **Windows: `scripts\vegaduta.ps1 models pull TAG` exists.** The Setup app runs it for a chosen model pack; the launcher printed its help and exited 0, so the model was never downloaded but Setup reported success. It pulls through the install's Ollama container or the Ollama on the computer and exits non-zero when the download fails. An unknown command now exits 2 instead of 0.
- **Windows PowerShell 5.1: a first install no longer stops on the first line Docker writes to stderr.** Under `$ErrorActionPreference = 'Stop'`, the 13 native calls that redirected stderr (`docker volume inspect` on a machine with no earlier install, `docker version` with the daemon down, ...) became terminating errors before their exit code was read, so the friendly "Docker daemon not running" never appeared. They go through one helper that reads the exit code instead.
- **Windows: preparing an install no longer stops another install's containers.** `scripts\vegaduta.ps1 secrets` (and the first `personal`) ran `docker stop` on every container of the default `vegaduta-personal` project when it found that project's data; as in the bash launcher it now only names the new project, and stops a container on a real start only when it holds a port the new install needs.
- **The Assistant's build container (`workspace-runner`) has a process limit.** It runs the commands the person approves (Maven, Gradle, npm) with network access, and unlike every other sandbox-style service it had a memory limit but no `pids` limit, so a runaway or malicious build could fork until the Docker VM ran out of process slots. Personal and Lite now cap it at 2048 processes and threads (`WORKSPACE_RUNNER_PIDS_LIMIT`; Maven, Gradle and Next.js builds peak at 60 to 190). Its JDK base image is also pinned by digest now (`eclipse-temurin:21.0.12.1_1-jdk-noble`, the one `tool-sandbox` already uses), so rebuilding from one commit gives the same image.
- **The Python Agent SDK release can pass its own checks.** `pypi-release.yml` ran `ruff format --check`, which failed on 13 of 34 files, so the first `pysdk-v*` run would have stopped at step one while `ci.yml` only ran `ruff check`. The files are formatted, `ruff` is pinned to 0.17.x in the `dev` extra, and CI runs the same two commands as the release.
- **The scanner CLI can be run.** `java -jar vegaduta-agent-scanner-0.1.0.jar` died with `NoClassDefFoundError` because the jar carries only its own classes. The build now attaches `vegaduta-agent-scanner-<version>-cli.jar` (a shaded jar with the SDK's API and core, SLF4J's API and RE2/J, plus their licence texts), `CliJarIT` runs it with `java -jar` on every `mvn verify`, `sdk-release.yml` attaches it to the release, and the docs show the working commands (0.1.0 itself shipped only the thin jar; its full class-path command is in the Java SDK README).
- **The npm package would have been published empty.** The `npm` job of `rest-sdk-release.yml` published a fresh checkout with `--ignore-scripts`, so `dist/` (gitignored, built by `prepack`) was missing while `main` and `types` point into it. It now publishes the tarball the build job packed, attested and attached to the GitHub Release, after checking that `dist/index.js` is in it.
- **The Python Agent SDK's "no HTTP client" test checked seven module names.** `import ftplib`, `websockets`, `grpc`, `ssl`, `importlib` and `asyncio.open_connection` all passed. It is now an allowlist (standard library, the package itself, the two optional extras) with the stdlib network modules, dynamic imports and asyncio's connection calls refused, and the rule has its own tests.
- **`CITATION.cff` declares the platform's licence (BUSL-1.1, not Apache-2.0) and a version and release date.** `sdk/test/citation.test.mjs` checks both against `LICENSE` and `CHANGELOG.md`; the release steps in `docs/maintainers/releasing.md` bump them.
- **SDK docs match the code.** The Java SDK is `0.1.0` (a GitHub Release, not yet on Maven Central) rather than `0.1.0-SNAPSHOT`, the Python Agent SDK's extras are installed from source until it is on PyPI, the REST client docs no longer cite `POST /api/agents` as a spec drift (the drift list is empty), and `sdk/test/docs-claims.test.mjs` guards these claims. `contracts/README.md` no longer points at a workflow and a Maven group that do not exist and lists what each contract is checked by; the Razorpay and Twilio fixtures are now validated against their schemas (`contracts/consumers/tools/test_inbound_fixtures.py`).
- **The schema-migration Job no longer exits 75 without migrating.** The migrate-only run (the Helm `migrate` Job, `app.tenancy.migrate-only=true`) still started every `@Scheduled` job, and the ones that tick at start-up took the per-JVM held-lock permits, so the migration's own advisory lock was skipped by the cap ("this JVM already holds the max concurrent locked jobs") and reported as "another migrator holds the lock" (exit 75, no migration; it passed on a retry). A migrate-only process now registers no `@Scheduled` method at all, and the migration lock is exempt from the cap. The Job's retry behaviour is unchanged (75 is ignored and retried; 1 fails the release).
- **The jobs and worker shapes start again.** `core-jobs` and `core-worker` (Helm `coreJobs`, which is on by default, and `coreWorker`; Compose `--profile jobs` / `--profile temporal`) failed to start in v0.1.9 with `Parameter 0 of constructor in ApiGuideToolSource required a bean of type 'ApiSpecProvider' that could not be found`: the API spec is served by a REST controller and these shapes scan none. The spec provider is now optional, so those shapes simply do not offer the `vegaduta_api_guide` tool. A new test (`ShapeInjectionPointsTest`) fails the build when a component of these shapes requires a bean only a controller provides, and `scripts/ci/boot-check.sh` now boots the real jobs and worker jars and a migrate-only run next to the api. A default Helm install (`coreJobs` enabled, API sweeps off) therefore ran no background jobs at all before this fix, because the jobs shape crashed at start; after upgrading, check that your scheduled sweeps (retention, outbox, usage rollups and the like) are running as you expect.
- **Dependabot pull requests can pass `CI OK` again.** The `DCO sign-off` job failed every Dependabot pull request, because the bot's commits carry no `Signed-off-by:` trailer, and `CI OK` requires it. `scripts/ci/dco-check.sh` (self-tested by `scripts/ci/test-dco-check.sh`) now skips a commit only when the pull request was opened by `dependabot[bot]` or `github-actions[bot]` and the commit is that bot's own; every human commit, including one pushed onto a Dependabot branch, still needs `git commit -s`. [Dependabot pull requests](docs/maintainers/dependabot-prs.md) documents the check and how a maintainer regenerates `requirements.lock` on a Dependabot branch, which Dependabot cannot do.
- **The browser add-on works again.** The `browser-runner` image started a Playwright 1.49.0 server (pinned in `package.json` and its lock) on a 1.63.0 base image, so core's 1.63.0 client was refused at connect (`428 Precondition Required, Playwright version mismatch`) while the container reported healthy: every `browser_*` call and the sign-in takeover failed. `package.json` and the lock now pin 1.63.0, the version of the base image and of the Java client, and a new static gate (`scripts/ci/check-browser-runner-version.sh`) fails when the three drift apart.
- **Plain-HTTP visitors behind Cloudflare are redirected to your site, not to `https://0.0.0.0:3000`.** The console's `CF-Visitor` http-to-https redirect built its target from the bind address of the standalone server. It now uses the request's `Host` header, and only when that host is one the deployment is configured for (`NEXT_PUBLIC_APP_URL`, `NEXT_PUBLIC_SITE_URL`, `PLATFORM_ADMIN_HOST`, or the hosted vegaduta apex/app hosts); any other `Host` is served as before, so the redirect cannot be turned into an open redirect. Set `NEXT_PUBLIC_APP_URL` (or `NEXT_PUBLIC_SITE_URL`) to your public origin for this to apply.
- **The apex-to-app host redirect works in the container image.** `APEX_TO_APP_HOST` (the hosted `vegaduta.ai`/`.xyz` canonicalisation) read `request.nextUrl.hostname`, which is `0.0.0.0` in the standalone server, so it never fired; it now reads the `Host` header and keeps the scheme the edge forwarded. Self-hosted installs are not in that map and are unaffected.
- **The Postgres image no longer crashes with `SIGILL` on a CPU other than the one that built it.** pgvector was compiled with its default `-march=native`, so the published `ghcr.io/cd-lc/postgres` image (both the Compose and the CloudNativePG flavour) carried instructions of the CI runner's CPU; on a host without them (seen on an arm64 Docker VM with 0.1.9) the backend died the first time a vector was cast or an HNSW index was created, so core could not finish its first migration. pgvector is now built with `OPTFLAGS=""` (portable code; it still dispatches its fast distance kernels at run time). `scripts/ci/postgres-portable-build.sh` keeps it that way.
- **The weekly OWASP ZAP scan and the nightly end-to-end run no longer fail before they start.** `docker compose up --wait` exits 1 when any container exits while it waits, and the one-shot `keycloak-reconcile` container exits 0 a few seconds after Keycloak is healthy, so a healthy stack was reported as a failed boot (the ZAP run of 21 October and the e2e runs of 21 and 22 October failed this way). `scripts/ci/compose-up-wait.sh` now waits on the long-running services and runs the one-shot to completion separately; a failing reconcile still fails the boot.
- **CI now runs tests it was skipping.** The `rl-trainer` suite, the `workspace-runner`, `whatsapp-qr` and `desktop-agent` tests, the second `mcp-stdio-runner` suite (`server.limits.test.js`) and eight `code-intel` modules (including the symlink-confinement and workspace-integrity security tests) passed locally but ran nowhere; `ci.yml` and `make test` pick them up (a new `code-intel` test module is picked up by name).
- **`make lint` is green again.** It reported 30 `ruff` errors in `services/sidecar` and `services/code-intel`; they are fixed and the whole-tree `ruff` step for those two services is blocking in CI.
- **`make gates` is green on a clean checkout, and CI runs the same checks.** `docs/api/asyncapi.json` was missing its trailing newline, so `asyncapi.mjs --check` failed locally while CI never ran it; that check and the platform-guide docs bundle check (its copy of `docs/local-models.md` was stale) are now steps of `static-gates.yml`, and `scripts/ci/test-gates-parity.sh` fails when a `make gates` command has no step there. A pull request that changes only `contracts/outbound/` or the SDK enums that `AuditOutcomeAlignmentTest` and `CoreConsumerContractTest` read now triggers the `core` and `sdk/java` jobs.
- **Dependabot covers the services it missed.** `browser-runner` and `whatsapp-qr` (npm) and the Dockerfiles of `workspace-runner`, `whatsapp-qr`, `browser-runner` and `deploy/omnibus` get update PRs; `scripts/ci/dependabot-coverage.sh` fails when a new `package.json`, `go.mod` or `Dockerfile` directory is left out.
- **Two documentation deep links work on the published site.** `extending.md` linked to the configuration "Authentication" section and the cloud-portability "Redis" section with the GitHub slug (double dash), which mkdocs slugs with one dash, so both landed at the top of the page. The two headings are now "Authentication (OIDC and Keycloak)" and "Redis Cluster and Sentinel readiness", whose slug is the same on GitHub and on the site, and `mkdocs build --strict` fails on a broken anchor.
- **The OpenAPI breaking-change gate no longer carries 12 stale allowlist entries** (the response-body corrections were merged long ago); a future removal of those bodies would otherwise have been allowed silently.
- **A full install now reports itself as the full tier, so `/add-ons` shows the "all add-ons included" notice.** `docker-compose.yml` and the Helm web chart left `NEXT_PUBLIC_DEPLOY_ENV` empty, so the documented full-install behaviour (Add-ons out of the rail, an "included" notice instead of switches) never happened on any shipped full deploy. Compose now defaults `WEB_DEPLOY_ENV` to `full` and the chart sets `NEXT_PUBLIC_DEPLOY_ENV: "full"`; `compose-validate.sh` and the Helm guard tests assert it. A hosted deploy that sets `production` or `staging` for the top-bar pill is unchanged.
- **Lite and full installs no longer show an amber environment pill in the top bar.** The pill printed any `NEXT_PUBLIC_DEPLOY_ENV` value except `personal`, so a lite install showed a warning-coloured LITE badge that read like staging. The tier values `personal`, `lite` and `full` select install behaviour and show no pill; `production` and `staging` still do. `RUNTIME-CONFIG.md` and the environment-variable reference now say so.
- **The Assistant task card's "Add phone number" link no longer lands on a 404.** It pointed at `/account`, which does not exist. The card now asks for the number inline and saves it through `PUT /api/account/phone`, unlocking the WhatsApp, SMS and call channels without leaving the chat.
- **`/learn/tutor` with the tutor switched off no longer offers a dead "Back to Learn" button.** `/learn` was removed; the button is now "Back to Help" and goes to `/help`.
- **The console no longer files a false CSP violation on every page view.** zod 4 probes `new Function("")` when it builds a schema, which the enforced CSP (no `'unsafe-eval'`) blocks and reports to `/api/csp-report` even though zod catches the error. zod now runs in jitless mode, so the report log only holds real violations.
- **`next build` no longer dirties the tree.** `web/next-env.d.ts` is generated by `next dev` and `next build` (with different contents) and is already gitignored, but a copy was tracked. It is untracked now; nothing else changes.
- **Keyboard and screen-reader fixes on the landing page and the Build Center.** The scrollable install and SDK code blocks on the open-source landing page are focusable, named regions, and the envoy readiness summary has a `group` role so its label is announced (axe `scrollable-region-focusable`, `aria-prohibited-attr`). Colour contrast of the orange brand accent on light backgrounds is not changed.
- **The public-surface Playwright specs match the shipped landing and signup.** `tests/e2e` asserted a landing page, a "Register now" button and a wizard that no longer exist, so the nightly's default specs could not pass. `landing.spec.ts` now covers the open-source landing (default) and the classic one (`E2E_SITE_MODE=classic`), `signup.spec.ts` follows the current wizard, and `accessibility-smoke.spec.ts` scans a DOM snapshot instead of indexing a live locator count. The nightly's stack-boot failure is separate and not part of this change.
- **The i18n parity baseline is current again.** `npm run i18n:parity:check` failed on every locale because `en.json` had grown since the baseline was written; it was re-baselined deliberately with `--update --force` (no locale text changed).
- **A workspace that deletes itself is now erased as completely as one a platform admin deletes.** `POST /api/tenant/data-deletion` and the sole-member `DELETE /api/account` left the tenant's Valkey state behind (free-tier usage counters, LLM rate buckets, per-user budget counters, workflow-trigger and inbound-dedup markers, MCP-grant buckets), and the first also wrote no platform deletion-audit row, so the platform admin could not see the workspace had erased itself. All three paths now run one sequence: audit row, Valkey purge, erasure, audit completion.
- **`--profile jobs` on the full Compose stack can now hand the background work to `core-jobs`.** The API kept running every sweep and poller next to `core-jobs` because nothing gave it the `shape-api` profile. `deploy/compose/docker-compose.yml` now appends `CORE_SHAPE_PROFILE` (empty by default) to the API's Spring profiles; set it to `,shape-api` with the `jobs` profile.
- **A refused local-model repair no longer switches the platform LLM pool back on.** When an admin had switched the pool off, any chat re-enabled it before checking that the local model row could be repaired; a host the SSRF guard refuses or a blank bootstrap key left the pool on and the chat still failing. The switch now flips only after the row is usable or repaired.
- **The platform-model warm-up no longer writes into a member's LLM call log.** `POST /api/platform-llm/warm` runs its one-token probe on the signing-in member's tenant, so a `PLATFORM_HOSTED` row (and spend) appeared in that tenant's call log every few minutes; the probe is now excluded from the call log, spend counters and metrics.
- **Slack's Events API Request URL can be saved.** `/webhook/slack` dropped Slack's `url_verification` handshake (`{token, challenge, type}`, no `team_id`) before answering it, so Slack refused the URL and Slack inbound could not be set up. The handshake is now answered first, with the challenge echoed only when it is a short token (`[A-Za-z0-9._-]`, at most 256 characters); every event still resolves its tenant and verifies the signature.
- **Concurrent first sign-ins no longer fail on `uq_user_permission_grant`.** A user's first requests (a page load fires several at once) each tried to seed the same permission rows; the losers hit the unique constraint at commit, which the code's own catch could not see, so the permission lookup logged an ERROR and the request ran with roles only (403 on `PERM_*` surfaces). The seed insert is now `ON CONFLICT DO NOTHING` and every request returns what is stored.
- **The async-hop guard now flags Reactor `.timeout()`.** A timeout that fires continues on Reactor's timer thread with no tenant context; `AsyncHopTenantScopeRulesTest` did not look for it. The three classes that use it (the FAST turn stream and its recovery branches, the demo studio mission) are allow-listed with the reason their handlers are already scoped.
- **`app.a2a.enabled` has an explicit default.** The A2A surface (agent cards, `/api/a2a`, REST) has always been on unless `APP_A2A_ENABLED=false`, but the key was in neither `application.yml` nor the configuration reference; both now say `true`.

- **A reset of a high-risk or security setting needs a reason, the change history loads in one request and shows agents too, and two writers of one key get a 409 (package G4).** (1) `DELETE /api/settings/tenant/{key}` and `DELETE /api/agents/{agentId}/settings/{key}` answer 400 `SETTING_INVALID` for a `high` or `security` key without `?reason=`, like the PUT; the reason is kept in the history and the `SETTING_RESET` audit entry, and the console asks for it in the same confirmation dialog (one prompt for a whole group reset; cancelling it leaves those settings alone). (2) New `GET /api/settings/history?scope=all|tenant|agent&agentId=&since=&limit=&offset=` (tenant admin; one agent with EDITOR on it) and `GET /api/agents/{agentId}/settings/history` return the history of every key with a `scope`/`agentId` per row, `total` and `hasMore`; the Change history tab makes one request per page instead of one per setting, merges workspace and agent changes with a Level column and filters by level. New `app.settings.bulk-history-default-limit` (100) and `bulk-history-max-limit` (500). (3) The console shows which direction is stricter for a security key (`strictness` from the catalog) in the field and in the confirmation, and an agent editor who is not a workspace admin sees security keys read-only with the reason instead of a 403 after saving. (4) Two writers changing the same key at the same moment: the second gets `409 SETTING_CONFLICT` (nothing stored) instead of a 500; both 409 codes are in `docs/api/openapi.json`. (5) Migration V669 removes an agent's overrides when the agent is deleted and records a `RESET` row (reason `agent deleted`) in the history. Defaults are unchanged; nothing new is required of an existing client except the reason on a reset of a high or security key.
- **Five settings the console accepted but nothing read are now read, and the fail mode now applies to chat turns (package G3).** `delegation.maxBranches` and `delegation.branchTimeoutSeconds` bound `delegate_parallel`, `run_subtasks` and the swarm verifier's repair round for the calling agent (agent, then workspace, then `app.agents.collaboration.*`); `quality.judge.model`, `quality.judge.callTimeoutSeconds` and `quality.eval.defaultRepeats` are read per workspace by the judge client factory and the eval trigger; `guardrails.failMode` decides, per workspace and agent and never looser than the platform, whether a failing inbound moderation check, inbound content-guardrail check or outbound content-guardrail check refuses the turn (the chat gate still read the static platform mode). Defaults are unchanged. `SettingsWiringGuardTest` fails the build when a catalog key has no call site in main code. `CollaboratorToolFactory#delegateToTeam` takes the calling agent's id.
- **Gemini keeps its tools, fail-open gaps are closed, the turn journal is purged, the edge bundle checks access and the verifier sees the conversation (package C6).** (1) Gemini signatures: the reply keeps only the signatures of the function-call parts, in order, so a signed lead-in text part no longer gives the call somebody else's signature (the cause of "Function call is missing a thought_signature"); `GeminiThoughtSignatureReplayTest` drives Spring AI's real request conversion and reads the second request. The no-tools fallback `app.chat.resilience.thought-signature-degrade` is now OFF by default (**behaviour change**: the provider's error is reported instead of a silent tool-free answer); when an operator turns it on, the reply opens with a notice and the model is shown what the tools returned. (2) **Behaviour change:** a per-agent tool-argument policy lookup that fails now follows `app.guardrails.fail-mode` (refused when `closed`, the default; it used to let the call through in every mode). (3) `app.turn-journal.retention-days` (default 90, `0` keeps for ever), `purge-interval` (6h), `purge-batch-turns` and `purge-max-batches`: a sweep deletes whole old turns in every tenant schema; `app.turn-journal.enabled` is explicit in `application.yml` (default `false`, unchanged). (4) `GET /api/edge/prompts?agentId=` returns an agent's own `edge.*` text only to a caller with VIEWER access to that agent (a tenant member without access gets the tenant's text, as for an unknown id). (5) `app.guardrails.tool-verifier.context-messages` (default 6, `0` = the old behaviour): the RIGOROUS tool-call verifier is shown the last messages of the conversation, so "yes, send it" is judged against what it answers; the framing line is the prompt key `judge.tool_verifier.context`.
- **The last model-facing texts are in the prompt registry, with agent and tenant overrides (package C2).** The framing line of a workspace-authored skill (`toolresult.skill.workspace_framing`), the circuit-breaker result (`toolresult.tool_breaker.*`), the "NOT executed" feedback for arguments that do not match a tool's schema (`toolresult.invalid_arguments.*`), the text `generate_image` returns (`toolresult.image_generation.*`; the chat module's answer to a never-offered `generate_image` is `toolresult.unavailable.generate_image`), the four notices a person reads when a reply is cut off, stopped by the provider's safety system, has an unusable tool call or is empty (`message.finish.*`), the AI workflow generator's meta-prompts (`workflow.generator.*`), the untrusted-text notice and closing instructions of a research goal (`research.goal.*`), the "Ask AI" form-field writer (`edge.ask_ai.system`, one key for `AssistService` and the console's on-device draft), the failure-triage and technical-explanation prompts (`diagnostics.*`) and the default persona of a studio app's AI (`studioapp.default_system`) were Java or TypeScript literals. Each shipped default is byte-identical to the old text (golden tests), except one documented change: the console's on-device Ask AI draft now sends the server's line-wrapped wording instead of its single-line copy (same words). The JSON fallbacks of the tool-budget results carry the registry's instruction instead of a literal. `app.chat.finish.truncated-notice`, `refusal-notice`, `malformed-tool-call-notice` and `empty-reply-message` are deprecated aliases of their keys' platform default and are no longer set in `application.yml`. See `docs/prompts.md`.
- **The nudge texts have one source of truth: the prompt registry (package C3).** The text sent to the model after an announced-but-untaken step, a reply cut off at the output limit and an unusable tool call existed twice, as the registry keys `nudge.continue_unfinished` / `nudge.reply_cut_off` / `nudge.retry_tool_call` and as the properties `app.chat.continuation.nudge.<lang>`, `app.chat.finish.continue-nudge` and `app.chat.finish.retry-tool-call-nudge` (with a precedence rule between them, and the property text copied into `application.yml` and `*.properties`). Now only the registry holds the wording and every call site reads only the resolver. The three properties (and `app.chat.escalation.low-confidence-markers`, the same duplicate for `detector.escalation.low_confidence_markers`) are deprecated aliases: a value that differs from the shipped text is applied as the platform-scope default of its key, logged once at startup with the key to edit instead, and any stored agent, workspace or platform override beats it; the shipped texts are byte-identical and the properties are no longer set in `application.yml`. One behaviour change: a stored workspace or agent override of the low-confidence marker list now beats the property (before, the property beat everything). See `docs/prompts.md` and `docs/configuration.md`.
- **Evals and the Agent Lab can test the real pipeline (package 5d).** An eval replay reached the chat service through a reflective bridge and ran text-only unless a dataset opted in, so a green eval said little about the agent with its tools. The bridge is now typed (`ReplayTurnRunner` in `agents-turn`, `AgentChatServiceTurnReplayer` in `app`; the reflective class and its stand-in are gone) and a dataset picks its replay mode with two settings: text only (the default; new datasets take `app.quality.eval.default-tool-execution`, `false` as shipped, existing datasets are untouched), the real tool pipeline where a gated call parks as in production, or the real pipeline under a sandbox auto-approval (`autoApproveTools`). The auto-approval skips only the "needs a person" park, for tools outside `app.quality.eval.auto-approve.excluded-tools`, only for tenants the operator lists in `app.quality.eval.auto-approve.tenant-ids` (empty = none; checked when a dataset sets it, when a run is triggered and at every replay), under a request id the server makes so a chat request cannot ask for it; the run row records `auto_approved` (V667) and it is part of the dataset's content hash. Each replayed tool call now carries the status the turn recorded for it (ran, failed, not run, held for approval, replayed), stored with the case and used by trajectory evals instead of reading the wording of the output; a trajectory spec on a text-only dataset is reported `UNAVAILABLE` instead of being ignored silently (`app.quality.trajectory-eval.enabled` stays opt-in). The Agent Lab can run a cell with the agent's real tools (`withTools` on the run, `app.agent-lab.with-tools.enabled`, off as shipped; every other lab caller stays tool-less). The live quality judge's sample rate is settable at run time (platform setting `platform.quality.judge.sample-rate`, env `APP_LLM_JUDGE_SAMPLE_RATE`; 5% unchanged), and `rl.selection.server-turn-ref` (off) lets the console chat endpoint give untagged turns an RL turn reference so the bandits reach real conversations instead of only the Playground. See "Evals, the Agent Lab, the quality judge and RL exploration on the real pipeline" in `docs/configuration.md`.
- **The turn journal records every depth and the turns agents run for each other, says what a tool call really did, and can be found (package 5c).** Off by default behind `app.turn-journal.enabled`, unchanged. It used to journal only the streamed depth: CAREFUL, RIGOROUS and PLANNED turns and the turns a delegated agent or a specialist runs left no record, and a tool call the guard refused or parked for a human was written as `status=ok` because only an exception was an error. A call is now `ok`, `error`, `blocked` or `parked`; a parked call also writes an `APPROVAL` event with the approval request id, and the turn that continues the task after the decision writes the matching `decided:...` event. The turn's id is made with its `TurnContext`, returned as `X-Journal-Turn-Id` while the journal is on, and a session's turns are listed with `GET /api/agents/{agentId}/turn-journal?sessionId=` (audit-log permission, authorization snapshot updated; tenant migration V666 adds a session digest to the journal rows, `app.turn-journal.max-list` caps the listing). "Did the tool run" is no longer read from the first words of the result: the layer that answers instead of a tool (tool guard, dispatch gate, argument policy, hooks, circuit breaker, MCP argument check) registers the exact text with a typed kind (`ToolResultMarker`), so a configured approval-park wording, or the JSON-wrapped MCP refusal, is no longer recorded in the effect journal as an executed effect; the old prefixes are still accepted. Delegated calls (`ask_agent_*`) and specialist handoffs now run the stages a buffered turn has: the question is screened by inbound moderation and the delegate's own content guardrails, model-level hooks apply, the answer goes through the delegate's output guardrails, finish-reason handling and the continuation policy apply, and the sub-turn has its own journal record that names its parent turn. A readiness check (`app.wiring.strict-gateways`, default off) logs an ERROR, or fails the start, when a feature whose flag is on never installed its static gateway (turn journal, the four learning loops), and `AgentChatService`'s optional collaborators now have a Spring-context wiring test. See ADR-0020 and `docs/configuration.md`.
- **Prompts have a console, the request inspector works across replicas, and edge turns read the effective wording (package 4f).** `/admin/instructions` gains **All prompts** (every registry key with the wording in force, the scope it comes from and its version; search, risk-class filter; an editor at workspace, agent or platform scope that shows placeholder and length problems under the text as you type; version history with a line diff and one-click restore or revert; on/off for prompts that may be switched off) and **Preview final prompt** (an agent's real system text, each block tagged with key, scope, version and token estimate). The Request X-ray lost its turn on any replica but the one that ran it, and everything on a restart: copies are now also written to Redis with the same 6 h life and per-session depth (`app.llm.request-snapshot-store.*`, default `redis`; `memory` is the old behaviour), off the model-call path, and each system message lists the registry blocks it is made of. The in-browser (edge) runtime no longer has its own copies of the reasoning nudge, the "helpful assistant" fallback, the on-device refusals and the codebase Q&A instruction: they are `edge.*` keys served by `GET /api/edge/prompts` (signed-in users), with the shipped text bundled as the offline default (a test keeps the two equal). Default wording is unchanged.
- **Tool descriptions and their parameter descriptions are in the prompt registry (first half of the built-in tools).** The description of each of the browser, web, delegation, scheduling, coding, document, messaging, device, payment, shell, desktop, table and search tools, and of every described parameter in its input schema, is a key (`tool.description.<tool>`, `tool.param.<tool>.<param>`; `tool.template.<tool>` for the three delegation tools that fill a roster or a name in themselves) with a shipped default byte-identical to the old string (a golden test per module builds each tool and compares). One decorator, `ToolDescriptionResolver`, applies an agent, tenant or platform override while the tool list of a turn is built, so every path that resolves tools (streamed, buffered, delegation, specialist, jobs, post-approval, prompt preview) sees it; a tool with no override is returned unchanged, and an override can only change description texts, never a tool's name or schema shape. Tools that act outside the platform are security critical. See `docs/prompts.md`.
- **Tool descriptions and their parameter descriptions are in the prompt registry (second half: memory, skills, workspace files, code intelligence, Google, media, knowledge, payments, Sarvam, workflow).** The same keys and the same decorator as the first half (`tool.description.<tool>`, `tool.param.<tool>.<param>`), with a shipped default byte-identical to the old string (golden tests in `core/agents-tools` and `core/app` build every tool and compare). The two mandate payment tools share their names with the commerce tools and are the `.mandate` variants; the workspace file tools are templates with `{root}` (the projects folder). Tools that send, write, move money or run code are security critical. The tools the platform's own MCP server offers to external clients (no agent or tenant to resolve for) and the two shim pseudo tools stay in code. See `docs/prompts.md`.
- **The text the turn pipeline writes besides the standing directives is in the prompt registry.** The plan block, the lines that frame recalled memory and the user's notes, the rolling-summary preamble, the skills header, the delegation context header, the refusals and fallbacks a person reads (moderation, guardrails, a failed reply, an invented file link, the degraded-turn notice), the words of workspace hooks, the compaction summary and cleared-result note, the untrusted-content banner, the three nudges and the data of the invented-link detector were Java literals. Each is now a key with a shipped default byte-identical to the old text (`TurnTextsGoldenTest`) and an agent, tenant or platform override that reaches the streamed, buffered, delegated and post-approval paths. The nudges overlay their existing `app.chat.*` settings instead of replacing them. See `docs/prompts.md`.
- **The text the guardrails, the tool loop and the swarm write for the model is in the prompt registry, and the English-only detectors are resource files.** The tool results returned when a call is refused by the tool policy, the verifier, a procedure or an argument policy, when an output is withheld, when a call is held for approval (the `app.guardrails.approval-park.*` texts) and when the tool-call or tool-round budget is used up, the answer to a tool that is not switched on or was invented, the instruction of the injection judge, the tool-call verifier, the moderation classifier and the toxicity judge, the "Operating procedures" header and its markers, the refusals of the jailbreak, policy and toxicity guardrails, the two tool-routing shims and the structured-output shim for local models, and the `run_subtasks` worker directive were Java literals that no tenant or agent could change. Each is now a key (`toolresult.guard.*`, `toolresult.park.*`, `toolresult.tool_budget.*`, `toolresult.unavailable.*`, `judge.*`, `procedures.*`, `refusal.guardrail.*`, `shim.*`, `delegation.swarm_worker*`) with a shipped default byte-identical to the old text (a golden test per module) and an agent, tenant or platform override that reaches the guarded call, the judges (through `GuardrailPromptScope`) and the tool loop of the streamed, buffered, delegated, job and post-approval paths (the turn's transcript carries its agent and tenant); the content engine and the moderation layer open the same scope, so an agent override of a guardrail refusal applies on the inbound and outbound content gate too. The properties that already held some of this text (`app.guardrails.approval-park.*-message`, `app.guardrails.step-limit.exhausted-message`, `app.chat.tools.budget-exhausted-message`, `app.chat.tools.hard-stop-reply`) keep working as aliases: a registry override wins, then a property an operator changed, then the shipped text. The jailbreak, policy-violation and injection-marker regexes and the secret formats redacted from tool output moved to `guardrails/patterns/<locale>/<set>.txt` and `guardrails/patterns/redaction.txt`: the English files are exactly the old patterns, and shipping a file for another locale adds patterns for that language (every locale is checked together). See `docs/prompts.md`.
- **The prompts of the secondary model calls and the replay word lists are in the prompt registry.** The instruction of the conversation-compaction and rolling-session summarizers, the Reflexion and consistency passes, the task planner and re-planner (and its tool hint), the sampled quality judge, the cost-routing and agent-selection questions, the text of a distilled skill, the swarm verifier, the graph-memory triple extraction and write arbitration, the lesson, skill-draft and skill-self-check learners, the deep-research seed prompt, the retrieval query rewriters, the six LLM judges of the quality evaluators, the golden-case grader, the Agent Lab graders and the workflow Question Classifier, Parameter Extractor, Supervisor, limits-inference and credential-redaction texts were Java literals that no tenant or agent could change. Each is now a key with a shipped default byte-identical to the old text (a golden test per module) and an agent, tenant or platform override that reaches the call wherever the call knows its agent (the swarm verifier, the query rewriters and the classifier and extractor nodes read the tenant's). The tool-name lists that decide whether a turn may be replayed (read-only prefixes, mutating words, never-replay tools) and the low-confidence reply markers are now keys too (`detector.*`), one entry per line: the replay lists are security critical, and an override can add to the mutating words and never-replay tools but never remove a shipped entry. Default behavior is unchanged. See `docs/prompts.md`.
- **An invalid enum value in an agent file or template is rejected, not silently defaulted.** `reasoningDepth: "BALANCED"` is not a depth (FAST, CAREFUL, RIGOROUS, PLANNED) and was quietly turned into FAST on import, so three of the four shipped example agents, their console copies (`web/public/agent-bundles`) and the "Personal Assistant" starter template (V502) never had the setting they appeared to. Every enum-typed field of an agent design (`providerName`, `tuningMode`, `reasoningDepth`, `autonomyTier`, `experienceMode`, `economyProviderName`, `fallbackProviderName`, `voiceModelProvider`, `videoModelProvider`) is now checked before anything is created: preview and import answer 400 naming the field, the bad value and the allowed values (a blank or absent value still means the default). The examples now say `FAST`, which is what they always ran with (small local models; no extra self-check calls), `V663` repairs the stored starter template the same way, `examples/agent-bundles/validate.py` checks the same fields, and tests keep the checker, the seeded templates and the shipped files in step with the enums. A hand-written file that used a value the server did not know now fails to import with a clear message instead of importing with a different setting.
- **One default per setting, and no setting that does nothing.** A key had two defaults when its `@Value` default and `application.yml` disagreed, and the one that applied depended on whether the yml was loaded. They now agree, and `ConfigurationDriftTest` (core/app) fails on a new disagreement, on a yml key no code reads, and on a key the code reads that `docs/configuration.md` does not name (reviewed exceptions, each with a reason, in `archrules/config-drift-allowlist.txt`). Code defaults moved to the yml value (no change to a running install): `app.agents.default-max-tokens` 1024 to 4096 (new agents, Assist and parameter inference), `app.guardrails.run-budget.usd` 0 to 2.0 (the stricter value: delegation spend is capped even without the yml), `app.tools.mcp-discovery-cache-ttl-seconds` 20 to 300, `app.scheduled-tasks.delivery-channels` 5 to 11 channels, `app.tools.search.always-on` gains `local_desktop_*`, and the health probes' defaults (`app.storage.endpoint`, `code-intel.base-url`, `app.workflow.temporal.target`, `management.otlp.tracing.endpoint`), `app.replica-mode` (A2A card signing) and the auxiliary model's Anthropic fallback now equal the yml. Settings that were set in the wrong place and did nothing now work: `AGENT_LAB_FREE_PLAN_ENABLED` and `EVALS_FREE_PLAN_ENABLED` (declared under `app.llm.*`, read from `app.agent-lab.*` / `app.evals.*`), the `PLATFORM_RELIABILITY_*` variables (declared under `app.tenancy.reliability.*`, read from `app.platform.reliability.*`; their defaults are unchanged), and `META_EMBEDDED_SIGNUP_*`, which never reached the guided WhatsApp service (it read `app.omnichannel.meta.embedded-signup.*`; that spelling still wins when set). `tool-sandbox.connect-timeout-millis` and `request-timeout-millis` are applied to the sandbox client (they were bound and ignored; defaults 5 s and 30 s now bound a call, where before the shared client default applied), and the two unread duplicates `AgentJobProperties.deadlineSweepInterval` and `AgentJobDurabilityProperties.stepJournalPurgeInterval` are gone (the `@Scheduled` placeholders already read the keys). `scripts/docs/gen-config-tables.py` and `scripts/ci/gen-config-reference.py` count a family row (`app.models.defaults.<provider>`) as naming its ancestor; the default tables of `docs/configuration.md` were regenerated and 67 rows for keys the guide did not name were added.
- **Vendor addresses, model defaults, retrieval pool, graph-memory limits and RL tuning are settings.** Each vendor's base URL was typed 3 to 4 times (validators, live model fetcher, hosted pool, model catalog); `app.llm.endpoints.<vendor>.base-url` is now the one place, so a vendor can sit behind a proxy or regional endpoint (must be http(s); defaults are the public endpoints). The per-provider default model, the retired-model replacements and both keyword orders are `app.models.*`, and `app.models.context-windows.<model>` overrides a catalog window. Knowledge search's RRF constant and candidate pool are `app.knowledge.search.*`; graph memory's 20-fact / 200-path recall caps, the arbitration context cap and the forget scan page are `app.graph-memory.*`; the RL starting exploration rate, REASONING_DEPTH and GEN_PARAMS arms, the UCB1 constant, the feedback-to-reward map and the export rating thresholds are `rl.policy.*` / `rl.feedback.*`, and `rl.policy.*` / `rl.reward.*` finally appear in `application.yml`. Every default equals the old constant. Still constants (other work packages own those files): the chat-client factories' base URLs, `LlmPricingCatalog` (table and fallback rate), `AgentModelSelectionService` tier presets, `ModelContextWindows` and the Ollama `num_ctx`, `AnthropicThinking`'s model pattern, and the memory recall top-k and distance cut-off. See docs/configuration.md.
- **Background model calls no longer take an arbitrary model, and the language heuristics no longer assume English.** Context compaction, the session summary and the "Ask AI" assist took `listModels(provider).get(0)` of whichever credential came first (the first model alphabetically). They now go through one resolver: the workspace's AI Model Control preference, then `app.auxiliary-model.<purpose>.provider/.model`, then the model of the agent the call is made for, then the best credential's catalog default; the choice is logged. Quick Task agent choice reads words in any script (it matched `[a-z0-9]` only). Cost routing no longer sends a message with accented Latin letters (Spanish, French, German, ...) to the economy model on the strength of English signal words (`app.routing.simple.accented-latin-simple`, default `false`). Default tables of `docs/configuration.md` that printed `"` or `" + CONST + "` show the real defaults; 21 keys the guide did not name are documented; `make config-docs` (`scripts/docs/gen-config-tables.py`) reports any default that drifts from the code.
- **The tool-call verifier no longer fails open on its own.** A RIGOROUS agent's verifier approved the call when its model call errored or answered nothing, while the injection judge and the dispatch gate failed closed under `app.guardrails.fail-mode`. It now follows `app.guardrails.tool-verifier.fail-mode` (default `inherit` = the global mode, so closed: the call is refused); set `open` to restore the old behaviour. The stale comments in `application.yml` and `docs/security/owasp-mapping.md` that said the semantic judge or the dispatch gate fail open are corrected.
- **An approval request can expire.** A request nobody decided waited, approvable, forever; approving it weeks later replayed a stale action. `app.approvals.expiry.ttl` (default `0` = off, unchanged) closes older pending requests: stored as rejected by `system:approval-expiry` with a model-facing reason, audited as `EXPIRED`, and a later approve is refused. A job parked on it fails as `APPROVAL_TIMEOUT` instead of "a human rejected".
- **Guardrail windows and judge sampling are settings.** The 16,000-character jailbreak scan window, the 4,000-character judge window (injection judge, toxicity judge, tool-call verifier, moderation classifier) and the toxicity block score of 60 are `app.guardrails.jailbreak.max-scanned-chars`, `app.guardrails.judge.max-input-chars` and `app.guardrails.toxicity.block-score`, with unchanged defaults. `app.guardrails.judge.temperature|max-tokens|model` pin the judges' sampling (unset = inherit the agent's, as before). The agent-job approval replay grace (90 s) and shutdown quiesce wait (3 s) are `app.agent-jobs.approval.replay-grace` and `app.agent-jobs.quiesce-worker-wait`.

- **Approving a tool call now tells the agent what happened.** The "approved action ... completed" note was written under the raw client session id while every turn reads its history under the scoped id, so no later turn (and no resumed agent job) ever saw it and the model asked for the same action again. The scoped id is now recorded in the approval's replay envelope and the note goes there; if it cannot be written the approver is warned instead of it being only a log line.
- **A turn parked on an approval is no longer nudged or abandoned.** The turn now records that it is parked (`TurnApprovalState`) after the approval request is stored, and the streamed-turn continuation loop skips a parked turn instead of guessing from the word "approval" in the reply. If the request cannot be recorded the model gets an error result and the turn is not left stuck. The text the model reads (`app.guardrails.approval-park.*`) no longer says "STOP NOW / do not continue the task"; it says the task resumes once approved. Other tool calls made in the same turn after the park are recorded as pending approvals (up to `max-queued-calls`) instead of being dropped.
- **The tool-step limit stops the turn.** It was turned into a "this tool call failed" result by the outermost tool wrapper and, after partial text, into a normal completion by the half-response closer; both now let it reach the caller (once the model has ignored the budget notice described in the next entries). A failed continuation pass is logged with its pass number and closes the reply with the interrupted marker.
- **A tool result over the size cap is truncated, not thrown away, and plain text is no longer called malformed.** The guard replaced any tool output over 20,000 characters with "failed validation and was not returned to the model" (the side effect had already happened) and withheld text that merely started with `[` or `{` (a log line, a numbered list) as "likely truncated JSON"; a second, lower 16 KB cap sat outside it, and the pattern guardrail's own truncation was unreachable. There is now one cap for every tool, built-in or MCP: `app.tools.output.max-chars` (20000, marker included) with a `per-tool` override map and a model-facing marker that states the original length and how much was cut. `workspace_read` pages to fit it, so its "read on with offset=N" note is no longer the part that is cut off. The other caps on the way (MCP 64 KB security ceiling, OpenAPI 16,000, step journal 64,000, approval record 32 KB) are documented next to it.
- **A turn that runs out of tool calls is told so as a tool result, and the documented limit is the one that applies.** Past `app.guardrails.max-tool-calls-per-turn` the guard threw, and the model was never told "stop calling tools": it got the generic "this tool call failed ... suggest what to try instead" for every later call (or, once that was fixed, a dead turn). Calls over the ceiling are now not run and answered with the same `{"error":"tool_budget_exhausted"}` result the tool loop uses, so the model can write its final answer; only a model that keeps calling after `app.guardrails.step-limit.grace-calls` (10) more calls is stopped with the step-limit error. A hidden constant of 25 tool ROUNDS per invocation silently ended turns that were configured for 30 calls (fully autonomous agents) or 150 (the `personal` profile); `app.chat.tools.max-rounds-per-invocation` is now in `application.yml` and, at its default `0`, derived from the call ceiling, so the call policy decides first. The autonomy tiers' scaling (half with a floor of 3, double) and the wording of both stops are configuration (`app.guardrails.step-limit.*`, `app.chat.tools.*`). See "Tool calls: budget, rounds and result size" in the configuration guide.
- **Clearing an old tool result no longer kills a Gemini turn.** The placeholder that replaces a tool result once a reply has read more than `app.chat.tools.result-clearing.threshold-chars` of tool output was a bracketed sentence; Spring AI's Gemini adapter parses every tool result as JSON and failed the turn on it, and the hosted pool is Gemini-first. It is now a JSON object (`{"context_cleared":true,"tool":...,"chars":...,"restore":...}`, the shape the token-budget compaction already used), checked against the adapter's real conversion in a test.
- **A reply cut off, refused or empty is no longer served, stored or cached as an answer.** The tool loop runs inside Spring AI, so the app only saw `.content()` and never why the model stopped. A turn now reads the provider's finish reason (Anthropic `stop_reason`, OpenAI `finish_reason`, Gemini `finishReason`, Ollama `done_reason`) on both the streamed (FAST) and the buffered depths: a reply cut off at the output limit is continued (up to `app.chat.finish.max-continuations`, default 2, no tools offered) and, if still cut off, ends with a notice; a reply the provider's safety system stopped says so instead of "could you try rephrasing your message?"; an unusable tool call (Gemini `MALFORMED_FUNCTION_CALL`) is retried once when no tool has run yet. None of those, nor the empty-reply line nor a reply closed after a failure, is written to the exact or semantic response cache, announced as a completed message or sent to the quality judge, and the transcript keeps them as cut off / failed replies (the model is not shown them as its own words). The "no response" line and every notice are configuration (`app.chat.finish.*`).
- **The hosted agent-turn endpoint no longer sends tools with empty arguments or blank call ids.** `POST /api/completions/agent-turn` turned a tool call with invalid or cut-off JSON into `{}` and let the client run it; it now hands the model the error as the call's result and asks again (`app.agent-turn.repair.max-attempts`, default 1), and drops the call with an explanatory reply if that fails. Gemini function calls, which carry no id, get a generated `call_...` id (the endpoint answered the client's next request with a 400 for the blank one). `finishReason` is normalised (`stop`, `length`, `content_filter`, `malformed_tool_call`, `tool_calls`) and a truncation is no longer overwritten with `tool_calls`.
- **A long tool call no longer kills the stream.** The 90 s stream idle timeout counted only tokens, but the tool loop runs inside the model stream and a tool (a browser task, a coding task, an agent-to-agent call) emits none: the timer declared the provider dead, the turn failed and the recovery ladder replayed it, tools included, on another provider. The timer now counts model silence only: a running tool suspends it, up to `app.chat.resilience.tool-idle-ceiling` (30 minutes, so a hung tool is still caught), and it starts again when the tool finishes.
- **A careful, rigorous or planned turn that runs out of time no longer throws its tool work away.** At `app.llm.http.buffered-turn-timeout` the turn was answered again at fast depth with its tools removed and without ever seeing what they returned, so the model could claim anything about work it had not seen. After tools ran, the turn is now reported with the steps that ran and the ones still running (`app.chat.resilience.depth-timeout-partial-notice`), kept in the transcript so the next turn sees them, and never cached; a turn that had run no tool is answered again at fast depth with its tools on offer. An agent job's own `timeoutSeconds` now raises the limit for its turns instead of being cut at 240 s.
- **A provider failure after a tool ran is no longer replayed on another provider.** The BYOK fallback provider and the hosted pool's next rung re-ran the whole turn when the failing one had streamed no text, but a tool-calling turn streams no text while it works, so a send, create or delete could run twice. A turn that ran a tool with side effects is now replayed only if the effect journal recorded every such call (`app.chat.resilience.replay-after-journaled-effects`); a tool still running, a write that ran without a journal record or an unclassified mutating tool blocks the failover and the failure is reported (`chat.failover.blocked`). The journal protects an identical repeat only, which the setting documents.
- **Transient provider failures are retried properly.** HTTP 429 and 5xx are retried with exponential backoff, jitter and the provider's own Retry-After (header, or the wait stated in the error body), on non-streaming calls and on a streaming turn that fails before its first token and before any tool ran (`app.llm.retry.*`); a Retry-After beyond `max-retry-after` goes to the fallback provider instead. `POST /api/completions/agent-turn` no longer walks the whole provider pool for an input-shaped failure (413/422, context length, content policy); it answers it at once.
- **A Gemini turn keeps its tools.** Spring AI hands the reply's thought signatures back to the function-call parts in order, and the platform stored the signature of every part, so a signed lead-in text part gave the function call somebody else's signature and Gemini refused the next request ("missing a thought_signature"); the turn was then silently re-run with every tool removed. The signatures stored are now the function-call parts' own, lined up with their calls (checked against Spring AI 1.1.8's real request conversion, streaming and not). If the provider still refuses, the turn is answered once without tools in the open: the reply opens with a notice naming the steps that had already run, the model is shown what the tools returned, the tools are not run again and the reply is not cached (`app.chat.resilience.thought-signature-*`; `false` reports the provider's error). The hosted `agent-turn` endpoint, whose client keeps the conversation, still does not carry signatures.
- **A reply is no longer re-prompted because it says "Let me know if you need anything else".** The check for a reply that stopped at a step it announced read the whole tail of any reply on any model, fired on closings, cost an extra model call, started its clock after the pass it was judging (so the nudge never carried the tool results), and re-sent the model a text summary instead of the conversation. It now looks at the last sentence only, treats an offer of help as a closing, runs by default only for a weak tool caller (a hosted row in small-model mode or measured text-only, or a local provider; `app.chat.continuation.heuristic-scope`), is never made for a turn parked on an approval, requires a pass after the first to have called a tool, and replays the real conversation (the model's tool calls, the tool results as tool messages, the original attachments). The same policy now applies to the careful, rigorous and planned depths, which had a separate check that re-ran the whole turn with the user's message edited; that check and its wording are gone. Patterns and nudge are per language (`app.chat.continuation.*`). Behaviour change: a frontier model's announce-and-stop reply is no longer re-prompted unless `heuristic-scope` is `ALL_MODELS`.
- **A short follow-up keeps the task's tools and its model.** "yes", "continue" and "try again" cut the agent's tools to the four that match nothing and, with cost routing, went to the economy model in the middle of a task. A message of at most `app.chat.follow-up.short-message-chars` characters now keeps the tools the session already used (window, count and the switch are settings) and, when the session used tools, stays on the agent's own model. Tool and complexity heuristics also work outside Latin script: words are matched in any script, text without spaces is matched as pairs of characters, a message in another script that matches no tool gets the full tool budget (`app.tools.auto-selection.unscorable-uses-full-budget`), and the "simple message" thresholds, sentence terminators and signal words are settings (`app.routing.simple.*`) instead of English constants.
- **The next turn can see what the last turn's tools did.** Session history stored the model's prose only, so on turn N+1 it could not see the file id a tool had returned, which write calls it had already made, or that an action was waiting on a person. Each assistant turn that called tools now stores a compact tool ledger with it (tool, arguments summary, result summary, status, approval request), masked like the tool-call log and cut to `app.chat.tool-ledger.*` limits, and the model is shown it ahead of that turn's reply, with a note that it is the platform's record and may be relied on. The stored reply text, the transcript and the tenant-scoped session key are unchanged.
- **FAST and buffered turns offer the same tools in the same order.** The buffered depths used the resolution order, so the tool list and the prompt-cache prefix changed with the reasoning depth. What still differs between the streamed and the buffered turn (which FAST turns are buffered, whether output guardrails withhold or only audit, the verifiers' no-tools rewrite) is now `app.chat.parity.*`; a rewrite that is a fraction of the answer it replaces is no longer accepted, and the evidence the verifiers see is bounded.
- **The small-model tool-call workaround no longer wraps every model.** Recovery of tool calls written as text, and its hold-back of any reply starting with `{`, `[` or a code fence, cost a frontier model its live streaming for a problem it does not have. It now wraps a known weak tool caller or a model of unknown capability (`app.chat.tools.text-call-recovery.scope`); `ALL_MODELS` restores the old behaviour.
- **Response caches no longer answer "yes" with another conversation's "yes", or a tool turn from a cache.** The exact-match and semantic caches keyed on the single message and served and stored any turn. They now key on the conversation before the message too, and an agent that offers tools is not cacheable (`app.chat.cache.*`).
- **SDK artifacts now ship the license they declare.** Every `io.github.cd-lc:*` jar carried the repository's BSL-1.1 `LICENSE`/`NOTICE` in `META-INF` while its POM declares Apache-2.0; the `sdk/` subtree now has its own Apache-2.0 `LICENSE` and `NOTICE` (also copied into the Python and TypeScript package directories), and the jars bundle those.
- **`ApprovalNotificationBlockedException` is a `GovernanceException` again.** It extended `RuntimeException`, so `catch (GovernanceException)` - the documented one-stop handler - missed it.
- **REST clients: streams, retries and errors are publish-grade.** The Python client's 60 s request timeout also applied to SSE reads, so a stream idle for a minute died; `stream()` now bounds connect+headers only (`http.client`, blocking reads). Both clients retry idempotent GET/HEAD on 429/5xx/network failures honouring `Retry-After`, re-resolve a callable token once on 401, wrap transport failures in a `NetworkError`, expose `code`, `headers` and `retry_after`/`retryAfterMs` on `ApiError`, and send an SDK `User-Agent`. The TypeScript client gains `timeoutMs` (client and per-call), `maxRetries` and `userAgent` options.
- **SDK metadata is publish-ready.** `vegaduta-agent` uses PEP 639 license metadata and carries `LICENSE`, coverage and mypy config and the missing `Security`/`OS Independent`/`Typed` classifiers; `vegaduta-sdk` (Python) gains full PyPI metadata; `vegaduta-sdk` (TypeScript) gains `exports`, `repository`/`bugs`/`keywords`, a `prepack` typecheck+build, a pinned `typescript` devDependency and a package `README.md`.

### Added

- **The v1.0.0 definition of done is written down** (`docs/maintainers/v1.0.0-definition-of-done.md`): what 1.0.0 promises and does not, the stability, deprecation, upgrade and support rules from 1.0.0, the release checklist with the evidence each box needs, the requested lean personal edition and the Spring Boot 4.1 / Spring AI 2.0 candidate (in or out is the project lead's decision), and the measured personal start-up numbers with a proposed target.
- **A release path for the generated REST clients.** `.github/workflows/rest-sdk-release.yml` verifies, builds, attests and publishes the npm and PyPI `vegaduta-sdk` packages for `restsdk-v*` tags (publish stays off until `SDK_PUBLISH_NPM`/`SDK_PUBLISH_PYPI` are set); setup and procedure in `docs/maintainers/releasing-rest-sdks.md`. New tests cover the classification package (lethal-trifecta detector, resolver overrides, composite and annotated sources) and the Spring AI wiring, plus the retry/timeout/error paths in both REST runtimes.
- **`GET /api/public/status` reports the edition.** A new `edition` object, `{tier, billingEnabled, authMode, features}`, where `features` maps each feature (`billing-payments`, `social-channels`, `a2a-directory`, `mcp-server-exposure`, `operator-console`, `cloud-speech`, `voice-realtime-share`, `video-demo-media`) to on or off.
