It is computed by the same code the bean conditions use, so it cannot disagree with what was registered. OpenAPI and the SDK models are regenerated.
- **One variable chooses the edition: `VEGADUTA_EDITION`** (`personal` default, `lite`, `full`) in `deploy/compose/.env`. `deploy/compose/personal.yml` derives core's `APP_EDITION_TIER` and the console's `NEXT_PUBLIC_EDITION` from it, so the two cannot disagree (`WEB_AUTH_MODE` and `WEB_BILLING_ENABLED` remain for the console's sign-in and billing flags), and `scripts/vegaduta` writes it into `.env` (never replacing a value you set) and exports it to a `--native-core` JVM. `NEXT_PUBLIC_DEPLOY_ENV` only labels the top bar now.
- **Switch one feature back on without changing the edition.** `app.edition.features.<name>.enabled`, for example `APP_EDITION_FEATURES_CLOUD_SPEECH_ENABLED=true` in core's environment (`deploy/compose/core.env`), then restart core: beans are decided once at boot. Names are the feature ids above with `-` written as `_` in the variable. The value is read like any boolean property (`true`/`false`, `1`/`0`, `on`/`off`, `yes`/`no`); an explicit value beats the tier, and for `billing-payments` it also beats `app.edition.billing-enabled`. Cloud speech is the intended add-on: set the flag, restart core, then add the provider key in the console. `VEGADUTA_EDITION=lite` or `full` turns every feature on at once. See [Configuration](docs/configuration.md#edition-tier-and-features).
- **Tests that keep the editions honest.** `EditionWiringTest` and `EditionClosureRulesTest` find gated classes by annotation (no list to maintain) and fail the build when a bean gated by one feature is injected by an ungated bean, since that would crash startup only on a personal install. `scripts/ci/boot-check.sh --personal` boots the personal profile set and prints a start-up phase table (`make boot-check-personal`); that boot has not been run for this change.
- **Service environment variables are documented.** `docs/configuration.md` has a "Service environment" section with the defaults and effect of about 100 variables read by sidecar, code-intel, app-builder, tool-sandbox, workspace-runner, voice-agent, rl-trainer and mcp-stdio-runner (capacity, tenant-isolation and sandbox settings that were written nowhere).
- **The 1.x API stability policy is written down.** `docs/api/DEPRECATION.md` has a "Stability from 1.0.0" section: what is covered, what is not, which release may change what, and that a deprecated item is removed only in the next major release. It takes effect with 1.0.0.
- **The Windows launcher is tested on Windows.** A `windows-launcher` job in `ci.yml` (part of CI OK) runs Pester tests (`tests/install/vegaduta-ps1.Tests.ps1`) against `scripts/vegaduta.ps1` under both Windows PowerShell 5.1 and PowerShell 7, with a fake `docker`: syntax and cmdlet-parameter compatibility with 5.1, the secrets, native stderr under `Stop`, `personal -DryRun`, `upgrade`, `models pull`. `personal` also takes `-DryRun` (print the plan, start nothing) and `-Search` / `-NoSearch` (private web search, as `--search` / `--no-search`; off unless asked), and the state file gains the `search=`, `tools=` and `browser=` keys the bash launcher and the Setup app read.
- **A documentation page for every channel, and a channels overview.** `docs/channels/` now has a page each for WhatsApp (Meta and Twilio), Messenger and Instagram, Slack, Discord, Telegram, Teams, email, X and voice, plus `docs/channels/README.md`: one table of all fifteen channel types (how each connects, its endpoint, what you provide, its page), what every channel shares and how each inbound call is authenticated. The docs site gets a Channels section (the existing channel pages moved into it in the navigation only), and `scripts/ci/channel-docs-check.sh` (in `make gates` and the static gates) fails when a `ChannelType` value has no row in the overview.

- **Twenty-two more settings an owner can change without the operator, and a decision for every other property (package G6).** The settings catalog gains `agent.steps.*` (calls answered past the tool limit, and how the autonomy tiers scale it), `chat.semanticCache.*`, `chat.followUp.{maxStickyTools,keepPrimaryModel}`, `chat.toolLedger.*` (workspace-wide), `chat.attachments.max{Image,Audio,Video,Document}Mb`, `memory.tenant.maxMemories`, `memory.edge.maxFacts` and `skills.prompt.{maxSkills,maxBlockChars}`; each is read where the behaviour happens, defaults to the property it replaces, and is bounded. The AI Model Control page gains rows for the background compaction and session-summary models. The four `agent.steps.*` keys need a workspace administrator (an agent editor cannot undo the tighter ceiling of a supervised agent). `docs/settings.md` lists every other new property as deployment-only (with a reason written for that property) or deferred.
- **The turn tells the playground, in fields, when it retries, fails over, continues, waits for approval and how the reply ended (package G2).** A chat stream that sets `stepEvents` now carries five lifecycle step events besides the tool and run events: `finish {finishReason, detail?}` (once per reply, before `run_finished`; `stop`, `length`, `safety`, `malformed_tool_call`, `empty`, `interrupted`), `retry {attempt, maxAttempts, reason}` (a model call tried again before its first token, or an unusable tool call asked for again), `failover {toModel?, reason}` (the agent's fallback provider or the next rung of the hosted pool), `continuation {pass, maxPasses, reason}` (`cut_off`, `unfinished_step`, `approval`) and `approval_required {tool, approvalId}` (after the held call's `tool_result`). Fast and buffered depths send the same events. The console's live chips ("Continuing", "Trying again", "Switched to a backup model", "Waiting for your approval") now show against the real backend, with the reason in the language bundle's words, and "Continue" for a cut-off reply and the refusal notice are keyed on `finishReason`, so rewording `app.chat.finish.*` or another locale cannot remove them; the reply-text markers remain only as the fallback for a turn with no `finish` event. A turn that did not set `stepEvents` is byte-identical. Documented in `docs/api/README.md` and as `agentStep_*` messages of `docs/api/asyncapi.json`.
- **A run can be narrowed to some of the agent's tools without changing the agent (package G1).** The Run settings drawer's tool switches used to edit the agent's saved tool access, for everyone, behind a confirmation. They are now for one run only: `ChatRequest.toolNames` (and `ChatTurnRequest.toolNames`) narrows a turn to the listed tools of the agent. It only narrows: a name the agent does not resolve for the caller is refused with a 400 that names it, a kept tool is the same wrapped callback behind the same approval, autonomy and guardrail gates, a tool the person picked is kept out of relevance trimming, an RL tool-subset arm does not further cut a selection the person made, and the tool-search pair is not kept alongside (it would reach tools the selection left out). Omitted changes nothing (byte-identical request and turn); an empty list runs the turn without tools. The selection rides the approval replay envelope (`replay.runToolNames`) so the turn the platform starts after a human decides keeps it (a tool removed from the agent since is dropped instead of stopping the task), and the turn journal's INTAKE event lists it as `runTools=` (shown in the turn timeline). The playground keeps the switches as session state, sends `toolNames` with each message, labels them "This run only", shows a header chip while a run is narrowed, and has a reset; it no longer writes the agent. `openapi.json`, the SDKs and `docs/platform-documentation/api/streaming.html` carry the field.
- **Every approved action resumes the agent, not only a replayed MCP tool (package C5).** A code-workspace run or apply, an agent's channel send and any other action a tool holds for approval inside its own `call()` used to leave the agent waiting for ever: only the `mcp_tool.*` executor wrote the outcome into the conversation and started the turn that carries the task on. `HumanApprovalGateService` now reports how every approval ended (ran, failed for good, denied or expired) through `ApprovalOutcomePort`, whichever executor ran it; `ApprovalOutcomeFollowUp` (agents-tools) writes the note into the scoped history and starts the same continuation turn (the goal restated, the real result capped by `app.approvals.continuation.max-result-chars`, the denial reason; once per request). A tool that gates itself records its request with `evaluateInTurn`, which adds the conversation envelope (the tool, agent and session, the user's goal, the scoped history id) next to the action's own fields; the tool guard opens a `ToolCallScope` around each tool call for that. Who may decide a request, the admin-only rule and `requestedBy` are unchanged, an auto-approved action (it runs inside the turn that asked) and an action raised outside an agent turn are recorded as before and not continued. An approved code-workspace apply now records what it did on the request (it recorded nothing), the inline approval card and the approvals queue show such an action's own fields, and the chat finds it in its conversation by the envelope. The notes written after a call ran or failed, their length cut and the stand-in for "ran, no output" are `app.approvals.continuation.completed-note|failed-note|completed-no-output-text|max-note-result-chars` (defaults byte-identical to the old text). See `docs/configuration.md`.
- **A settings catalog: tenant admins and agent editors can change the settings that shape agent behaviour, with bounds, risk levels, history and audit (package B0).** New module `settings` and `GET /api/settings/catalog`, `GET /api/settings/effective`, `PUT`/`DELETE /api/settings/tenant/{key}`, `PUT`/`DELETE /api/agents/{agentId}/settings/{key}` and `GET /api/settings/{key}/history` over 37 curated keys (the seven runtime limits, approvals, reply finishing, retries, caching, follow-ups, routing, memory, delegation, evaluation and the guardrail strictness knobs). Each entry names its type, unit, bounds, risk, scopes and the existing property it defaults from; resolution is agent over tenant over platform over code default through `SettingsResolver`. Security entries can only be made stricter than the platform value, by a tenant admin, with a typed reason; `app.settings.locked.<key>` pins a key for everybody. Every change is versioned in the immutable `setting_override_history` (V668), audited with actor, old value, new value and reason, and the runtime limits keep their storage so the older `/runtime-limits` endpoints and the catalog stay consistent. See `docs/settings.md`.

### Changed

- **Auxiliary Postgres containers move to PostgreSQL 18.** `keycloak-db`, `temporal-postgresql` and `langfuse-postgres` now run `postgres:18.4-alpine`, and their volumes mount at `/var/lib/postgresql` to match the 18+ image layout. Fresh installs are unaffected; **existing installs must migrate the pre-18 volumes** before the new image starts (the 18+ image refuses both an old cluster and the old `/var/lib/postgresql/data` mount). The `upgrade`/`backup` dump covers all three databases and [deploy/README.md](deploy/README.md#upgrade) walks through recreate-and-restore.

## [0.1.9] - 2026-10-21

### Added

- **Conversations follow you.** `GET /api/chat/threads` lists the signed-in user's chat sessions (title, agent, last activity) and `DELETE /api/chat/threads/{agentId}/{sessionId}` removes one; the Assistant merges them into its thread list, so history shows on a new browser or device. Needs `APP_CHAT_BRANCHING_ENABLED=true` (404 otherwise; the console then keeps its local list). The Assistant now records its turns in the conversation tree, and reopening a thread older than the 24 h session window reads the stored tree.

### Fixed

- **Service and database images clear the release's critical-vulnerability gate again.** Two advisories published after v0.1.8 stopped the v0.1.9 image scan: Pillow 12.3.0 (critical GHSA-8g5g-8vmr-9782, fixed only in 13.0.0) in the sidecar, voice-agent and code-intel images, and certifi 2026.10.18 inside the CloudNativePG base of the `postgres:16-pgvector-age` image. Pillow is now 13.0.0 in all three; code-intel needs `services/code-intel/overrides.txt` for that, since fastembed caps Pillow below 13 although code-intel only uses its text embeddings, and so installs its lock with `--no-deps`. The CNPG image replaces its certifi with the hash-pinned 2026.10.19 wheel at build time (`deploy/postgres/replace-certifi.py`), because its pip is removed upstream.
- **Service images carry certifi 2026.10.19.** certifi 2026.7.22, pinned in the app-builder, code-intel, rl-trainer, sidecar and voice-agent locks, has a critical advisory (GHSA-6q48-6c7w-9gw2) published after v0.1.8, and the release's image scan stopped on it. The five locks now pin 2026.10.19; nothing else changes.
- **WhatsApp QR messages are no longer split between processes.** The jobs and worker shapes ran a second WhatsApp QR receiver next to the API's, so on a full install with the bridge each took a share of a linked number's incoming messages. They now switch it off, like the Signal receiver (`app.whatsappqr.receive.enabled: false` in `application-jobs.yml` and `application-worker.yml`).
- **The chat artifacts directive keys sit where the code reads them.** The `artifacts` block in `application.yml` was nested under `app.chat.branching`, where nothing reads it; it now sits under `app.chat.directives`. Defaults and the `APP_CHAT_DIRECTIVES_ARTIFACTS_*` variables are unchanged.
- **A personal install keeps chat history.** The 0.1.8 note said personal installs already had it on; they did not: `personal.yml` never set `APP_CHAT_BRANCHING_ENABLED` and core defaults it to off, so the Assistant's history stayed in one browser and a conversation expired after 24 h. The `personal` profile now turns it on (`app.chat.branching.enabled`, still `APP_CHAT_BRANCHING_ENABLED=false` in `deploy/compose/core.env` to switch it off). Conversations that had already expired are not recovered.
- **A personal install's local model answers again by itself after a secrets change.** When the install kept its database but got a new `.env` (a re-install, or a repaired install), the local model's platform-pool entry was still listed as "in use" while every chat answered "No platform LLM is configured": its stored key no longer decrypted under the new `APP_ENCRYPTION_KEY`, so the chat path left it out. Core now repairs that entry (re-keys it, re-enables it, switches the pool back on) at every start and, when a chat still finds the pool empty, on the spot during that chat. The troubleshoot page says why the entry cannot be used instead of calling it in use, and the chat notice points to Troubleshoot.
- **Windows: the personal install no longer stops with "required variable WORKSPACE_RUNNER_SECRET is missing a value".** The PowerShell launcher (`scripts/vegaduta.ps1`, which the Setup app runs on Windows) generated a shorter list of secrets than `scripts/vegaduta`: it lacked `WORKSPACE_RUNNER_SECRET`, which `personal.yml` and `lite.yml` require, and `SEARXNG_SECRET`, which web search requires. It now generates both, and an existing install gets them on its next start. A test fails CI when the two lists differ again.
- **No secret a compose file requires can stop an install again.** Both launchers (`scripts/vegaduta`, `scripts/vegaduta.ps1`) now generate every secret-like variable any file under `deploy/compose` marks as required (so the opt-in WhatsApp QR and DefectDojo overlays no longer need hand-made secrets), not only the ones in their own table; and before `up`, each asks Docker Compose itself what is still missing and fills it, stopping with a plain "set X in .env" only for a value that is not a secret. The PowerShell launcher also no longer crashes on a Compose error written to stderr while it checks the database password. `tests/install` covers all three paths.

## [0.1.8] - 2026-10-21

### Fixed

- **The first chat no longer waits for the platform model to wake up.** A hosted model that scales to zero took 70 s or more to answer the first message. The console now asks core to load it (`POST /api/platform-llm/warm`, a one-token request to the pool's top row, at most once every 4 minutes per core instance) as soon as someone signs in or opens a chat. If a reply still has not started after 8 seconds, the chat says the model is starting up and that later replies are fast. Off with `APP_PLATFORM_LLM_WARM_ENABLED=false`.

### Changed

- **Chat history is kept by default on full installs and in the Helm chart.** `APP_CHAT_BRANCHING_ENABLED` now defaults to `true` in `deploy/compose/docker-compose.yml` (lite and personal already had it) and the chart gains `core.branchingEnabled` (default `true`), so each message is stored in Postgres for 30 days instead of expiring from Redis after 24 h. Conversations that had already expired are not recovered. Set it to `false` to go back.
- **Local models answer sooner.** The Compose `local-ai` overlay now loads the model when the stack starts and keeps it in memory (`OLLAMA_KEEP_ALIVE=-1`, `OLLAMA_FLASH_ATTENTION=1`), so the first reply no longer pays a cold start, and sends `reasoning_effort: none` so reasoning models skip their hidden thinking pass (`LOCAL_LLM_REASONING_EFFORT` changes it; new core property `app.llm.ollama.reasoning-effort`, blank by default outside the overlay). The Assistant shows a short note when it runs on a local model, with a link to Agent settings where it can be tuned for speed or quality.
- **Academy links everywhere, Academy source nowhere.** The Academy is a separate, proprietary deployable, so the open-source console links to it but carries none of its content: the in-console `/learn` index, the screen→lesson map (`consoleLessonMap.ts`), the curriculum index and every lesson-id deep link are removed, and per-screen "Learn this" buttons become plain links to the Academy. The OSS landing header and footer, the docs site nav and every tier's compose/Helm defaults point at the hosted `academy.vegaduta.ai` (`WEB_ACADEMY_URL`/`NEXT_PUBLIC_ACADEMY_URL`, still overridable; `""` hides the links on a fork). The personal tutor stays at `/learn/tutor`.

## [0.1.7] - 2026-10-21

### Fixed

- **Windows Setup no longer fails with "A required privilege is not held by the client".** The release package carried one symlink (`deploy/models/catalog.txt`), which Windows only lets administrators or Developer Mode create, so Setup stopped while unpacking. The release now ships the file itself, and Setup copies the file whenever the system refuses a symlink.
- **A database left by an earlier install no longer stops the start.** Installing into a new folder generates a new database password, but the data volume kept the earlier install's one, so `db-roles` failed ("password authentication failed", exit 2) and nothing came up. `personal` and `up` (bash and PowerShell) now notice this, set the database to this install's password (the data is kept) and start again.
- **Re-installing never locks you out of your own data.** The install's secrets (database passwords, `APP_ENCRYPTION_KEY`) are also kept outside the install folder (`~/.config/vegaduta/secrets.env`, `%LOCALAPPDATA%\VegaDuta\secrets.env` on Windows and WSL), so a new folder reuses them. When Docker holds an earlier install's data and no copy exists, that data is left untouched and the new install starts under its own project (`VEGADUTA_PERSONAL_PROJECT`).
- **Windows: re-running Setup with VegaDūta already running works.** The personal checks no longer fail on ports 3000/8080 held by VegaDūta's own containers, use `WEB_HOST_PORT`/`CORE_HOST_PORT`, and also catch ports Windows reserves (Hyper-V/WinNAT), which look free but cannot be used. Setup stops with the fix instead of retrying a port conflict three times.
- **Windows: personal installs get Valkey and the tools runner, as on macOS/Linux.** `vegaduta.ps1` now adds the `valkey` profile and, with the Tools or Browser add-on, routes STDIO connectors to `mcp-stdio-runner`.
- **A failed model download no longer stalls the install for an hour.** The Ollama container retries the download and restarts after five failures instead of staying up but never healthy.

## [0.1.6] - 2026-10-20

### Fixed

- **The local model follows Ollama when it moves.** Installing Ollama on the computer after an install that used the Ollama container now switches `personal` to it (Windows and macOS/Linux launchers) instead of keeping the container, and core repoints its own "Local model (Ollama)" pool row to the new address and model. Before, the row kept calling the old Ollama, so the console showed a local model in use while every chat failed. A row whose model is not on its Ollama (never pulled, or removed) switches to the configured model when that one is there, and an Ollama that is installed but not running (after a restart, or its app was closed) is started; if it still does not answer, the container takes over.
- **No GPU, no GPU mode.** Docker on WSL2 lists the nvidia runtime even on a laptop without a graphics card, which switched the Ollama container to GPU mode and made it fail to start ("no adapters were found"). The GPU overlay is now used only when `nvidia-smi` reports an adapter.
- **Installing from a checkout or a download of `main` just works.** An empty `VEGADUTA_VERSION` used to stop `personal` with an error; it now uses the latest published release and records it in `.env`.

### Changed

- **The local model's context window follows Docker's memory.** Under 6 GB the container model runs with an 8192-token window, under 10 GB 16384, otherwise the 32768 default (`LOCAL_LLM_NUM_CTX` overrides), so a small laptop is not pushed into out-of-memory kills by the model's working memory.
- **Local AI stays the default on small machines.** `personal` no longer drops to the cloud-key path below 8 GB of RAM: it picks the smallest catalog model (CPU only is fine) and warns that the first reply is slow; only machines under 4 GB keep the cloud-key path. The model is now sized to the memory Docker actually has when it runs in the Ollama container (a WSL2 or Docker Desktop VM is often much smaller than the machine), with a hint to raise Docker's memory for a bigger one. `--local-ai` given by hand now gets a model sized the same way instead of Docker's `qwen3.5:4b` default, and under WSL2 the script offers (with a yes, a backup and the one restart step) to give Docker 8 GB through `%UserProfile%\.wslconfig`.

## [0.1.5] - 2026-10-20

### Changed

- **Personal installs come with a free local model.** `personal` (bash and `vegaduta.ps1`, so the Setup app too) now picks the model that fits the machine's RAM from the shared catalog and downloads it without asking: free open-weights models, no account or subscription. On macOS (Homebrew) and Windows (`winget`, per-user) Ollama itself is installed and started when missing, so the model runs natively and can use the GPU; Linux and WSL, and any failed install, use the Ollama container. Previously it only adopted an Ollama that was already installed, so most machines ended up with no model. `--cloud` (`-Cloud`) opts out and is remembered; machines under 8 GB of RAM keep the cloud-key path; installs that never chose get the model on their next run. The first start waits up to an hour for the download, and the Setup app gives Docker room for the model.

### Changed

- **Setup app repairs the machine instead of stopping.** Clicking Install now installs Docker Desktop (silently accepting its licence, so no first-launch screen), starts it, turns on WSL2 on Windows (one permission prompt; after a required restart Setup reopens by itself), gives Docker's WSL2 VM enough memory through `%USERPROFILE%\.wslconfig` when the computer can spare it, and turns on Docker's start-at-sign-in so the stack returns after a reboot. A failed start is classified (stale registry login, full disk, stopped engine, interrupted download, slow first boot), repaired and retried up to three times. Missing or stopped Docker no longer blocks the Install button. The setup app also accepts the `SHA256SUMS.txt` asset name used by v0.1.0.
- **Merged landing page and release-aware public copy.** The signed-out homepage keeps the OSS landing's technical spine (capabilities, standards, SDK-as-dependency, install paths) under the marketing chrome (ambient backdrop, display typeface), and adds direct "Download the release" and "Try it live" calls to action. Install instructions now use the signed release artifacts (desktop installers, pinned GHCR images, the published Helm chart) instead of a source build, and every public license mention follows the Business Source License 1.1 relicensing (SDK stays Apache-2.0).
- **Tenant surfaces stop offering server-side controls.** On a full install (`NEXT_PUBLIC_DEPLOY_ENV=full`) `/add-ons` leaves the rail and renders an "all add-ons included" notice instead of switches, since every extra already ships. The local-model wizard (`/providers/local-model`), its entry points on `/providers`, the local-model card on `/troubleshoot` and the Assistant's model warm-up are now personal-install only (`localModelAllowed`) — on lite and full the LLM pools are operator-managed infrastructure a tenant can browse but not change.

### Security

- **Browser images ship the current stable Chrome.** core-full and mcp-stdio-runner-browser install Chrome-for-Testing stable at build time (behind a fixed `current` path for `PUPPETEER_LAUNCH_OPTIONS`) and drop the Chrome 131 builds puppeteer@23.11.1 downloads for itself, so the release image scan no longer fails on fixed critical CVEs.
- **Release images move off Debian bookworm.** Node services to `*-trixie-slim` (22.23.3 / 24.21.0), Python services to `3.12.15-slim-trixie`, the JRE to `eclipse-temurin:21.0.12.1_1-*-noble`, PostgreSQL to `postgres:16.15-trixie` and CloudNativePG to `16.15-system-trixie`. The release's critical-vulnerability gate now fails only where a fixed package exists (`only-fixed`), and `.grype.yaml` records the scoped wont-fix exceptions (re-matched automatically the day Debian ships a backport).

### Changed

- **The OSS landing now sells the whole suite.** `/` leads with three ways in - run the platform (Personal, Lite, Full, Kubernetes), build on the SDKs (Java, Python, REST clients), extend with plugins and apps (IDEs, browsers, mobile, MCP) - plus a "Get VegaDūta" section with per-OS installer cards, the terminal installer, Helm and the verification step.

### Fixed

- **Downloads page shows the brand name correctly.** Headings use the `BrandName` wordmark (orange "Dūta") instead of plain text.

## [0.1.4] - 2026-10-19
### Changed

- **License: Business Source License 1.1.** The platform (everything outside `sdk/`) is now under BSL-1.1: free for any use except offering the software itself to third parties as a competing hosted service, and each version converts to Apache-2.0 four years after its release. The `sdk/` directory stays Apache-2.0 so clients embed anywhere. Versions already released under Apache-2.0 remain under Apache-2.0.

### Added

- **Assistant on every install.** `/assistant` is no longer personal-only: the rail lists it on lite and full too, and each person keeps their own Assistant agent (the lookup no longer adopts a teammate's shared agent that happens to be named "Assistant"). `/add-ons` also appears on lite, where its commands now render as `scripts/vegaduta up --lite ...`; the full stack keeps it off the rail (its service set is fixed at deploy time).
- **Lite: the single-box add-ons work on `up --lite`.** `--profile tools` runs STDIO MCP connectors in a new `mcp-stdio-runner` container (its own network, no published port, no volumes), `--profile workspace` adds `workspace-runner` and the Assistant's projects folder (`VEGADUTA_PROJECTS_DIR`), and `--browser` layers the browser-runner overlay. Lite ships the Assistant's features on (routines, editable/persistent threads, resumable answers, data tables, learned skills and proposals) — each is an env switch away; without `--profile rag` core now reports the sidecar honestly off instead of failing calls to a missing host, and `APP_EMBEDDINGS_PROVIDER=auto` lets a cloud key or local Ollama serve embeddings.
- **Full: the recent feature flags are wired.** `SCHEDULED_TASKS_ENABLED`, `APP_CHAT_BRANCHING_ENABLED`, `APP_CHAT_RESUMABLE_STREAMS_ENABLED(+_STORE)`, `DATA_TABLES_ENABLED`, `APP_SKILLS_LEARNING_ENABLED`, `APP_LEARNING_PROPOSALS_ENABLED`, `BROWSER_V2/PROFILES/TAKEOVER_ENABLED`, `APP_ADDONS_TENANT_ADMIN_MAY_CHANGE` and `APP_ADDONS_HOST_TOKEN` now pass through `.env` (all keep their shipped-off defaults; core.env remains the escape hatch for the rest).
- **Realm reconcile runs on every boot, not only via the CLI.** Keycloak's realm import does not substitute `${env.*}` inside a client `secret`, so the `agentic-ai-core` client lands with the literal placeholder and every Keycloak admin call (tenant signup included) answers 401 until `reconcile-realm.kcadm.sh` runs. A new one-shot `keycloak-reconcile` service (lite and full) shares keycloak's network namespace, waits for it healthy, and runs the same script — a plain `docker compose up` now converges too.
- **Tier-aware setup guidance.** The Ollama-unreachable message, the local-model troubleshoot page and the browser-add-on empty state name the command for the install's shape (`scripts/vegaduta personal --local-ai=host`, `up --lite ...`, `up --full ...`) instead of always `personal`.
- **`/downloads`: a public page listing the latest release's artifacts.** The Setup-app cards (macOS .dmg, Windows .zip, Linux tarballs), the `curl | sh` installer, the source tarball, SHA256SUMS + sigstore bundle, GHCR images and the Helm chart all link to real release assets (the versioned dmg name can never be guessed), resolved from the GitHub releases API server-side and cached an hour. With no release yet the cards fall back to the releases tab and the page says so. Linked from the OSS landing nav and footer; the route is public.
- **Add-ons on every tier, with honest switches.** `/add-ons` is no longer hidden on the full stack: the overview now carries `mayChange` (the caller's AddonAccess verdict) so the console disables the switches for a caller who cannot change the set instead of letting the click 403, entries the full launcher cannot run (`rag`, `workspace`) are marked "not available on this installation tier", the apply command drops them, `enable` refuses them (`not_available`), and `--browser` renders as `--profile browser` (the full stack's runner swap, not the single-box overlay).
- **Personal: `--minimal` and `--no-valkey` for the smallest stack.** `scripts/vegaduta personal --minimal` runs just `postgres` + `core` + `web` (no SearXNG, no MCP runner, no Valkey, no browser); `--no-valkey` / `VEGADUTA_VALKEY=0` drops only Valkey and keeps the rest. Without the store, core's rate limits, budget windows and spend counters run in the bounded in-process fallback (`LocalRateLimitFallback`) — still enforced, still fail-closed; the choice is remembered in `.vegaduta-state`. Lite/Full keep Valkey always on.
- **Omnibus image: the personal tier in one container.** `ghcr.io/cd-lc/omnibus:<version>` carries PostgreSQL (pgvector + Apache AGE), a Redis-protocol store, core and the console behind one `docker run`; the release workflow builds it after the per-service images publish and signs it like the rest. Data and generated secrets live under `/data`. Tradeoffs: docs/personal-mode.md, "One container".
- **Slimmer image options.** `core/Dockerfile` accepts `--build-arg JRE=jlink` for a jlink-minimal runtime (same jammy base, ~half the Java footprint; the omnibus image uses it), and `deploy/postgres/Dockerfile.alpine` builds the same pgvector + Apache AGE extensions on `postgres:16-alpine` for size-sensitive installs.

### Fixed

- **Redis-backed counters no longer stall when the store is down.** After one failed Redis call, `RedisFixedWindowCounter` skips Redis for ~10 s instead of paying a connect timeout on every request before the in-process fallback engages — the same path `--no-valkey` installs depend on.
- **CI and security-scan pipeline.** The license scan pins the ScanCode 32.5.0
  toolchain stack it was released against, SARIF uploads (Trivy, OWASP ZAP,
  grype) now skip cleanly on repositories without code scanning enabled, the
  web typecheck job gets a larger Node heap, the internal-reference lint
  baseline is fully drained, and the docs-link gate passes again.

## [0.1.3] - 2026-10-19

### Added

- **Scheduled tasks: Signal, Matrix and iMessage delivery.** A task's result can now land on the personal-mode channels too, with the same approval gate, per-channel recipient validation and in-app fallback as the existing channels; the console form lists them. `SCHEDULED_TASKS_DELIVERY_CHANNELS` defaults to all channels on.
- **Chat-first personal install.** On a one-person (local-auth) install `/` lands on the Assistant (`/assistant`) instead of the Build Studio — the chat is the product there.
- **Browser device node.** `DevicePlatform.WEB` + `POST /api/devices/capabilities` + a `capabilities` column (V660): a paired device now declares what it can do. The new public `/device` page turns any browser into a device node — claim a pairing code, declare its honest browser capabilities (camera still, geolocation, notifications, clipboard, one-shot screen share, vibration), then execute queued commands (`GET_LOCATION`, `CAPTURE_PHOTO`, `SCREENSHOT`, `NOTIFY`, `VIBRATE`, `CLIPBOARD_*`) through the platform's own permission prompts. Add to Home Screen to keep it alive.
- **Scheduled tasks: `a2a` delivery.** A task can deliver its result to an external A2A-speaking agent (card URL as the recipient) through the new `A2aDeliveryPort` — the same operator allow-list, SSRF guard and signed-card verification as the `a2a_call_agent` tool, so an external agent gateway can act as the last mile for proactive messages.
- **Scheduled tasks: `voice` delivery.** A task can place an outbound phone call that speaks its result through the new `VoiceDeliveryPort` on top of the tenant's voice credential (Twilio today), trimmed to ~900 characters.
- **Setup app packaging.** New `installer/package.sh` builds the downloadable artifacts reproducibly: a universal `VegaDuta Setup.app` in a `.dmg` on macOS (optional `--payload` embeds the release tarball for a fully offline install, `--web-port`/`--core-port` stamp the launcher's ports), a Windows zip whose exe ships with a `READ-ME-FIRST.txt` explaining SmartScreen's "More info → Run anyway", and Linux tarballs with a README. `release.yml` gains a `setup-pkg-darwin` job so the `.dmg` is a release asset covered by `SHA256SUMS`; `installer.yml` can produce it on manual dispatch. Still unsigned - Gatekeeper/SmartScreen warn once and the READMEs say what to click.
- **Personal: the Assistant's browser as a light add-on.** `scripts/vegaduta personal --browser` (PowerShell `-Profile browser`; `--no-browser` turns it off) adds one `browser-runner` container (`services/browser-runner`, a Playwright 1.49.0 server with Chromium, `deploy/compose/browser.yml`) that the slim core attaches to with `app.browseruse.remote-url` (`APP_BROWSERUSE_REMOTE_URL`), instead of the 6 GB `core-full` image. The runner publishes no port, drops all capabilities, has a read-only root and a 2 GB limit, and sits on a network shared only with core. The Browser button and `GET /api/browser/automation/capabilities` report an unreachable add-on with its address. Verified with slim core plus the runner on a live stack and with `BrowserRemoteRealTest` (opt-in, set `BROWSER_REMOTE_URL`): navigate, read, screenshot, click and the private-address refusal against a real Chromium.
- **Personal: sign in without an e-mail address and password.** Passkeys (Touch ID, Windows Hello, a phone or a security key), a **PIN** of 6 to 12 digits that works only together with a secret stored in the browser you set it up in (ten wrong PINs revoke that browser), and one-time **recovery codes** now sit alongside passwords. First-run and invite setup can finish with a passkey or a PIN instead of a password; the account then has no password at all and shows 8 recovery codes once. The sign-in screen leads with a passkey button and a PIN pad and asks for no e-mail address unless you choose a password. **Account security** (`/login?security=1`) adds or removes passkeys and PIN devices and makes new recovery codes; these changes need a sign-in from the last 10 minutes, and the last way in cannot be removed. Passkeys need HTTPS on a real host name or `http://localhost`: an IP address cannot use them, and a changed host name invalidates them. New migration V655, `app.auth.local.passkey.origins` to limit the allowed addresses. Tested with a software authenticator and in component tests; not yet tried with a real passkey or in a browser.
- **Personal: web search and connectors are on from the first start.** A fresh personal install starts private web search (SearXNG, no API key) and the connector runner, and registers three pinned starter connectors: `fetch` and `time` (on) and `sequential-thinking` (off). Opt out with `--no-search` / `--no-tools` or `VEGADUTA_SEARCH=0` / `VEGADUTA_TOOLS=0`; the choice is remembered, and an existing install keeps what it had. The runner joins `personal.yml` on its own network with no published port and no mounted folder, at 768 MB and 8 servers at a time. A starter you delete or edit is never put back (V659). Never run on a real Docker stack.
- **Add-ons.** A new **Add-ons** page (`/add-ons`, personal install) lists the optional extras (build terminal, web search, document reading, the Assistant's browser, free local model packs) with disk size, memory, licence and whether your computer suits them, and turns them on or off after a confirmation. Model packs are downloaded for you with live progress; the others show "Waiting" until your computer has started them and the exact command to run. Three more are listed as "Coming soon". New `/api/addons` (migration V656) and an installer-only `/api/addons/host/**` behind `app.addons.host-token`, which nothing sets yet.
- **Setup app: an Extras step.** The wizard lists the same add-ons with disk, memory and licence, ticks the recommended ones (build terminal and web search), totals what you picked against this computer's free disk, Docker memory and RAM, and warns when it is short. Running Setup again shows what is installed and adds or removes extras; switching one off only stops it and never deletes chats, documents, projects, sign-ins or downloaded models. Headless: `--extras id,id`, `--extras none`, `--list-extras`; `--documents` and `--browser` still work.
- **Workflows from chat.** New `create_workflow` tool: the Assistant turns a repeatable or multi-step request ("every Monday summarise sales and email me", "check each new form and route it") into a draft workflow using the console's graph generator, saves it through the normal workflow create path (tenant limit, owner and version history as usual) and replies with the nodes, any warnings and a `/workflows/<id>` link. It never activates, schedules or runs the draft. Offered to agents that list it in their tool allow-list; `app.tools.create-workflow.enabled` (default `true`) switches it off. Assistant prompt V6 teaches when to use it.
- **Parallel sub-tasks from chat.** New `run_subtasks` tool: the Assistant (or any agent that may delegate, with or without collaborators) fans up to 8 independent, self-contained briefs out to parallel throwaway copies of itself and merges the answers. Workers keep the agent's tools minus delegation, scheduling, human requests, `coding_task` and terminal writes, and share the run's budget and the 240 s / 4000-character limits of `delegate_parallel`. `app.tools.run-subtasks.enabled` (default `true`) switches it off; Assistant prompt V5 teaches when to use it.
- **Personal: Google connect works out of the box or says why not.** `GOOGLE_OAUTH_REDIRECT_URI` defaults from `PUBLIC_API_URL`; release tarballs include `deploy/compose/google-oauth.env` when the `GOOGLE_OAUTH_CLIENT_ID`/`_SECRET` repo secrets exist; personal installs use a Google Desktop-app client with PKCE and any loopback port (`app.google.oauth.mode=loopback`; other shapes stay on the Web client); the Google panel shows setup steps instead of a failing button (`GET /api/channels/google/oauth-config`).
- **Assistant knows how to set the platform up (every install).** New read-only `vegaduta_setup_guide` tool: matches a request ("connect Jira", "my agent has no tool for this") to the console page that sets it up, reports this install's live state (MCP servers, channels, Google) and what else must be on (an MCP server also needs its tools enabled for the agent), and searches the bundled platform docs. `vegaduta_api_guide` (OpenAPI spec) is now offered on shared servers too. Assistant replies can link console pages (`[Tools](/tools)`). `APP_ASSISTANT_PLATFORM_GUIDE_ENABLED`; docs bundle refreshed with `scripts/ci/sync-platform-guide-docs.sh`.
- **Per-tenant prompt directives (API).** `/api/prompt-directives` (tenant admin) switches the built-in prompt directives off or rewords them and appends a free-text block for all agents; security directives need `acknowledgeRisk`. Stored per tenant (V654), cached 30 s, audited; no settings = the unchanged built-in prompt.
- **Instructions page.** Console `/admin/instructions` shows every instruction the platform gives the model: switch each built-in directive off, reword it or restore the default (safety-critical ones ask you to confirm the risk), add instructions for all agents, and preview the assembled text. Layering: built-in < these settings < the agent's own System prompt.
- Console: failed calls and warnings no longer pop up as banners; they appear in the notification bell instead.

- **Browser add-on for the personal install.** `application-personal.yml` turns on browser v2, encrypted login profiles and sign-in takeover (TOTP, domain policy and confirmation gates unchanged); the tools appear once Chromium is present (`CORE_IMAGE_VARIANT=-full`, `CORE_MEM_LIMIT=2560m`, `BROWSER_ALLOWED_DOMAINS`). `GET /api/browser/automation/capabilities` now returns `browserUnavailableReason` when `browserAvailable` is false. See docs/personal-mode.md.
- **Browser in the Assistant.** A Browser button opens the agent's own browser beside the chat: sign in to a site yourself in a live view, hand control back, and the agent carries on logged in (with the saved login in use shown). It opens by itself only when the agent asks for help (captcha, 2FA, login); where the install lacks the browser it says so instead of hiding.
- **Preferences from behavior.** When you ask for the same style three times in 30 days ("shorter", "no emojis", "reply in Hindi", in chat or in a thumbs comment) or keep deciding one kind of approval the same way, the assistant files a preference proposal in the Learned section; nothing is used until you approve it, approval habits never change an approval rule, and no model is called (`app.learning.preferences.enabled`, on in `personal`; V623).
- **Routines from habits.** A request you repeat on a rhythm (the same thing on at least 3 distinct days at about the same time: daily, every weekday or weekly) becomes a proposal in the Learned section; approving it creates the scheduled task. Nothing is scheduled before you approve, it runs off the chat turn and makes no model call, and your scrubbed requests are kept 45 days. Flag `app.learning.routines.enabled` (on in `personal`; set `APP_LEARNING_ROUTINES_TIMEZONE`); V633.
- **Daily memory review.** Once a day per person, off the chat path and without a model call, the assistant compares your remembered facts and proposes tidy-ups in the Learned section: merge near-duplicates, keep the newer of two contradicting notes, forget notes unused for 60 days. Approving performs it through the Memory services (V643 `learning_review_runs`, flag `app.learning.review.enabled`, on in the personal profile). The top of the Learned section shows what was learned this week (`GET /api/learning/review/summary`).
- Learned skill drafts are self-checked before you see them: with `app.skills.learning.self-check.enabled` (on in the personal profile) the assistant replays each draft against the turn it came from, drops the ones that would have made the answer worse, and marks the rest "checked: passed" or "checked: weak" in the Learned section and the skill library.
- Approvals inbox redesigned on the shared console components; console features the personal installation cannot run now say so and point to the Lite/Full or Helm install; one type scale and font pair across the console; files the assistant writes are now readable and writable by the shell sandbox, so a dev server it starts can serve them.

- A personal installation now says so when a page needs something it does not run. Deep links to single sign-on, team sharing, billing and payments, the public directory, Studio, branding, leads and MCP access (and Code Intel on any install without that service) show one "Not available in the personal installation" state with the reason and a pointer to the Lite or Full edition (Docker Compose or the Helm chart). One capability map (`web/app/lib/editionCapabilities.ts`) and one component (`FeatureUnavailable`) drive it.
- **Learning proposals.** A generic store (`learning_proposals`, V612; `GET /api/learning/proposals`, `POST /api/learning/proposals/{id}/approve` and `/reject`) where the assistant files what it wants to learn (lessons, preferences; routines and consolidation to follow) and nothing applies until you approve it. Approving a lesson stores an agent-private memory fact, approving a preference adds a bullet to your `user_profile` block; text is scrubbed of secrets and personal data, capped (`app.learning.proposals.max-pending`) and expires. On in the personal profile (`app.learning.proposals.enabled`), off elsewhere. The Learned section of the work rail lists proposals by kind with Approve, Edit and Reject.
- **Lessons from mistakes and corrections.** With `app.learning.lessons.enabled=true` (needs `app.learning.proposals.enabled`; on in the personal profile) a finished turn is checked, off the request thread, for a tool call that failed and was retried successfully with different arguments, or a message that corrects the previous answer. A heuristic pre-filter decides first, so most turns cost no model call; then one small call on the agent's own model writes one imperative sentence from the scrubbed evidence and files it as a pending lesson proposal. Approving it stores an agent-private memory fact. Per-agent cooldown and daily cap apply.
- The Assistant's work rail has a **Learned** section: skills the assistant drafted from finished tasks (approve or reject; nothing is used before approval), the ones already approved, and the facts it remembered about you (edit or forget), refreshed after every turn.
- A turn whose reply stops at an announced step ("Let me create the pom.xml:") without doing it now carries on automatically, up to `app.chat.continuation.max-passes` extra passes, so long tasks finish instead of stalling; the personal profile's per-turn tool cap rises from 40 to 150.

- **Python Agent SDK reaches feature parity with the Java SDK's experimental surface.** `vegaduta_agent` gains: a kill switch (`HaltSwitch`/`HaltGate`/`halt_drill`), tiered autonomy L1–L4 (`AutonomyGate`/`AutonomyRegistry`), delegation mandates that can only narrow with digest-chained verification (`Mandate`, `MandateGate`, `verify_mandate_chain`), a tamper-evident audit trail (`HashChainedAuditSink` — byte-compatible canonical form with the Java chain — plus `HmacSha256` signing, `verify_audit_chain` and `evidence_bundle` JSON/Markdown export), durable SQLite stores (`SqliteApprovalStore`, `SqliteAuditSink` alongside `SqliteEffectJournal`), tool supply-chain pinning (`ProvenanceGate` on `Governance(provenance=...)`), governed memory with a poison probe (`GovernedMemoryStore`), cost attribution and showback (`CostLedger`, `CostObserver`, `showback`), policy replay and shadow gating (`policy_replay`, `ShadowGate`, `TraceRecorder`, `replay_trace`), four-eyes approvals and signed decision receipts (`FourEyesAuthorizer`, `allowed_deciders`, `sign_receipt`/`verify_receipt`), async tools (`wrap_async` / `tool.acall`), a dependency-free OpenAI-style adapter (`adapters.openai`), a jar-less risk-map scanner (`risk_map`, same deduction model as `agent-scanner`), Slack/Teams approval payloads (`slack_message`/`teams_message`), interrupted-effect listing (`interrupted_keys`), and `WebhookHook` (transport injected — the SDK still imports no HTTP client). All in `sdk/python-agent`; 65 tests pass, ruff clean.

### Changed

- **The Assistant can answer API questions from the real spec and show live results.** On a personal install the new read-only tool `vegaduta_api_guide` searches and describes this install's OpenAPI document (served at `GET /api/openapi.json`, generated from the running code), and the answer can end with a `json_ui` `apiCall` block: the console calls the endpoint with the signed-in user's own session and shows the result as a table, key-value list or raw JSON, picking the shape from the response. A GET runs by itself; POST, PUT, PATCH and DELETE wait for the user to press **Send**. Paths are limited to same-origin `/api/...`. Off with `app.assistant.api-guide.enabled=false`.
- **The Assistant page works like an IDE welcome tab.** The empty state is now a launchpad with three guided journeys (fine-tune your agent, create a workflow, build an app), each with a tickable checklist, an **Ask the Assistant** button that prefills a starting prompt, and a link to the page where the work happens. `⌘K` / `Ctrl+K` opens a command palette (new thread, send mode, tune, workflows, app builder, starter prompts), and a status bar under the box shows who is answering, the send mode, the attached folder and running tasks.
- **Personal installs call their model what it is.** On a local-auth install the console says **Local model (Ollama)** wherever it used to say "Platform LLM", "platform pool" or "Platform-hosted (shared pool)" (providers page, AI models page, agent workbench badges and notes, routing and intelligence-source controls, the admin overview tile and the powered-by chip), and the "Use platform-hosted LLM" toggle is not shown: the pool row on such an install is the Ollama on this machine, so there is nothing hosted or shared to opt into. One helper, `poolLabel()` in `web/app/lib/personalInstall.ts`, carries the rule.
- **The local-model wizard uses the pool row, not a per-agent key.** Finishing **Set up a local model** now points the install's local pool row at the chosen model (`POST /api/local-models/use`, the switch `/troubleshoot` and the CLI already use) and creates the first agent on the pool like every other agent, instead of creating a per-agent `OLLAMA` provider credential, which made the same model read as "platform" on one screen and "your own provider" on another.
- **The whole model catalog, with details.** `GET /api/local-models/recommendation` now returns every catalog row (`catalog`, each flagged `fits` against the new `budgetGb`), not only the ones that fit, and every row carries parameters, context window, VRAM, licence, thinking and best-for from the extended `catalog.txt` (old columns unchanged in place). The wizard shows two groups, **Fits your machine** and **Needs more memory (from N GB, you have M GB)**, every row selectable, with a detail card for the selected model (size, download, memory needed against detected, GPU memory, tool-calling grade, thinking, licence, speed expectation, best for); a model that does not fit can still be downloaded after an explicit acknowledgement. The catalog gains `qwen3.5:35b`, `qwen3:14b`, `llama3.1:8b`, `phi4-mini`, `phi4:14b`, `ministral-3:8b`, `mistral-small3.2`, `gemma4:e2b`, `gemma4:e4b`, `gemma4:31b` and `deepseek-r1:32b`. Previewable without a backend at `/ui-kit/console-preview/local-model`.
- **One chat surface on a personal install: the Playground, Quick Task and Jobs pages fold into /assistant.** Personal installs only (local-auth); every other install keeps those three pages and does not list the Assistant in its rail. The box gains an agent picker (the Assistant by default, any agent of the workspace, or "Auto" for a quick task; `?agent=` deep links and the last-used agent are honoured, and opening a thread switches to the agent it was had with) and a mode switch: **Chat** (one turn), **In background** (hands the request to an agent job at once; the old Jobs launcher) and **Quick task** (previews the agent and tools, confirms, runs, and can deliver the result to WhatsApp, SMS, a call or just the thread; the old /quick-task page). Quick tasks and their tool calls render as a card in the conversation, and the Work rail lists routines with their next run beside the running and finished jobs. On a personal install `/playground`, `/quick-task` and `/jobs` redirect to `/assistant` with the agent, mode and `?prompt=` carried over; the per-job page, `/quick-task/history`, the tenant-wide ledger (now `/jobs/all`) and agent job schedules (now `/jobs/schedules`) stay. The three rail rows are hidden on a personal install only; the workbench lab keeps its own playground for an agent under construction.
- **Setup app: the document sidecar is opt-in for personal.** The default install no longer pulls the ~2 GB ML sidecar; a checkbox (or `--documents`) adds PDF and scan reading. Chat, the Assistant, agents and text uploads work without it.
- **Personal install: fewer irrelevant screens, one-click finish.** The nav rail, command palette and /learn drop Leads and Plans on a local-auth install, and the Setup app opens the first-run link itself when the stack is up.

### Fixed

- **Setup wizard: a failed install can be retried and shows live progress.** Clicking Install after a failure did nothing visible - the run guard allowed only one install per process, and the event stream replayed the old error, which the page treated as terminal. Runs are now restartable once finished or failed, the event history resets for a new run, a `begin` event clears stale UI in every open tab, and the page shows a step strip (Download → Install files → Configure → Start containers → Finish), a spinner with elapsed time, a real download percentage (with an indeterminate bar when the server sent no length), a "Try again"/"Back" choice on failure, and an "already running" state instead of silence. Ticked extras are highlighted and the install button counts them.
- **Security: the Setup wizard no longer accepts commands from a web page.** The installer's loopback API (`/api/install`, `/api/docker/*`) previously took any POST - including a `text/plain` fetch a malicious site can send without a CORS preflight - while the wizard was open on a fixed port. It now mints a per-process token, puts it in the wizard URL, requires `Authorization: Bearer` on every `/api/*` call, and refuses requests whose `Host` header is not loopback.
- **Security: release extraction cannot write outside the install directory.** `unpackRelease` rejected `../` path members but not a tar symlink whose target escaped the dir (a `a -> /etc` member followed by `a/x`). Link targets are now confinement-checked and every write resolves through the symlinks already on disk against the resolved install root.
- **Security: the desktop-agent bearer check is timing-safe** (`crypto.timingSafeEqual`, like the sibling sidecars).
- **Security: every public-facing outbound HTTP call resolves through the SSRF guard.** A new `GuardedJdkHttpClient` drops in for `java.net.http.HttpClient` but connects only through `SsrfGuard`'s resolver, closing the DNS-rebinding window between the URL check and the connect on ~30 call sites (MCP OAuth and registry, A2A, Google/Meta/Slack/Teams/Discord/X/Telegram channel calls, skill registry, web search, x402, embeddings, image/video generation, the LLM blocking and streaming paths, GCP KMS, Twilio). The 14 deliberately-raw remainders target operator-configured private hosts (the sidecar, SearXNG, Langfuse, internal probes) the guard is meant to refuse.
- **A model can no longer repeat the same lookup inside one reply.** An identical `web_search`, `fetch_web_page`, knowledge or guide call (same arguments up to key order) later in the same reply is answered with "use the earlier result" instead of running; repeating again after that ends the loop with a short fixed reply. Only idempotent retrieval tools are guarded by default (`app.chat.tools.repeat-guard.tools`); the 4B local model used to re-issue the same search before answering.
- **Streaming chat replays a request once when Ollama rejects the model's tool call as malformed XML** (`HTTP 500 ... XML syntax error ... element <function> closed by </parameter>`). The blocking path already retried 5xx; the streaming path did not. Partial: in the reproduced multi-step browsing case the replay failed the same way, so the cause (what the model writes, or what Ollama does with it) is still open.
- **Personal: embeddings follow the Ollama you registered.** `app.embeddings.provider=auto` decided at start-up from environment properties only, so an Ollama chosen in the console's local-model wizard (with the local-AI bootstrap off) was invisible: embeddings fell to the sidecar, collections already embedded by Ollama silently lost vector search, and without the `rag` profile embeddings failed. `auto` now also uses an Ollama registered at run time (a platform-pool row pointing at the managed model server, checked per call and cached 30 s); a cloud key and an Ollama named in the environment still win, and the sidecar is the fallback.
- **The agent's browser could not start in the container at all.** Playwright finds its bundled driver with the thread's context class loader, which on the browser's worker thread cannot see the driver jar nested in the Spring Boot fat jar (`Failed to create driver`); core now hands it its own loader. The personal stack also mounts `/tmp` `noexec`, so the add-on overlay points Playwright at the image's Node (`PLAYWRIGHT_NODEJS_PATH`). Start-up errors now carry the root cause and a log line instead of a bare "Failed to create driver".
- **Personal: a browsing request reaches the whole browser toolset.** The personal profile raises the per-turn tool budget (24 tools, 6000 schema tokens; the always-kept tools used most of the shared 12/3000), the selector keeps the built-in `browser_*` tools together once one is picked, and small-model mode keeps up to four of them (navigate, read, click, type) for a request about driving a page.
- **Personal: web, channel and browser tools reach the model.** The relevance selector no longer leaves a request that names a tool with no scored slots when the always-kept set already fills the cap (about 15 names against 12): a matching tool now gets a small reserved floor. Small-model mode also keeps the best tool for each intent a request voices (search the web and list files), and knows routines (`schedule_task`) and machine questions (`local_shell`) without a shared word.
- **Personal: a free local model is no longer billed.** The call log re-priced every call from the shared catalog, so a zero-cost local pool row was booked at the unknown-model default rate into `llm_call_log`, the monthly spend counter and the hosted-spend cap; it now stores the advisor's own estimate.
- **Guardrails: a stalled thread no longer blocks harmless tool output.** A linear (RE2/J) rule that missed its 250 ms wall-clock deadline (a laptop under load, a paused VM) is retried once with a fresh deadline before failing closed; the step cap is unchanged.
- **Personal: continuation passes and background calls keep the tenant.** The tool-result continuation in the fast stream turn runs inside the captured tenant scope, so `llm_call_log` and `rl_episode` rows are written instead of failing with a missing-schema error.
- **Personal: WhatsApp QR says what is wrong.** With no bridge configured the receiver stops instead of polling forever and the channel shows "WhatsApp bridge not configured"; the overlay passes `APP_WHATSAPPQR_BRIDGE_URL` and `APP_WHATSAPPQR_BRIDGE_SECRET` to core.
- **Personal: share pages and A2A discovery reach core inside Docker.** The share-page layouts and the `/.well-known/agent-card.json` proxy fell back to `http://localhost:8080`, which inside the web container is the web container itself; both now prefer `CORE_INTERNAL_URL` (compose already sets it to `http://core:8080`).
- **Setup preflight no longer fails on ports the running install owns.** Ports published by this stack's own containers are reported as in use *by VegaDūta* instead of blocking a re-run, matching what `doctor` already does.
- **Setup: the wizard finds a Docker CLI it did not put on PATH.** A Finder-launched app gets a minimal PATH and Docker Desktop links its CLI under `~/.docker/bin` first (the `/usr/local/bin` symlink appears only after the app's privileged first run), so a fresh install with a running engine still reported "docker command not found". The wizard now appends `~/.docker/bin` to its PATH for itself and every child process.
- **Setup: a failed `brew install docker-desktop` says how to recover.** Stale root-owned files from an older Docker install make brew stop on a sudo prompt it cannot answer; the wizard now prints the cleanup command and the manual install link instead of a bare failure.
- **Personal: host RAM and accelerator are persisted.** `HOST_RAM_GB`/`HOST_ACCELERATOR` were only exported for the `personal` run that computed them; a restart or doctor from another shell silently reported `0`. Both are now written to `.env` (and documented in `.env.example`).
- **Personal: the /connect page is honest about webhook channels.** Slack and SMS cards carry a "Needs a public address" badge (they cannot receive on localhost), and the WhatsApp card goes to the QR-pairing flow that works on a laptop instead of the Meta API page.
- **Personal: changing `WEB_HOST_PORT` or `CORE_HOST_PORT` works.** `scripts/vegaduta personal` keeps `PUBLIC_APP_URL` and `PUBLIC_API_URL` in step with the ports when they are the generated `http://localhost:<port>` form (a `--domain` install is untouched), and the compose defaults derive from the port variables.
- **Personal console.** The Agents page shows real labels instead of raw message keys, `/research` is hidden from the rail unless research runs are on, workflow nodes the install cannot run (`wait`, `voice_menu`, `bulk_email`, `crm_lead`, `device_control`) are no longer offered in the palette, and a refused refresh no longer clears the session cookie (two tabs refreshing together signed the person out).
- **Personal: smaller papercuts.** Valkey keeps 128 MB (a rate-limit counter evicted under pressure fails open), the localhost public-URL notice is INFO on a personal install, the shell tool tells the model it is on a Mac (BSD flags), `schedule_task` says to omit the delivery fields unless named, and the Docker Desktop cask is `docker-desktop`.
- **Nothing runs after an action is parked for approval.** When a tool call was held for the user's approval, the model was told so and could keep going with other tools (often ones that depended on the held one) and finish the task before the user decided. The turn is now paused at the first held call: every other tool call in that turn is refused, and the model is told to stop and say what is waiting. The Assistant's prompt and the "carry on" context say the same. Separately, `keyValue` nodes in a `json_ui` block were being rejected as unsupported (the node type was lower-cased before the lookup).
- **Local thinking models answer in seconds, not 30-plus.** The install's own Ollama now gets `reasoning_effort: none` (`app.llm.ollama.reasoning-effort`, default `none`; `low`/`medium`/`high` or blank to opt back in). Measured on `qwen3.5:4b`: a one-line question spent 40+ s and 600 tokens thinking before it answered, against about 5 s with reasoning off.
- **The Assistant can read the knowledge base, and its agent picker changes agent.** An Assistant whose knowledge base was left off (the switch at creation is best-effort and can fail quietly) said it could not read the person's documents; it is now switched back on when the Assistant loads, and the small-model tool choice offers `search_knowledge_base` for requests about uploaded documents. The picker could never leave the first agent: the `?agent=` link seeded the choice but also outvoted every later pick, and each pick was written back to that same URL. The link now only seeds or re-seeds the selection.
- **Attaching a file or photo in the Assistant works on a personal install again.** The projects folder is shared by core (uid 999, which saves chat uploads) and the workspace runner (uid 1000); when the runner created the volume first, core was refused with `AccessDeniedException: /workspace/Chat uploads`. A one-shot `workspace-perms` service in `personal.yml` now opens the named volume before core starts (a host folder chosen with `VEGADUTA_PROJECTS_DIR` keeps its own permissions).
- **A small local model is offered the tool that fits the request.** Small-model mode keeps six tools, chosen by word overlap, which missed most real requests ("what is the weather in Goa" shares no word with `web_search`), so the model was offered unrelated tools and then asked for one it did not have. An intent table now matches web-search wording, URLs, mail/calendar/Drive words, file and project words, memory words and document words to the tools that serve them.
- **A slow careful turn no longer ends as "every model failed".** A CAREFUL/RIGOROUS/PLANNED turn that hits `app.llm.http.buffered-turn-timeout` is now answered at FAST depth without tools (metric `chat.degraded.depth_timeout`, a `guardrail` step event in the trace) instead of discarding four minutes of work and reporting the pool exhausted. A hosted-pool row whose stored parameter count disagrees with its model tag by more than half now trusts the tag, so a row repointed from a 9B to a 4B model enters small-model mode (FAST, six tools, compact directives) instead of keeping the larger model's settings. The local-model warm-up compares the context Ollama actually loaded with `app.llm.ollama.num-ctx` and logs the `OLLAMA_CONTEXT_LENGTH` fix when the runner ignored the request (the MLX runner does).
- **Thinking models on the install's own Ollama now stream their reasoning.** The platform-pool row the local-LLM bootstrap (or `/use`) creates is served by the hosted-pool client, which did not apply the Ollama reasoning-field alias or the `num_ctx` override that a tenant OLLAMA provider row gets; so `thinking` step events never appeared on a personal install, and long tool turns could hit the 2k default context. Rows pointing at `app.local-models.ollama-base-url` (or marked OLLAMA) now get both.
- **Installers: personal install works from `curl | sh` on Debian/Ubuntu and from the Windows Setup app.** `install.sh personal` used a bash-only substitution that fails under dash; `vegaduta.ps1 personal` now understands the `workspace` and `rag` profiles and prints the first-run link, and the wizard passes PowerShell-style flags (`-Profile`, `-Lite`/`-Full`) on Windows instead of letting `--profile` leak into `docker compose`. The `workspace-runner` image is now built, signed and tagged by the release workflow, so a released personal install can pull it.

### Added

- **A bigger context window from the console, no Ollama restart.** "Speed and behaviour" gains a Context window choice (server default, 8,192, 16,384, 32,768 tokens). On Apple Silicon the MLX runner ignores the context core sends and loads 4,096, which cuts off long conversations and tool results; the choice makes a variant of the model with `PARAMETER num_ctx` through Ollama's `/api/create` (the weights are reused, nothing is downloaded), points the local model at it and re-measures it. `PUT /api/local-models/settings` takes `contextLength`.
- **Local model controls in the console.** Providers → Local model has a "Speed and behaviour" card: whether the model thinks before answering (Off, Low, Medium, High, Model default), how long it stays loaded (5 minutes, 30 minutes, 2 hours, always), and the context window it actually runs with, with the one-line fix when the runner loaded fewer tokens than the Assistant asks for. Saved on the local model's row (`GET`/`PUT /api/local-models/settings`, migration V611) and applied from the next message; `app.llm.ollama.reasoning-effort` and `app.local-models.keep-alive` are now only the defaults for a row that has not chosen.
- **Voice and camera calls work from a console on this machine.** The content-security policy allowed `wss:` but not the plain `ws://localhost:7880` of a local LiveKit, so the browser blocked the call and showed "The voice service could not be reached". When a first-party service is on a loopback host, `ws://` to that host is now allowed; hosted installs are unchanged.
- **A chat turn now says what it is doing before the first token.** Step-event turns (the Assistant, the Playground, the workbench lab) receive `phase` events - `guardrails`, `routing`, `context`, `model` - from the turn pipeline, and core returns the SSE stream the moment the request is accepted instead of after preflight, client selection and prompt assembly have all finished on the request thread (step-event turns only; voice and channel turns are byte-identical). The console renders them through one shared `TurnProgress` line ("Checking safety… · Gathering context… · Model is working… 42 s", then the tool in flight or the freshest line of the model's reasoning) in place of the bare "Processing…" / "…" that made a slow local model look hung. The Assistant shows reasoning by default (the header toggle still hides it).
- **Local models are loaded ahead of the first chat.** `POST /api/local-models/warm` (tenant admin; empty body = the pool row's model) runs Ollama's warm-up call with `app.local-models.keep-alive` (default `30m`); core calls it after a pull finishes, when the model is switched with `/use`, and after the local-LLM bootstrap, and the chat surfaces call it once when they open, so a laptop whose Ollama unloaded the model during a pause has it back before the user finishes typing.
- **Switch the local model at runtime from /troubleshoot.** The catalog card now marks which tag agents actually serve and offers **Use** on every installed model: `POST /api/local-models/use` repoints the platform-pool row that serves the configured model server (re-measuring its tool-call grade like an admin edit), or creates the row bootstrap-shaped when none exists — which is also how a personal install that started without Ollama gets its provider entry afterwards, no restart or reinstall needed. The recommendation response gains `servingModel` for the "in use" marker; the local-model setup wizard shows it too and links to the switch.
- **Personal installs drop the surfaces a single user never reaches.** On a local-auth install the rail, command palette and /learn screen index no longer advertise Shared with me, the public agent Directory, Studio, Cost & ROI, or the admin consoles for agent trust, commerce, payments, branding, MCP access, API keys and SSO (one list in `web/app/lib/personalInstall.ts`; the pages still answer on a deep link). The confusing "Edge AI" rail label is now "Browser models" — which is what /edge actually hosts.
- **Personal installs ask the model question too.** A first interactive `scripts/vegaduta personal` now offers the same choice as `up`: a recommended open-weights model on a detected host Ollama or in a new `ollama` Docker container, a cloud provider key, or any tag from the catalog. Picking "cloud provider" is remembered (a later run no longer adopts a freshly installed Ollama on its own). The container's `OLLAMA_MEM_LIMIT` now follows the chosen model's catalog RAM requirement (the 8 GB default could not hold `gpt-oss:20b`), and `doctor` warns against the model's real need. The local catalog gains `gpt-oss:120b`, `deepseek-r1:8b` and `deepseek-r1:14b`; the Ollama provider dropdown seeds the same tags the installer offers (gpt-oss, DeepSeek R1, qwen3.5, granite4.2), and the platform-admin Groq preset lists the current Groq lineup including `openai/gpt-oss-20b`/`120b`.
- **No dollar figures on self-hosted installs.** Every LLM-cost display now gates on `NEXT_PUBLIC_BILLING_ENABLED` (unset ⇒ billing off ⇒ no meaningful price): the Assistant reply metrics line, the playground token tooltip, the Run-cost card (becomes "Run usage" in tokens), the LLM-spend section's cost tiles/columns/share bars, the lab graph's cost metric, the quick-task call log's `~$` suffix and the research run's cost field. Tokens, call counts, latency and provider/model still show everywhere.
- **Assistant header "About this assistant" popover.** An `i` button explains that the Assistant is a shapeable agent — link to create agents, tune this one, add tools — and points at the Academy (free) via `NEXT_PUBLIC_ACADEMY_URL` (compose defaults it to `academy.vegaduta.ai`).
- **Assistant replies get the playground's X-ray row.** After a reply settles, the turn pulls its slice of the session's model-call records — the same `llm-requests` snapshots and `llm-usage` ledger the playground reads — so every reply can now open **Request X-ray** (the exact prompt/system blocks/options/tool schema sent to the model, with the token-composition bars), a compact metrics line (latency · tokens · cost · provider/model), and a ChatGPT-style action row: **Copy**, **Listen**, **Regenerate** (last reply), and thumbs feedback via the shared `TurnFeedback`.
- **Assistant: reply voice + a reasoning-visibility toggle.** Two header controls (persisted per browser): a speaker toggle that reads each finished reply aloud — on-device Kokoro when installed, the browser's built-in voice otherwise, plus a per-reply "Listen" button — and a brain toggle that opts into the model's raw reasoning (the live thinking line and `thinking` rows in the work trace are now hidden by default; "Analyzing…" status and tool-call progress still show).
- **Fix: on-device voice (Kokoro), WebLLM tier-1, and the App Builder's Babel runtime all 404'd in production.** `proxy.js`'s scanner blocklist (`SCANNER_PATH`) matches `^/vendor/` as a PHP-Composer probe and returned a hard 404 for every vendored runtime (`/vendor/kokoro.web.js`, `/vendor/web-llm.js`, `/vendor/babel.min.js`) — so the Kokoro worker's dynamic `import()` failed before a byte of the model downloaded, surfacing only "Download failed". The vendored-assets directory is renamed to `/vendored/` everywhere it is referenced (worker URLs, `webllm-sw.js`, SandboxPreview, `next.config.js` cache headers). Additionally, the transformers.js bundled inside `kokoro-js` loads its ONNX Runtime JSEP loader from `cdn.jsdelivr.net` at runtime — connect-src only under the page CSP, never script-src — so `scripts/vendor-kokoro.mjs` now vendors `ort-wasm-simd-threaded.jsep.{mjs,wasm}` and `ort.bundle.min.mjs` same-origin and `kokoroWorker.ts` pins `env.wasmPaths` to them.
- **Desktop / computer-use tools on personal installs**: `services/desktop-agent` — a loopback-only, bearer-authed sidecar the person runs on their laptop (macOS first: `screencapture`, JXA/CoreGraphics clicks, System Events typing and the accessibility element tree; Linux via xdotool/xclip/notify-send when present). New `local_desktop_*` tools: `local_desktop_screen`, `local_desktop_elements`, `local_desktop_press`, `local_desktop_click`, `local_desktop_type`, `local_desktop_key`, `local_desktop_open`, `local_desktop_activate`, `local_desktop_notify`, `local_desktop_clipboard` — offered only on single-user installs while `APP_ASSISTANT_DESKTOP_AGENT_URL` is set; actuating tools are approval-pinned by default (`APP_ASSISTANT_DESKTOP_APPROVAL_REQUIRED`, `false` on `personal`).
- **Scheduled tasks on personal installs**: `SCHEDULED_TASKS_ENABLED=true` on the `personal` profile — the `schedule_task` heartbeat loop (recurring unattended agent runs with in-app/email/Slack/Telegram delivery) now works out of the box, with `whatsapp_qr` added as a delivery channel through the linked WhatsApp QR session.
- **Assistant terminal without per-command approval on personal installs**: `app.assistant.local-shell.approval-required` (`APP_ASSISTANT_LOCAL_SHELL_APPROVAL_REQUIRED`) lifts `local_shell`'s always-confirm pin so the sandboxed terminal runs without parking every command — the `personal` profile sets it `false` by default (a `SUPERVISED`-tier agent still asks before every tool call, commands stay sandboxed and audited). Restore the confirmation card per command with `=true`.
- **WhatsApp QR channel**: link your own WhatsApp number as a companion device (WhatsApp Web-style QR scan) instead of only the official Meta Cloud API/Twilio business paths — the WhatsApp option that works on a personal install, because the included `services/whatsapp-qr` bridge (Node + Baileys) dials out, so no public webhook or tunnel is needed. New Channels → WhatsApp QR card shows the live pairing QR, the linked number and unlink; inbound runs the same pairing/approval pipeline as every channel (groups are not supported in this version) and sends can fall through to it as a third `WhatsAppProvider` (after Meta and Twilio). Compose overlay `deploy/compose/whatsapp-qr.yml` (profile `whatsapp-qr`, one `WHATSAPP_QR_BRIDGE_SECRET`) plus `docs/channels/whatsapp-qr.md`.
- Voice calls get the playground's audio controls: the call panel (and the assistant's call sheet) and the public shared-agent page now keep every completed exchange's text on screen — including live-call turns, which the voice-agent worker now publishes over the room's data channel — with a per-reply play/stop button, a spoken-replies speaker toggle, and a "reply voice" picker (on-device or browser voice). Callers on a paid plan can also change the agent's voice provider in place and pick one of the tenant's cloned voices as the live call's own voice, carried to the worker on the room token's metadata (`ttsVoice`/`ttsVoiceProvider` on `VoiceTokenRequest`).
- Live calls answer in voice even with no server-side TTS configured: when the worker has no ElevenLabs/Sarvam key it skips the TTS node entirely (audio output disabled rather than a plugin that fails each turn) and marks every agent transcript line `voiced:false`; both call panels then speak those replies through the caller's own on-device/browser voice chain — "response in voice" on a keyless install, for free.
- `/assistant`: background tasks are now visible and survive a reload. The Work panel lists the Assistant's recent jobs with their state, progress, **Stop**, **Show in chat** and a details link; the hand-off card returns when its conversation is reopened (a conversation that began in the background is listed on the left); a task that finishes in the open conversation posts its result there; a badge on the Work button counts live tasks on narrow screens. Conversations get search, rename and a delete confirmation.
- `/assistant`: a "+" menu in the box with **Upload files**, **Take a photo** (in-browser camera with a live "camera on" indicator) and **Start a call** (the console's voice call in a sheet, bound to the same conversation).
