Files can also be dropped on the page or pasted. On an install with a projects folder every attached file is saved there (default folder `Chat uploads`, 25 MB each, a PDF also gets its text saved as `<name>.pdf.txt`) and the Assistant finds things in it with the new `workspace_search` tool (file names and lines, every word must match) and `workspace_read`. New endpoint `POST /api/assistant/workspace/files`.

### Fixed

- The model's own reasoning now shows in a turn: Ollama's `reasoning` field is bridged to `reasoning_content`, a `ReasoningEmitAdvisor` streams it as `thinking` step events, and the worked-for block renders it live. New Assistants are created at PLANNED depth so the turn's plan surfaces too.
- Core images are ~90 MB lighter: the build strips Playwright's macOS/Windows driver binaries (a Linux container can only run the Linux halves).
- Calls can now share your screen: a monitor button in the connected-call controls starts edge sight on `getDisplayMedia` - the on-device VLM describes the screen to the agent and no frame ever leaves the browser.
- `generate_document` can embed web images: a section's optional `imageUrl` (public http(s), png/jpeg) is fetched server-side through the SSRF guard and embedded on that section's slide/page, so "add this photo to the deck" works without the model ever handling bytes.
- The Assistant's tool sync now picks up search-family tools (`web_search`, `deep_research`, `web_task`, `verify_fact`) when they appear — previously only connection-prefixed tools were added after agent creation.
- `generate_document` is now an always-relevant tool: document skills (pptx/docx/xlsx) route the model through `load_skill`, and the message's vocabulary ("ppt", "deck") never matches the tool name — so the relevance selector trimmed it and the model correctly reported no document tool. The tool now survives selection like the skill tools do. Set `TOOL_AUTO_SELECTION_ENABLED=false` to skip trimming entirely on small tool catalogs.
- `--local-ai=host` now actually works: `docker-compose.local-ai.host.yml` repoints the pool bootstrap URL and the SSRF allow-list at `host.docker.internal:11434`. Previously the provider registered `http://ollama:11434` (a compose service that does not exist in host mode), so the pool was excluded and every agent turn reported no LLM configured.
- Browser install wizard (`installer/`): honors `WEB_HOST_PORT`/`CORE_HOST_PORT` overrides in preflight, repairs the `PATH` a Finder-launched app gets (docker/brew were invisible), `--release-url` supports bundled `file://` payloads, and preflight failures surface instead of spinning forever.

### Changed

- **The Assistant can build projects in a folder (personal mode, `--profile workspace`).** A new `workspace-runner` container (JDK 21, Maven, Node 22, git; only the projects folder mounted, no platform secret, no Docker socket) runs `local_shell` commands, so the terminal now works in the container stack too. New file tools `workspace_list`, `workspace_read`, `workspace_write` and `workspace_edit` are confined to the projects folder (`~/VegaDuta-Projects` by default) and do not ask; every `local_shell` command still does. `/assistant` gains **Attach a folder** (pick or create a project folder) and, while a folder is attached, carries on by itself after each approval. The personal profile allows 40 tool calls per turn and 30-minute commands. The Assistant's prompt is upgraded in place from every earlier shipped version (the terminal guidance had been added to the wrong one). The carry-on message carries only the status and exit code (never the tool output, which stays wrapped as untrusted data); a leading `cd` into the folder the command already runs in is dropped; the four file tools and the terminal are always offered when resolved (keyword relevance trimmed them on build requests). The personal profile also waits 240 s instead of 90 s for a local model's first token (the cause of "every model failed" on long turns). Run end to end on a personal stack with the default `qwen3.5:4b`: the Assistant wrote a Maven project, the approved `mvn -q package` built it in the runner and the approved `java -jar` printed Hello, World; the 4B model then kept asking to run the jar again instead of finishing, which is why the docs recommend a provider key for real projects.
- **Personal mode hides what does not apply.** Shared with me, SSO and (where no service is configured) Code Intel leave the rail and command palette; the App projects tab on `/build` is gone without an App Builder; the top-bar health popover and the system-status panels skip Kafka and the sidecar when the install does not run them; the "personal" environment pill, the plans region of the tour map, the agents-per-plan subtitle and the plan badge on Usage are gone with billing off; tour and guide copy no longer nudges a one-person install to invite teammates or upgrade; the Data & Infrastructure page lists only the systems this install runs; a signed-out visitor goes straight to sign-in instead of the public landing page.
- **Personal mode: invite by link, no token limits, no team nudges.** On a personal install (local auth, no mail server) "Invite people" now returns a one-time setup link in the console to copy and send, instead of only writing it to core's log; `POST /api/admin/users/{id}/invite-link` issues a fresh one for someone who has not set a password yet (refused once they have). The console warns when it is only reachable on this computer. `GET /api/tenant/usage-quota` gains `limitEnforced` (false when billing is off): the top-bar token meter, the 80%/100% banner and the usage page's limit bar are hidden then, and the usage page shows tokens used without a cap. The "invite a teammate" setup step, the seats tile and the welcome-modal invite form are hidden on a personal install, and the free-plan caps card shows only when billing is on. The "every hosted provider failed" chat error no longer says "our hosted AI service" or "contact support"; it says the model did not respond and that the install's server log names which one. Unit-tested and typechecked; not run on a stack.
- **Guardrails fail closed.** A tool-dispatch policy lookup that throws now refuses the tool call, an injection judge that errors or answers nothing withholds the tool output, and content moderation or content-guardrail errors refuse the turn (or withhold the answer), instead of letting the work through. Set `APP_GUARDRAILS_FAIL_MODE=open` for the previous behaviour. A guardrail that is simply not configured still passes. Unit-tested only; not run on a stack or under the 32-user benchmark.
- New `TOOL_UNCLASSIFIED_ACTION` (`allow` default, `require-approval`, `deny`): what a tool with no classification row gets.
- **Approvals can no longer be decided twice or replayed against a changed tool.** Approve and reject now re-read the request under a row lock and refuse if it is no longer pending, so two concurrent deciders cannot both run the approved action. An approval records a fingerprint of the tool's name, description and input schema, and the replay refuses to run (and asks for a fresh approval) if the tool behind that name has changed. Requests recorded before this have no fingerprint and replay as before. Unit-tested, and `ApprovalDecisionConcurrencyIT` runs the lock against real Postgres (it fails with the lock removed); not run on a stack or under the benchmark.
- **Security hardening sweep** (details in [docs/security-model.md](docs/security-model.md) and [docs/configuration.md](docs/configuration.md#personal-agent-settings)):
  - Realtime voice tickets are single use: a ticket is burned on the first accepted setup frame (new `SingleUseTicketRegistry`; per JVM). The Twilio relay ticket is not made single use.
  - The browser realtime-voice socket no longer allows every origin: `app.voice.realtime.allowed-origins` defaults to the console origins (`app.web.allowed-origins`) plus same-origin; `*` stays available as an explicit choice. The Twilio relay socket takes `app.voice.relay.allowed-origins` (empty = same-origin check only, which admits Twilio).
  - Hook webhooks (tenant-supplied URLs) are sent through the SSRF-pinned client, closing the DNS-rebinding window; an operator egress allow-list now also applies to them.
  - Two API-key leaks closed: the Gemini image client and the Gemini Live realtime provider no longer put the key in an error message that could be logged. `LogMasking.secrets` scrubs `?key=`/`token=` parameters, Bearer/Basic values and vendor key shapes.
  - Inbound channel turns can be rate limited per tenant, channel and sender (`app.omnichannel.rate-limit.*`, opt-in: off by default, on in the `personal` profile; 20 per sender and 600 per channel per minute when on). A message over the limit is dropped without a reply on the channel; the API answers 429 with `Retry-After`. Voice is exempt.
  - New build gates: an ArchUnit rule that freezes raw HTTP-client construction outside `security.net` (48 baselined sites), and a public-surface snapshot that fails when an unauthenticated route is added.
  - `runner=local` for the coding harness now also needs `app.coding-harness.acknowledge-local-runner-risk=true` and a loopback `server.address`; existing dev setups using the local runner must add both.
- **Skill usage counting follows the learning flag.** `load_skill` only counts a use of a custom skill (an extra UPDATE per load) when `app.skills.learning.enabled=true`; with it off the counters stay at zero. The Level 1 skill list in the system prompt is capped at about 9,000 characters (descriptions shrink to names past it) and notes how many skills it left out; catalogs of more than about 35 long-described skills will see a shorter list than before.
- **Personal mode: sign-in, embeddings and local models work out of the box.** The web container now gets `CORE_INTERNAL_URL` (it was missing, so every sign-in answered "could not reach the server"; with `--native-core` it points at `host.docker.internal`). The `personal` profile sets `app.embeddings.provider=auto` and the sidecar off unless `--profile rag`; the default Ollama URL is the one on this computer and is always allow-listed; the launcher checks and offers to pull the chat and embedding models, passes the host's RAM and accelerator to a containerised core, remembers `--cloud`, prints the sign-in e-mail and only a live setup link, and has a `restart` command. With `--native-core`, the sidecar, SearXNG and a container Ollama publish on `127.0.0.1`.
- **STDIO MCP sandbox, macOS: a network grant is outbound TCP only.** It was `(allow network*)`, which let a child connect to unix sockets (Docker, ssh-agent, gpg-agent, password manager) and to loopback services whatever the file-read denies said. Loopback now needs `app.mcp.stdio.sandbox-allow-loopback=true`. App token stores under `~/Library/Application Support`, `~/.claude`, shell history and the native launcher's deploy/compose directory are hidden from the child; on Linux `sandbox-extra-deny-read` now masks the listed paths in bubblewrap.
- **Taint source table and local sign-in lock.** `web_search`, `web_task`, `delegate_parallel`, `search_conversation_history` and `run_skill_script` are taint sources; `load_skill` and `read_skill_file` are declared reads, so loading a skill after a web fetch is no longer an approval for an unknown tool; `browser_navigate` honours `http-get-allow-hosts`. An address that already signed an account in can still verify its password while the account lock is on (rate limited, and a wrong guess does not extend the lock).
- **Generated custom-tool code is screened harder.** The tool-sandbox verifier now denies `Boolean.getBoolean` / `Integer.getInteger` / `Long.getLong`, `ResourceBundle`, parallel streams and nested classes of denied classes, and core screens generated code against an allowlist before it reaches the sandbox.
- **`app.sidecar.enabled=false` is a supported shape.** With the ML sidecar off, core does not try to reach it, the three health surfaces report it as not configured instead of down, rerank fails open, `html`/`htm`/`docx` are extracted in process, and PDF/OCR, transcription and media rendering answer 503 naming the setting.
- **Knowledge retrieval hot path**: query vectors from an external embedder go straight from `float[]` to the pgvector literal, and asymmetric embedding models get their query prefix.
- **Skill library API**: `GET /api/skills` accepts `status`, `limit` and `offset` (with `X-Total-Count` / `X-Has-More`) and returns lifecycle fields next to the old ones; `POST /api/skills/import` also accepts a bare `SKILL.md`. Existing fields and endpoints are unchanged. Skill import now refuses entry names with control characters and two entries that normalise to one path.
- **Repository hygiene.** Internal reports and generated PDFs are no longer tracked (root `*.pdf`, `*.docx`, `*.pptx` are ignored); `core/MCP-STDIO-SANDBOXING-DESIGN.md` moved to `docs/architecture/`. New gates: `scripts/ci/internal-reference-lint.sh` (planning jargon ratchet, baseline only shrinks) and `scripts/ci/no-telemetry-check.sh` (no analytics or error-reporting SDK or host in `web/`, `core/`, `services/`). Dependabot now covers `/sdk/python-agent`.

### Added

- **Console: `/troubleshoot`** — an owner-facing "something is wrong" panel, listed under Configuration: checks the API connection, whether the local model server answers, whether the recommended model is downloaded (with a one-click pull and progress), and whether the model makes tool calls (the agentic test). Stack-level failures point to `scripts/vegaduta doctor --personal`. Built on the existing `/api/local-models/*` endpoints; no backend change.
- **Browser install wizard (`installer/`, experimental)**: `vegaduta-setup` is a single static Go binary that serves a setup wizard on `127.0.0.1` — preflight checks (Docker, compose, memory, disk, ports, mirroring `doctor`), one-click Docker install/start per OS, verified release download (SHA256SUMS, Sigstore when cosign is installed), unpack, then `secrets` + `up` delegated to `scripts/vegaduta`. Defaults to the `personal` tier and finishes on the one-time first-run setup link. On failure a deterministic playbook explains the common causes, with an opt-in on-device model (WebLLM, WebGPU) for the rest. `--yes` runs headless, `--from` reuses an existing checkout. The release job builds unsigned per-OS binaries (`vegaduta-setup-<os>-<arch>`) into `SHA256SUMS`; `.msi`/`.dmg` wrapping and code signing are the remaining packaging step.
- **Console: `/assistant`, one box for a laptop** (Phase 1 of [the personal assistant surface](docs/architecture/PERSONAL-ASSISTANT-SURFACE-2026-10-17.md)). A new route behind an "Assistant" rail entry: type anything and a deterministic dispatch ladder picks a chat turn (default), a background job (`/background`, or "Keep going in the background" when the step cap stops a turn), a routine (`/schedule every weekday at 8 ...`) or a research run (`/research ...`); each rung falls back to a plain turn when its server switch is off. Tool steps collapse under one "Working for 2 m 10 s · 6 tool calls, 1 failed" row per turn, with guardrail events shown as "Skipped: reason". A per-user "Assistant" agent is found or created on first visit through the existing agent, memory and provider endpoints (SIMPLE mode, default model, every available tool minus payments and `http_request_write`, knowledge base on, marker in the `project:assistant` memory note). Threads are indexed in the browser (IndexedDB) and re-read from the stored transcript; the Work rail shows pending approvals and running jobs. No backend change; the `/` landing is unchanged. Renders in the console preview harness.
- **`/assistant`: connect first, tools that follow the connection.** A request that acts on a service known not to be connected (WhatsApp, Telegram, Slack, SMS, email, Google Calendar/Drive/Sheets) is held back with a card that links to the page that connects it, then carries on by itself when the person returns to the tab; "Answer without it" sends it anyway. The Assistant's turns now start with the app's connected / not-connected list so it never guesses, its system prompt asks for several differently-worded searches, page reads and `run_code`, and the tool allow-list picks up the tools of services connected after the agent was created (it was fixed at creation, so a newly linked Google or channel was never offered). An unedited first-version prompt is upgraded in place. Web only, existing endpoints.
- **An Assistant terminal for personal installs.** Two tools, `local_shell_read` (allow-listed inspection commands such as `ls`, `cat`, `grep`, `git status`; runs without asking) and `local_shell` (any command; the tool guard parks every call for approval, even for a fully autonomous agent, and the card shows the exact command). Both run in the existing OS sandbox: read-only filesystem except a workspace folder, credential folders hidden, no network unless `local-shell` is listed in `app.mcp.stdio.sandbox-network-allow`; `sudo`, power control and wipes are refused. Offered only on a one-person install with the sandbox on and confining (native launcher), off by default elsewhere (`app.assistant.local-shell.*`); the container stack, where the sandbox is `none`, does not get it.
- **Personal mode.** `scripts/vegaduta personal` runs VegaDūta for one person on one machine: Postgres, Valkey, core and web, with no Keycloak, Kafka or Temporal (about 2.4 GB of container limits). The new `personal` Spring profile turns on local sign-in, enforced taint tracking, an OS sandbox for STDIO MCP servers, the skill learning loop and turns billing off. `personal --native-core` runs core on the host JVM (Java 21+). Also `personal down|status|logs`, `make up-personal`, `deploy/compose/personal.yml` with `personal.build.yml` and `personal.native.yml` overlays, and `scripts/vegaduta.ps1 personal` (container core only; not run on Windows). See [docs/personal-mode.md](docs/personal-mode.md).
- **Local single-user sign-in** (`app.auth.mode=local`, default stays `keycloak`). Core issues and validates its own RS256 tokens, creates one owner and a default tenant on first start, and prints a one-time setup link. Rotating refresh tokens kept in an HttpOnly cookie by the web proxy, Argon2id or bcrypt password hashing, a brute-force throttle, and a local user-admin adapter so invites and roles work without Keycloak. Public sign-up is closed in this mode. Migration V510. No passkeys, no TOTP and no "forgot password" (recovery is a documented SQL statement). New web variable `NEXT_PUBLIC_AUTH_MODE`.
- **Taint tracking for untrusted content** (`app.agents.taint.mode`: `off` (default; `enforce` in `personal`), `observe`, `enforce`). A turn that read untrusted tool output (web pages, mail, retrieved documents) is marked tainted; in `enforce` the output is wrapped as data and any later tool that writes tenant data, uses stored credentials or sends data out needs human approval for the rest of that turn. `observe` (opt-in) only counts and audits what would have been blocked, one audit row per tool per tainted turn. Metrics `agent.taint.*`, audit entries `tool.taint.would_block` and `tool.taint.approval_required`. Inbound channel messages from non-owners are not yet seeded as taint, and taint does not carry across turns.
- **Sandbox for STDIO MCP servers** (`app.mcp.stdio.sandbox`: `none` (default), `os`, `container`). `os` uses macOS `sandbox-exec` or Linux bubblewrap (an `unshare` fallback is marked degraded); `container` runs the server under `docker`/`podman` with no capabilities and an optional network. Preflight at start-up, and `GET /api/system/stdio-sandbox` reports what is enforced. Static `application-mcp.yml` STDIO connections are not covered.
- **Skills lifecycle and a self-improving loop.** Skills have a status (pending, active, rejected, archived) and an origin (imported, learned, authored), version history (20 kept), usage and an effectiveness score, a stale flag, approve / reject / archive / per-agent enablement endpoints and a bounded prompt block. With `app.skills.learning.enabled=true` (off by default, on in `personal`) a successful multi-step turn that used no skill can produce a draft that waits as pending until a person approves it. Secrets are scrubbed from the draft and a draft needing more than three redactions is dropped. Migration V590. Web: **Skill library** at `/skills/library` with import, preview, diff of learned drafts, review actions and export.
- **Guided local-model setup.** `GET /api/local-models/recommendation`, `POST /api/local-models/probe`, `POST /api/local-models/pull` and `GET /api/local-models/pull/{jobId}` detect RAM, cores and GPU (NVIDIA or Apple Silicon), recommend a model from the shared catalog, check tool calling and pull through Ollama. The console wizard at `/providers/local-model` walks detect, choose, pull, check, create provider and first agent. The catalog moved to `core/providers/src/main/resources/local-models/catalog.txt` (`deploy/models/catalog.txt` is a symlink to it). Ollama builds that refuse `json_schema` (the MLX runner) fall back to a prompted schema when `app.llm.structured-output.ollama-format.enabled` is on.
- **Local / OpenAI-compatible server provider** (`LOCAL_OPENAI`): vLLM, llama.cpp, LM Studio, Jan or LocalAI, with a live model list, presets, calls metered at exactly $0, and `POST /api/providers/local-openai/probe` for a tool-calling check. A private or loopback server must be listed in `app.security.outbound.allowed-private-hosts`.
- **Free local embeddings.** `app.embeddings.provider` accepts `ollama` and `auto` (a cloud key wins, then a configured Ollama, then the sidecar); a width that does not fit the pgvector column is refused unless the model is Matryoshka (or `app.embeddings.ollama.allow-truncation=true`). Each knowledge collection records which embedder wrote it (V550); ingesting with another embedder answers 409 and vector search skips a mismatched collection until `POST /api/knowledge/collections/{id}/reembed`.
- **Signal channel** through an operator-run signal-cli REST API (`deploy/compose/signal.yml`, profile `signal`): default deny with a pairing code for unknown senders, opt-in groups, approval-gated replies. Migration V600. See [docs/channels/signal.md](docs/channels/signal.md).
- **Matrix channel**: bring a homeserver URL and a bot access token; `/sync` long poll (`app.matrix.sync.enabled`, off by default), pairing or allow-list sender policy, mention rule in group rooms, threads, small text files inlined as untrusted data. No end-to-end encryption (an encrypted room gets one notice, then is ignored). Migration V605. See [docs/matrix-channel.md](docs/matrix-channel.md).
- **iMessage channel** through BlueBubbles on a Mac that stays awake: webhook or polling (`app.imessage.polling.enabled`), allow-list and pairing, approval-gated replies. Migration V610. Never run against a real Mac so far. See [docs/imessage-channel.md](docs/imessage-channel.md).
- **Optional billing edition** (`app.edition.billing-enabled`, default `true`). `false` removes the billing, checkout, invoice and top-up endpoints and billing mail, resolves every plan limit as unlimited, and never shows an upgrade prompt; usage is still counted.
- **`docs/security-model.md`**: what taint tracking, the STDIO sandbox and channel pairing do and do not protect against.

- **Free web search and polish**: `scripts/vegaduta up --search` (also offered by the first-run question) runs a private SearXNG so agents get `web_search` with no API key; three ready-made bundles use it; Export button per agent, Connect in the sidebar, and a "Start from a ready-made agent" action on the empty agents list.
- **Console: Agent files and Connect.** `/agents/bundles` lets a person start from four ready-made agents or import an agent file, with a preview of tools, autonomy and safety adjustments before anything is created; `/connect` shows Telegram, WhatsApp, email, Google, Slack, SMS and documents with connected status and opens the page that connects each.
- **Shareable agent bundles**: `GET /api/agent-bundles/{agentId}` exports an agent as one JSON file, `POST /api/agent-bundles/preview` shows what importing would do and `POST /api/agent-bundles/import` creates the agent (never `FULLY_AUTONOMOUS`, no knowledge-base ids, audited). Four ready-made bundles in `examples/agent-bundles/` with an offline validator.
- **Telegram without a public URL**: on the `lite` profile (`app.telegram.polling.enabled`), a connected bot receives messages by long polling, so a laptop or office server can talk to its agent from a phone with only a bot token. Webhook installs are unchanged.
- **Starter agent and tool-call check for local models**: V502 adds the credential-free "Personal Assistant (works on a local model)" template (web fetch, memory, documents, human approval) and `scripts/vegaduta up` now probes whether the chosen local model makes tool calls and says what to do if not; `hermes3:8b` joins the model catalog as unverified.
- **First-run model choice** in `scripts/vegaduta up`: an interactive first install asks whether agents use a free open-weights model on this machine (size picked from RAM, host Ollama used when present), a cloud key, or a catalog model; stored in the state file. `--cloud` skips the question; non-interactive runs are unchanged.
- **Module-size ratchet** (ADR-0023 section 6): `scripts/ci/check-module-size.sh`, run by `make gates` and `static-gates.yml`, fails when a `core/*` module grows past 300 main `.java` files or a class is added directly to `com.agenticai.core.agents`; the modules and root package already over are frozen in the shrink-only `scripts/ci/module-size-baseline.txt`.
- **API conventions, ADR-0026 roadmap** (all opt-in for clients; see [docs/api/README.md](docs/api/README.md#api-conventions)):
  - `Idempotency-Key` on authenticated JSON `POST`/`PATCH`. A retry replays the first response for 24 hours, a key still in flight answers 409, and a reused key with a different body answers 422.
  - `ETag` / `If-Match` on agents and workflows. A stale write answers 412.
  - Cursor paging (`cursor`, `X-Next-Cursor`) on the audit log and workflow run history.
  - An opt-in per-caller API budget with IETF `RateLimit` headers (`app.rate-limit.api.enabled`).
  - `@ApiDeprecated` with `Deprecation`/`Sunset`/`Link` headers and a 180-day policy ([DEPRECATION.md](docs/api/DEPRECATION.md)).
  - The OpenAPI spec now documents outbound webhooks, each operation's required permissions (`x-permissions`), and descriptions for the main integration routes.
  - `docs/api/asyncapi.json` (AsyncAPI 3.0) describes the SSE streams, webhooks and the voice relay.
- **OpenAPI spec states the API's real conventions** (ADR-0026): the `Error` schema is now the RFC 9457 Problem Details body the server sends (`application/problem+json`, legacy members kept); a `tenantApiKey` scheme (`Bearer vdak_...`) is listed beside the JWT on authenticated `/api/**` routes; the routes throttled by `PublicEndpointRateLimitFilter` document `429` with `Retry-After`; paged list endpoints document `X-Total-Count` / `X-Has-More`; error responses document `X-Request-Id`; `info.license` is set. The generated SDK `ErrorBody` types gain the Problem Details fields. ADR-0026 also records the decision not to add a general GraphQL API and the API roadmap.
- **OpenAPI breaking-change gate** (`scripts/ci/openapi-breaking.mjs`, in `make gates` and the static-gates CI job): fails when `docs/api/openapi.json` removes a path, operation or success-response property, renames an operationId, makes a parameter, request body or request property required, narrows an enum or changes a type, compared with the merge base. Deliberate breaks go in `scripts/ci/openapi-breaking-allowlist.txt` with a reason. See [docs/api/README.md](docs/api/README.md#breaking-change-gate).
- **Nightly load gate** (`.github/workflows/load-nightly.yml`, `make load-gate`): runs the 32-user chat benchmark on a throwaway kind cluster with the mock model, and fails on p95 turn latency or a failed-turn rate above 1%.
- **Consumer-driven contracts** (ADR-0025): `ConsumerContractVerificationTest` in core checks every `contracts/consumers/**` file against the generated OpenAPI spec. The web console contract is generated by `web/scripts/api-contract.mjs`; the Java, Python, Python Agent and TypeScript SDKs have their own contracts and consumer-side tests. `docs/api/openapi.json` freshness is now gated.

- **Small-model mode for pool rows of 8B parameters or fewer** (auto-set from the size the server reports or the model tag, overridable per row; control-plane migration V501): turns served by such a row offer at most six tools chosen by relevance to the message, use the compact prompt directives (a short tool-trust rule, skills listed by name only until `load_skill`) and run at Fast reasoning depth. `docs/local-models.md` now documents the four layers (recovery, argument validation, grade and router, small-model mode) and a free hosted fallback chain (local Ollama, then Groq `gpt-oss-20b`, then Gemini Flash Lite).
- **Constrained tool router for small local models.** A hosted-pool row graded TEXT or NONE on a server that can enforce a constraint now gets its tool turns routed through one: on Ollama and llama.cpp the reply is constrained to the JSON schema `{action: call|answer, tool: <offered tools>, arguments, answer}` (`response_format: json_schema`), on vLLM through `tool_choice: required` plus a `final_answer` tool. The chosen call runs through the same tool loop as a structured call (argument validation, feedback, round caps), and the model is called again with the result until it answers or `app.chat.tools.constrained-router.max-rounds` (6) is reached; the final answer arrives as one chunk. Rows graded STRUCTURED are not touched; a per-row override (`toolRouterEnabled`, control-plane migration V500) forces it on or off. Metric `chat.tool.constrained_router{outcome}`; switch `app.chat.tools.constrained-router.enabled`.
- **Tool-calling grade on every hosted-pool row.** Saving a platform-hosted LLM row now sends the model one two-tool probe and stores the result (`tool_call_grade`, control-plane migration V499): STRUCTURED (a structured `tool_calls` entry came back), TEXT (the call was written as text) or NONE (prose, or Ollama reports no `tools` capability, graded without a call). The grade shows as a badge in Platform Admin, LLM providers; a NONE row is skipped for chat turns while any other enabled row exists; `scripts/vegaduta models pull` measures it after a pull and `models list` has a TOOLS column from `deploy/models/catalog.txt`, where `llama3.2:3b` is now marked chat only and `qwen3:8b` and `granite4.2:3b` are offered. Switch `app.llm.tool-call-probe.enabled`.
- **Agents work on small and free models: text-form tool calls are recovered and bad arguments are sent back.** A reply that is a tool call written as text (`{"name": ..., "parameters": ...}`, `<tool_call>`, `<function=...>`, `[TOOL_CALLS]`, fenced or not) to a tool the agent was offered is now executed through the normal tool loop and the model is called again with the result, instead of the JSON being shown as the answer; it applies to every provider through the one seam all chat clients are built on, and a reply that is not such a call is untouched (a streamed reply is held only while its first characters could still be a call). Before any tool runs, its arguments are checked against the tool's schema: a call missing a `required` property is answered with the problems, the unknown keys and the schema so the model can retry (property types are not checked). Bounded by `app.chat.tools.text-call-recovery.max-per-invocation` (3) and the existing tool-round cap; switches `app.chat.tools.text-call-recovery.enabled` and `app.chat.tools.argument-validation.enabled`; metrics `chat.tool.text_call_recovery` and `chat.tool.argument_rejected`. See [Tool calls from small models](docs/local-models.md#tool-calls-from-small-models). Unit-tested against the exact reply from the playground incident; not yet run against a live small model.
- **Turn journal, first slice** ([ADR-0020](docs/architecture/ADR-0020-turn-journal.md), off by default behind `app.turn-journal.enabled`): a hash-chained per-turn event journal (intake, guard, model call, tool call, approval, verify, commit) written at the existing chat-turn stage boundaries, an in-memory and a Postgres store (tenant migration V498) and a read endpoint `GET /api/agents/{agentId}/turns/{turnId}/journal` (View audit log). Payloads hold hashes and redacted summaries only. **Turn inspector** page at `/agents/turns` ([ADR-0021](docs/architecture/ADR-0021-turn-inspector.md)): timeline, chain-verification badge, export as a test fixture.
- **Conformance by construction** ([ADR-0019](docs/architecture/ADR-0019-conformance-by-construction.md)): a build test fails for any list-returning GET without paging parameters unless it is in a ratcheting allowlist (151 entries at the start, 82 after this round) and a generated configuration reference (`docs/engineering/CONFIG-REFERENCE.generated.md`) with a check that new properties are documented; `docs/configuration.md` now documents all 1,240 previously undocumented properties.
- **Server paging on 69 more list endpoints** (additive: no parameters keeps today's response; `limit`/`offset`, `X-Total-Count`, `X-Has-More`), `actor`/`q` filters on `GET /api/audit-log`, STAGING/PRODUCTION `label` on workflow version rows, and an `accessLevel` field (EDITOR/VIEWER/RUNNER) on agent and workflow responses.
- **Shared console controls** `Select`, `Checkbox`, `Combobox` and `ConfirmDialog` (`web/app/components/ui`) with an ESLint ratchet that forbids new native `<select>` and `window.confirm`/`prompt` (the allowlist shrinks as files are migrated); `web/dev/ui-preview` renders real pages without signing in. `web/scripts/i18n-parity.mjs` reports locale coverage, prunes dead keys (202,300 removed) and enforces a ratchet ([docs/engineering/I18N.md](docs/engineering/I18N.md)).
- **List paging for silently capped endpoints** ([docs/api/LIST-ENDPOINT-PAGING.md](docs/api/LIST-ENDPOINT-PAGING.md)): `GET /api/audit-log` takes `limit` (1..1000, default 50 once a paging param is sent; callers sending neither keep the old newest-200) and `offset`; `GET /api/platform/users`, `GET /api/notifications`, `GET /api/quick-task/recent` (default still 50) and `GET /api/platform/ai-reports` gain `offset` (and `limit` where missing). All return `X-Total-Count` and `X-Has-More`, so a UI can show that a list is truncated. Bodies are unchanged.
- **Standard console navigation module** (`web/app/components/nav/navSections.ts`, extracted from `AppShell`): the rail, the hub flyout, the mobile drawer and the command palette now share one blueprint of sections (Build, Connect, Test & Monitor, Plans, Help, Administration) with per-item permission gating (`perm`, `adminOnly`, `adminHidden`). New rows for Cost & ROI (`/roi`), Payments (`/admin/payments`), agent quality and SSO (`/admin/sso`). Behaviour-free apart from the new rows; covered by `navSections.test.ts`.
- **One shared pager** (`PaginationControls`): page sizes 10/25/50/100, a client mode (slice in the browser) and a server mode (`limit`/`offset` plus `X-Total-Count`), and a notice when the server silently capped a list. Used across the admin, platform-admin and tenant list pages, which also gain the same loading, empty and error states and filters. The audit log, platform users, notifications, quick-task history and AI reports pages use server paging (the matching endpoints are listed under List paging above).
- **New console screens for existing endpoints:** DPDP privacy controls at `/admin/data-privacy` (six tabs: consent policy, retention, purposes, contacts export/erasure, audit export, workspace data), tenant SSO and SCIM tokens at `/admin/sso` (`/api/tenant/sso/**`), workflow version history and comments, agent version rollback and diff, an agents index, a notifications page, a WhatsApp opt-in card (record or withdraw a number's opt-in) and a budget top-up card on the usage page. No new backend behaviour: these are UI for APIs that already existed.
- **Pilot kit** ([docs/pilots/README.md](docs/pilots/README.md)): a one-page 30-day pilot process, a tenant-entered pre-pilot baseline per agent (Cost & ROI page, `GET/PUT /api/tenant/roi/agents/{id}/baseline`, every field labelled tenant-entered), and a one-page pilot report per agent and date range as HTML or PDF (`GET /api/tenant/roi/agents/{id}/pilot-report?format=html|pdf|json`) containing only recorded data, the tenant's own numbers in an assumptions box, and ROI "based on your assumptions". A [case-study template](docs/pilots/case-study-template.md) with a consent checklist. `scripts/vegaduta seed-demo` fills an empty tenant with fictional Example data; a seeded tenant shows an Example watermark on the Cost & ROI page and on every report (migration V497).
- **Bundled Ollama chart.** `ollama.enabled=true` deploys a single-node Ollama (persistent model volume, model pulled on first start, Ready only once usable) as the free pool; off by default. `deploy/kind/values-kind-ollama.yaml` wires core's pool bootstrap to it and documents the memory it needs.
- **MCP specification alignment** ([docs/architecture/mcp-spec-alignment.md](docs/architecture/mcp-spec-alignment.md), checked against modelcontextprotocol.io on 2026-10-14; the current revision is 2026-07-28, previous 2025-11-25): requirement-by-requirement status for the MCP client, `/mcp` server and stdio relay. **Server `/mcp`:** Origin validation (403 with a JSON-RPC error when a present `Origin` is not in `app.mcpserver.origin.allowed`; no `Origin` is unaffected; **on by default**, `mode=report` to observe first) and an optional OAuth 2.1 mode (`app.mcpserver.oauth.enabled`, default off): RFC 9728 protected resource metadata, audience-bound tokens, exact-match scopes without wildcards, the inbound token never passed downstream; `vmcp_` grant keys keep working as the simple option. **Client:** `application_type` in dynamic client registration; a regression test that a token for one server is never sent to another. **Tool-definition pinning:** a hash of each remote MCP server's tool list is recorded; a change is audited (`mcp.tools.changed`), listed at `GET /api/mcp/tool-pins` and, with `app.mcp.tool-pinning.mode=enforce`, withheld from agents until `POST /api/mcp/tool-pins/{id}/approve` (default `warn`: record and report only; migration V491). **Transport:** HTTP+SSE marked deprecated in the registration form, the log and the docs (still works); optional `Mcp-Method`/`Mcp-Name` request headers (`app.mcp.client.routing-headers.enabled`, default off). **Console:** a tool-pin review card (what changed, Approve) above the Tools → Servers and Enabled servers lists, a read-only "How callers authenticate to /mcp" card on the MCP access page (`GET /api/mcpserver/grants/auth-options`), and an "SSE (deprecated)" label in the add-server form. **Pending your decision** (listed in the doc): default tool-pin mode, Origin default, SSE removal timeline, Keycloak claim setup. **Not done:** the stateless 2026-07-28 protocol itself (no `initialize`, `server/discover`, `subscriptions/listen`) because the MCP Java SDK in use stops at 2025-11-25; OAuth step-up on 403. Unit, wiring and one integration test with a server that changes its tool list; not run against a live stack or a real authorization server.
- **WhatsApp policy guard** ([docs/channels/whatsapp-policy.md](docs/channels/whatsapp-policy.md), facts fetched from Meta's pages on 2026-10-14): attaching an agent to a WhatsApp number now requires a declared task (stored with who/when on the binding), an open-ended-looking agent gets a warning the tenant can override (override stored and audit-logged), template sends need an opt-in record, Meta errors 131049/131050 pause marketing templates to that recipient, and the phone number quality rating shows on the channel page when Meta returns it. Four task-specific WhatsApp templates (front desk, booking, payment follow-up, lead follow-up); open-ended templates are marked not for WhatsApp. **Changed:** `PUT /api/channels/whatsapp/default-agent` now needs `task`; the WhatsApp cost calculator no longer pre-fills third-party rates (Meta's documentation publishes none) and shows "check Meta's current rates" until you enter yours, with the date the model was checked. Unit-tested; not run against a real WhatsApp account.
- **Appointment booking on Google Calendar, and append-only Sheets writes.** Three new agent tools on the existing Google connection: `google_calendar_find_free_slots` (open slots inside working hours, skipping busy, declined and "available" events; read-only), `google_calendar_move_event` and `google_calendar_cancel_event` (both classified irreversible, so the confirmation gate asks a human; `notify_attendees` is required). `google_sheets` gains `append_rows`, written as RAW text so customer-typed values cannot run as formulas. No new OAuth scope. See [Google Calendar booking and Sheets append](docs/connectors/google-calendar-sheets.md). Unit-tested with fixtures from Google's documented responses; not run against a real Google account.
- **Local model catalog.** `scripts/vegaduta models list|pull|rm` shows `deploy/models/catalog.txt`, marks installed models and installs or removes them in the stack's Ollama (container or host).
- **A2A HTTP+JSON/REST binding** (spec section 11) at `/api/a2a/rest` and `/api/agents/{agentId}/a2a/rest`: `message:send`, `message:stream`, `tasks/{id}`, `tasks/{id}:cancel`, errors as `google.rpc.Status` with `ErrorInfo` and the spec's HTTP statuses. Both cards now list it in `supportedInterfaces`. Operations the server does not offer (ListTasks, SubscribeToTask, push configs, extended card) answer with the spec's `UnsupportedOperationError`/`PushNotificationNotSupportedError` on both bindings instead of method-not-found. Requirement-by-requirement status: [a2a-conformance.md](docs/architecture/a2a-conformance.md). gRPC is not implemented. Unit-tested and routed through Spring MVC in a test; not run on a stack.
- **OWASP mapping** ([docs/security/owasp-mapping.md](docs/security/owasp-mapping.md)): every item of the OWASP Agentic, LLM and MCP Top 10 lists mapped to a control, a test, or an explicit gap, with draft issues for the High gaps in `.github/launch-issues/`.
- **Operator egress allow-list** `app.security.outbound.egress-allow-list` (`host` or `*.suffix`): when set, tenant-configured outbound URLs reach only those hosts. **Per-turn cap on remote A2A calls** `app.a2a.client.max-calls-per-turn` (default 5). **Write-time screen for `add_to_knowledge_base`**: agent-written notes that carry injection phrases or credentials are refused before ingestion. A read-only **Outbound allow-list / Agent-to-agent** panel on Platform Admin -> Safety & Moderation shows these settings (`GET /api/platform/security/outbound-policy`). Unit-tested; the panel was not viewed in a browser.
- **DPDP-oriented controls** (engineering controls, not a compliance claim; legal readings are marked "to be confirmed by counsel" in [docs/compliance/dpdp-obligations.md](docs/compliance/dpdp-obligations.md), which lists the Act and Rules provisions read from the official MeitY text, each with its date and source). All opt-in per tenant or agent, under `/api/tenant/privacy/**` (`PERM_MANAGE_DATA_GOVERNANCE`, migration V495): a per-agent notice and consent step for channel conversations (notice text per language, `LANG xx`, `YES`/`NO`/`STOP`; until a contact agrees there is no model call, tool, memory write, cache lookup or stored message text; an append-only consent record with timestamp, channel, notice version, language, notice hash and purpose); purpose tags on knowledge collections and agents that close a tagged collection to an agent with another purpose; per-tenant and per-channel retention periods for transcripts, recordings and extracted memory, purged nightly with an audit event per purge (nothing is deleted until a tenant enables a rule; the default period is 365 days and shorter periods warn); per-contact export and erasure that covers messages, memory including graph memory, agent-written knowledge chunks, the live conversation window and the contact record, and returns what it did not reach; and a time-range audit export (CSV or JSON with seals) with a [breach runbook](docs/operations/breach-runbook.md). Not covered: children's data, workflow-trigger and outbound sends (not consent-gated), graph memory retention, operational LLM logs, backups. Unit-tested and tested against Postgres with two tenant schemas (`DpdpControlsIT`); the 32-user benchmark has not been run on a stack with this change.
- **Cost and ROI per agent** ([docs/cost-and-roi.md](docs/cost-and-roi.md)). New console page `/roi` and `/api/tenant/roi/**`: estimated cost per agent, workflow, channel, owner and day from recorded tokens and a tenant-editable, dated price table (V496; no seed and no fallback rate, so an unpriced model is listed and excluded, never guessed); outcomes (resolved, handed to a human, abandoned, corrected by a human) on the existing resolution meter and `turn_feedback` EDIT rows; handover, human-correction and approval-denial rates; a monthly budget bar over the cap the gateway enforces with 80% and 100% bell alerts (`roi.budget-alerts.enabled`); CSV export. ROI is computed only from the value and minutes-saved per resolution the tenant types in per agent, and every ROI figure is labelled "based on your assumptions". `llm_call_log.reasoning_tokens` is now recorded.
- **Public site fixes from the stage E2E pass (WEB-5).** `/robots.txt`, `/sitemap.xml` and `/llms.txt` are rendered per request, so their origin comes from the pod's `SITE_URL` instead of a `localhost` default frozen at build (SE-05); in oss site mode the sitemap no longer lists routes that redirect to `/` (SE-24) and `/llms.txt` serves an open-source variant without plans or UPI (SE-67). Unknown URLs render the 404 page instead of the signed-out landing (SE-22). The landing header's Dependencies link scrolls when already on `/` (SE-23). `NEXT_PUBLIC_ACADEMY_URL` has no localhost default; unset hides the Academy links (SE-25). `/plugins` no longer lists a plugin as Available while the catalog says Coming soon, and `/plugins` and `/account-deletion` omit the contact mailto when `NEXT_PUBLIC_CONTACT_EMAIL` is unset (SE-46). `/docs` on the admin host is no longer redirected to the 404 `/platform-admin/docs` (SE-82). Unit-tested; not run on a stack.
- **Plain SMS is a conversation channel.** A feature phone with no data plan can now text a tenant's Twilio number and talk to an agent: `ChannelType.SMS`, the same pairing, default-agent, conversation-inbox and human-handoff machinery as every other channel (`PUT /api/channels/sms/default-agent`, Channels → WhatsApp → "Plain SMS on this number"), replies through the approval gate as `channel.sms.send` (`TwilioSmsChannelAdapter`), a one-line "you are on SMS, keep it short and plain" hint on each turn, and replies cut at a sentence boundary to `app.omnichannel.sms.max-reply-chars` (default 1,500; Twilio's ceiling is 1,600). A workflow bound to the SMS trigger channel still takes over, as before. New event `channel.sms.message.received` for agent triggers and webhooks. See [SMS conversations](docs/sms-channel.md). Unit-tested; not run against a real Twilio number.
- **Free means free: zero-cost hosted-pool rows** (`zero_cost`, migrations V484 and V485; Platform Admin → LLM providers → "Free (self-hosted)"). A pool row the operator runs themselves (the `--local-ai` Ollama, vLLM, llama.cpp, any OpenAI-compatible server) was priced at the unknown-model fallback rate ($0.50 / $2.00 per MTok), debited the per-user daily budget, counted against the FREE plan's 400k-token monthly quota and sorted last in the cheapest-first failover order. Such a row is now priced at $0 everywhere (`LlmPricingCatalog.zeroCost()`), tried first, and when every enabled row is zero-cost the turn reserves and meters nothing (`app.free-tier.meter-zero-cost-rows=true` keeps the token quota). The local-LLM bootstrap creates its row as zero-cost; existing Ollama rows are backfilled. Existing paid rows are unchanged. See [Zero-cost pool rows](docs/configuration.md#zero-cost-pool-rows).
- **Edge relay: a laptop is the model behind your number.** A signed-in browser with an on-device model registers on `/edge` as the relay for the workspace's SMS and/or WhatsApp channels (`/api/channels/edge-relay/**`, `PERM_MANAGE_CHANNELS`). While it is online, an inbound message on those channels is queued in Valkey, the browser answers it with the local tier ladder (agent persona plus the last twelve messages of the session), and the server thread that owns the message sends the reply through the normal approval gate. No relay online, a full queue, a one-minute timeout, a decline or any Valkey error means the hosted model answers the same message on the same thread; a timed-out job is cancelled so a late reply is dropped. Several devices can relay at once. `app.omnichannel.edge-relay.*` (`EDGE_RELAY_*`), on by default; `enabled=false` removes it. See [Edge relay](docs/edge-relay.md). Unit-tested on both sides; not run against a real number.
- **QR code on the agent Share panel**: the public share link is also shown as a scannable code, so a printed poster or a screen lets any phone open the agent without typing.
- **"AI for everyone" gap analysis** ([docs/architecture/AI-FOR-EVERYONE-2026-10-14.md](docs/architecture/AI-FOR-EVERYONE-2026-10-14.md)): what the edge, hosted and channel paths offer today to people without a laptop, a GPU, English, data or money, where each falls short (with code pointers), and the ordered list of what to build next.
- **Keycloak admin console on the kind stage.** `tests/load/kind/keycloak-admin-console.sh enable` sets the master realm's Frontend URL so the console works through a port-forward at `http://kc-admin.127.0.0.1.nip.io:18081/admin/master/console/` without publishing `/admin` or the master realm on the public ingress. (`keycloak.admin.consoleUrl` alone does not help: the console builds its Keycloak client from the realm Frontend URL, so its login iframe still hit the public host and timed out.)
- **A guard against new async hops that drop the tenant.** `AsyncHopTenantScopeRulesTest` (ArchUnit, `core/app`) fails when a class submits work to an executor, calls a `CompletableFuture` async stage, uses a Reactor `subscribeOn`/`publishOn`/`interval`/`delay`, starts a raw thread or declares an `@Async` method without `TenantScope`, `ContextPropagatingExecutor` or `TenantScopedFlux` in the same method, unless the class is listed with a reason in `async-hop-allowlist.txt`; a stale entry fails too. See [Extending VegaDūta](docs/extending.md#8-hand-work-to-another-thread).
- **Stage validation tooling and results.** `tests/load/kind/cell.sh`, an all-features values overlay, request-based autoscaling on kind (Prometheus, prometheus-adapter, HPA target), per-status failure counters and a stream-completeness check in `turn-overhead.js`; results of the all-features run, the rolling-restart drill, the autoscaling step, and an image A/B in `docs/architecture/HOT-PATH-PERFORMANCE.md` section 4c.
- **The BYOK spend counter now self-corrects.** `LlmSpendCounterReconciler` (`billing` job pool) recomputes `llm_spend_month` for the current and previous UTC month from `llm_call_log` for every active tenant, every `app.llm.spend-counter.reconcile-interval` (default `1h`, `0`/`off` disables). Calls logged by pods on an older image during a rolling deploy never updated the counter (measured on the stage: 4.050 against a true 4.269), which let a tenant overshoot its cap until month end. The correction takes the counter row lock before it computes the SUM, so a concurrent increment is not lost; new metric `llm.spend.counter.drift.usd{outcome}`. Tested on real Postgres in `LlmSpendCounterReconcilerIT`; effect on a single very busy tenant row not measured.
- **Generated shape ownership tables.** `scripts/docs/gen-shapes.py` regenerates the scheduled-bean tables in `docs/deploy/DEPLOYMENT-SHAPES.md` from the `@Scheduled` sources and the `application-shape-*.yml` profiles; `--check` and the generator's fixture tests (`scripts/docs/test_gen_shapes.py`) run in `make gates` and the static-gates workflow. The profile reader stops with an error on any `app.scheduling` syntax it does not understand rather than returning an empty list, and the tables follow the pool model (`app.scheduling.pools` and `pool-lifecycle` in `application-shape-jobs.yml` are read, so a new pool needs no generator change; the generator fails if a pool has no row in the shapes table). The first run found two scheduled beans missing from the hand-written tables (`DataRetentionPurge`, `ImpersonationService`); the prose count of locked API-shape jobs (previously a hand-written 14 of 15 rows, all of which the sources show locked) is now generated.
- **Parallel knowledge ingestion (opt-in).** `KNOWLEDGE_INGESTION_CLAIMING_ENABLED=true` replaces the single poll-wide advisory lock with per-job claiming (`FOR UPDATE SKIP LOCKED`, a heartbeat in the existing `updated_at` column, no migration), so that `ingest` replicas can work different jobs instead of one pod holding the lock for the whole queue. A job whose runner died is re-run by another replica after `KNOWLEDGE_INGESTION_CLAIM_LEASE_SECONDS` (default `300`), with the dead run's partial chunks removed first, at most `KNOWLEDGE_INGESTION_CLAIM_MAX_ATTEMPTS` (default `3`) times, then the job is marked `FAILED`. Off by default: with the flag off the lock path is unchanged except that it now starts a job only if it is still `QUEUED`. Tested on real Postgres in `KnowledgeIngestionClaimingIT` (6 concurrent threads in one JVM over 60 jobs, the attempt cap, fencing, reclaim purge, a heartbeat thread); not run as separate replica processes, and Helm `core-ingest` still needs `leaderElection` for more than one replica.
- **BYOK spend check reads one counter row instead of summing the call log.** The monthly LLM spend guard (`LlmGatewayBudgetService`) used to run `SUM(estimated_cost_usd)` over `llm_call_log` on every BYOK turn. Migration `V481` adds `llm_spend_month` (one row per tenant schema and UTC month, backfilled for the current month); `LlmCallLogService.record` adds each call's cost to it in the same transaction as the log row, behind a savepoint so a failing counter write is logged and does not lose the log row. A month without a row falls back to the old `SUM`, and a failed read still fails open. During a rolling update, calls logged by a pod that predates this change are missing from the counter for the rest of that month (see [Hot-path performance](docs/architecture/HOT-PATH-PERFORMANCE.md)).
- **Fewer Redis round trips per chat turn.** Appending a conversation turn to session memory was 3 to 6 sequential Redis commands, twice per turn; it is now one Lua call with the same ordering, TTL and trimming (`app.chat.session-append-single-roundtrip`, default on, `false` restores the old sequence). The cancel watcher no longer sends a blocking `DEL` per in-flight turn every 500 ms: one Lua call per pod on the shared Redis connection covers all of them (`app.chat.cancel-poll-interval`, default `500ms`; the poll interval is unchanged). Counted against a real Valkey (template calls, with connections opened checked separately via `INFO stats`): 8 to 2 commands per turn without the summariser, 9 to 2 with it; one cancel poll covering 200 turns is 1 command and opens no new TCP connection. If Redis refuses the scripts (ACL, scripting disabled, Redis Cluster cross-slot) both paths fall back to the old commands; a timeout or dropped connection never triggers a replay. Opt-in `app.chat.stream-end.async` (default off) moves the end-of-stream audit, event, RL close and call-log write to a bounded pool that drains on graceful stop. Not measured end to end on a stack; see `docs/architecture/HOT-PATH-PERFORMANCE.md`.
- **Horizontal scaling by workload class** ([ADR-0014](docs/architecture/ADR-0014-horizontal-scaling.md), [Deployment shapes](docs/deploy/DEPLOYMENT-SHAPES.md)). The chat path stays in one process; what scales is replicas and background work.
  - Helm: with `replicaCount > 1`, an HPA or KEDA the chart sets `APP_REPLICA_MODE=multi` and moves **all nine** per-pod stores to Valkey (it switched three; guardrail procedure state, the tool effect journal, sandbox files, the E2B quota, resumable streams and Studio approvals stayed in memory). New `replicaReadiness` flags default to true, so existing multi-replica installs keep rendering; `replicaStrict` makes fatal findings refuse to boot. Existing installs roll their core pods once (the config checksum changes).
  - `ReplicaStoreCoverageTest` fails the build when a store with a Valkey switch is neither checked at boot nor wired in the chart. `ReplicaModeSafetyCheck` also reports the in-memory tool effect journal.
  - Autoscaling: `hpa.metrics` passthrough, slow scale-in by default, opt-in KEDA `ScaledObject`, and `examples/values-autoscaling.yaml` on open requests per pod. New `examples/values-scale-out.yaml`.
  - The umbrella chart refuses a replica or autoscaling setting whose worst-case database connections (pods x Hikari pool) exceed the server limit (`postgres.parameters.max_connections`, or `global.postgresql.maxConnections` for an external server).
  - Optional `core-ingest` pool (`global.components.coreIngest`, off by default): document ingestion and re-embedding run in their own Deployment on the `core-jobs` image. New `app.scheduling.keep-bean-classes` and profiles `shape-jobs-core` / `shape-jobs-ingest`; `JobsPoolsCoverEveryJobTest` proves a split never drops a job. The API shape now also leaves `KnowledgeReembedJobRunner` to the jobs shape.
  - Optional `core-webhooks`, `core-intake` and `core-billing` pools (`global.components.coreWebhooks`, `coreIntake`, `coreBilling`; all off by default, each needs `coreJobs`): webhook delivery and the event outbox relay, the inbound email and X DM pollers, and the usage rollup / payment reconciliation / comp-grant sweeps each get their own Deployment on the `core-jobs` image. New `app.scheduling.pools`, `app.scheduling.pool`, `app.scheduling.offloaded-pools` and profile `shape-jobs-pool`; the main jobs pod now lists the pools that run elsewhere in `APP_SCHEDULING_OFFLOADED_POOLS` (with only `core-ingest` on it is equivalent to `shape-jobs-core`, which stays). The `intake` pool also keeps the per-JVM MCP, browser and sandbox sweeps (`app.scheduling.pool-lifecycle`) because its pollers run agent turns; the other pools run none of them, and all pool pods still run the boot listeners not listed in `skip-listener-classes`. Intake and billing pools default to a Hikari max of 8. The connection-budget guard counts every enabled pool. With every pool off nothing changes. The scheduling partition is unit-tested for all pool combinations at matcher level and through `SchedulingRoleRegistrar`; no pool pod has been started.
  - Not run on a cluster with two or more API replicas; render and unit-test verified only.
- Site-mode switch `NEXT_PUBLIC_SITE_MODE` (`WEB_SITE_MODE` in Compose): the signed-out pages default to a technical open-source landing and About with no commercial content; `classic` restores the original marketing pages.
- Free local models: `scripts/vegaduta up --local-ai[=host] [--local-model TAG]` adds an Ollama service (or uses the one on
  the host), pulls the model and registers it as the platform pool, so FREE accounts chat without any API key. `doctor`
  checks the host Ollama and the model. New guide: `docs/local-models.md`. `WEB_EDGE_AGENTS=1` is now the example default.
- **Sync from the private upstream, 2026-10-09 to 2026-10-12** (120 commits; same three-way method as the earlier syncs, OSS-only hardening kept):
  scale and reliability limits (bounded per-tenant executor and admission control for inbound channel webhooks that never drops a message, idle back-off for per-tenant pollers, fair per-tenant caps for knowledge ingestion, a cap on held advisory locks, opt-in retention purge with dry-run, circuit breakers on by default, `limit`/`offset` on four list endpoints with a "Load more" control; see [Configuration](docs/configuration.md#scale-and-reliability-limits)); MCP stdio runner child caps, queue, idle TTL, heap limit and graceful drain; code-intel concurrency, repository size and quota limits; platform admin: complete tenant erasure and demo reclaim, ops console and broadcast banner, per-tenant monthly spend caps (402 `spend_cap_reached`), read-only "view as" for platform admins (`X-View-As`, GET only, platform-admin required), multipart newsletter with `List-Unsubscribe`; the published template standard with an automatic and optional AI review gate before submission (`POST /api/templates/{id}/validate`, 422 `TEMPLATE_NOT_READY`); Keycloak user listing now pages. New migrations V475 to V480 (the upstream numbers V474 to V479 collided with an OSS `V474`).
  Not imported on purpose: the D-U-N-S company credential, landing/marketing/legal page changes, production deploy documentation and scripts, the Azure and GCP deploy workflows, the WhatsApp QR and Discord bridge services, two SAP-specific agent templates (OSS ships no SAP connector).
- **Public-page wiring**: `NEXT_PUBLIC_DOCS_URL` (`WEB_DOCS_URL`) points every "Docs" link at the published site; `NEXT_PUBLIC_COMMERCIAL_URL` (`WEB_COMMERCIAL_URL`) adds a "Hosted version" link to the open-source pages. The OSS landing gains "Use it as a dependency" and "Where it runs" sections (SDKs, plugins, Android app, MCP server, with real statuses); the classic footer links plugins, the Java SDK, the source and the self-host guide; `/llms.txt` has a Developers section.
- **SDK release workflow** (`.github/workflows/sdk-release.yml`): `sdk-v*` tags build, test, attest SLSA provenance, create a GitHub Release and, once enabled, upload signed artifacts to the Maven Central Portal. Dependabot and the labeler cover `sdk/java`.
- **VegaDūta Agent SDK** (`sdk/java/`, Maven group `io.github.cd-lc`, design in
  [ADR-0013](docs/architecture/ADR-0013-embeddable-agent-sdk.md)): an embeddable,
  governance-first library for Java agents. `vegaduta-agent-api` (framework-free
  contracts), `-core` (policy gate, lethal-trifecta detector, approval gate with
  four-eyes rule, atomic budgets, effect journal, RE2/J guardrails, hooks with signed
  webhooks, audit, Micrometer and OpenTelemetry observers), `-spring-ai` (governed
  `ToolCallback`s, `@Governed` scanning, `GovernanceAdvisor`), `-spring-boot-starter`
  (`vegaduta.agent.*` auto-configuration, optional approvals endpoint), `-jdbc`
  (durable stores for PostgreSQL and H2), `-testkit` (assertions) and `-bom`. Java 17,
  Spring AI 2.0.x, Spring Boot 4.x. Not yet published to Maven Central.
- Local LLM pool bootstrap: `app.local-llm.bootstrap.enabled=true` registers a local Ollama (or other OpenAI-compatible
  server) as the FREE-tier platform pool at startup with no manual API calls. It is idempotent, api shape only, never
  fails startup, and warns with the exact `ollama pull <model>` command when the model is missing. Pool rows with a blank
  key are now supported and send no `Authorization` header. Replaces the broken `deploy/seed/local-hosted-llm-provider-seed.*`
  (removed: it inserted a plaintext key core cannot decrypt). Unit-tested only, not run on a stack.
- Cloud KMS providers for envelope encryption (`APP_CRYPTO_KMS_PROVIDER=aws|azure|gcp`, new module `crypto-kms`):
  AWS KMS (`GenerateDataKey` / `Decrypt`), Azure Key Vault (`wrapKey` / `unwrapKey`, `DefaultAzureCredential`, JDK
  HTTP client) and Google Cloud KMS (REST over the JDK client, Application Default Credentials). `local` stays the
  default and behaves as before. Envelopes record which provider and key wrapped them, so a changed provider fails
  clearly; the local provider stays registered so older envelopes still decrypt. Only the payment signing keys use
  this path; the ~41 `EncryptionService` callers still use `APP_ENCRYPTION_KEY`. The Azure provider records the key version and wrap
  algorithm in each wrapped key and reuses its clients; the GCP endpoint must be https. Unit-tested with fakes; not
  run against a real KMS. Adds 3 artifacts, 1.83 MB, to the app runtime classpath (AWS `kms` 1.53 MB,
  `azure-security-keyvault-keys` 0.30 MB, the module jar), measured with `dependency:list`.
- Helm: per-cloud values (`deploy/helm/examples/values-aws.yaml`, `values-azure.yaml`, `values-gcp.yaml`) for the
  managed PostgreSQL, Redis, object storage and Kafka of each cloud, with IRSA and Azure Workload Identity ServiceAccount settings (GKE Workload Identity shown as
  an opt-in comment), and External Secrets Operator examples per cloud
  (`deploy/helm/examples/external-secrets/`) that produce the Secrets the chart reads. Rendered, `kubeconform`-checked
  and covered by `test-guards.sh`; not installed on a real cluster.
- Terraform: AWS reference modules `aws-network`, `aws-eks` (with IRSA OIDC provider), `aws-rds-postgres` (TLS-only,
  deletion-protected; no AGE, so `global.postgresql.age=false`), `aws-elasticache` (TLS + AUTH token),
  `aws-s3`, `aws-irsa-core` and `aws-secrets`, each with a "Wiring into the chart" section. The RDS master password
  is generated into Secrets Manager and not rotated by default (the chart reads it from an env var fixed at pod
  start); `aws-eks` turns on VPC CNI network policy enforcement for the chart's default-deny policies. Checked with
  `tofu fmt`/`validate` by hand only; not planned or applied against AWS.
- Azure Blob Storage backend (`APP_STORAGE_TYPE=azure-blob`): Azure has no S3 API, so it needed its own
  `FileStorageService`. It shares the tenant-isolation layer with S3 (`TenantScopedFileStorage`), uses the JDK HTTP
  client (no Netty), and with `STORAGE_CREDENTIALS=default` authenticates through `DefaultAzureCredential`
  (AKS workload identity, managed identity), storing no key. Helm: `global.objectStorage.type: azure-blob`.
  Integration-tested against the Azurite emulator; not yet against a real Azure account.
- Database login without a stored password (`DB_AUTH=aws-iam | azure-entra | gcp-iam`, Helm
  `global.postgresql.auth`): a fresh RDS IAM, Entra or Cloud SQL IAM token for every new connection, cached and
  refreshed before expiry, shared by Flyway, tenant schema migrations and JPA. TLS is mandatory in these modes.
  New module `dbauth`. The default (`password`) is unchanged, except that a missing or blank `POSTGRES_PASSWORD`
  now fails startup with an explicit message (enforced in code, tested with Spring Boot's DataSource
  auto-configuration loaded) instead of an unresolved placeholder. With a token mode the Helm NetworkPolicies add
  the egress it needs for core and the migrate Job (public 443, the Pod Identity agent or GKE metadata server /32).
  AWS IRSA works because the `sts` module is on the classpath. Unit-tested and Helm-rendered; not yet run against a
  real cloud database.
- Managed Redis and Kafka: `REDIS_SSL` and `REDIS_USERNAME` (Helm `global.valkey.tls` / `username`) for
  ElastiCache, Azure Cache for Redis and Memorystore, and `global.kafka.securityProtocol` / `saslMechanism` /
  `existingSecret` for SASL (Event Hubs, MSK SCRAM, Confluent Cloud). Defaults are unchanged. A test binds the real
  application YAML against fake environments, so a wrong variable name fails the build.
- `STORAGE_CREDENTIALS=default` (Helm `global.objectStorage.credentials: default`): the S3 client can use the
  AWS SDK default credential chain (EKS IRSA / Pod Identity, ECS or EC2 role, `AWS_*` env), so AWS S3 needs no
  stored access keys. `static` (the key pair) stays the default; an unknown value fails startup.
- `deploy/kind/values-kind-observability.yaml`: Prometheus Operator scraping for the kind quickstart; the
  Kubernetes architecture page now records what was verified on a live cluster.
- Public legal pages: `/pricing`, `/refund-policy`, `/shipping-policy` and `/contact`
  (shared `LegalPage` layout), linked from the landing footer, the terms page and the
  sitemap. The three paid-plan pages hide when billing is disabled.
- Initial open-source baseline of the VegaDūta platform under Apache-2.0:
  - `core/`: Java 21 / Spring Boot modular server with agents, 14 LLM provider
    integrations (bring your own key), MCP client (stdio, SSE, Streamable HTTP)
    and MCP server, A2A, AG-UI, OpenAPI tool import, `SKILL.md` skills,
    workflows with human approval (in-process and Temporal engines),
    guardrails, audit log, RAG on pgvector, graph memory on Apache AGE,
    omnichannel adapters, and schema-per-tenant multi-tenancy.
  - `web/` tenant console and `web-admin/` platform-admin console (Next.js).
  - `services/`: ML sidecar, code intelligence, app builder, tool sandbox,
    MCP stdio runner, voice agent, RL trainer.
  - `deploy/compose`: Lite and Full tiers; `deploy/helm`: umbrella chart
    `vegaduta` and per-service charts; `scripts/vegaduta` CLI.
- Open-source project infrastructure: CI on every pull request and push
  (build, test, lint, shellcheck, hadolint, Compose and Helm validation),
  static gates, CodeQL, OpenSSF Scorecard, dependency review, Dependabot,
  a signed release pipeline (multi-arch images, cosign keyless signatures,
  SPDX SBOM and SLSA provenance attestations, OCI Helm charts), and community
  files (governance, security policy, contributing guide, AGENTS.md).
- Release pipeline hardening: releases only build commits whose `CI OK` and
  `CodeQL OK` checks passed; floating image tags (`latest`, `<major>.<minor>`)
  move only after every image, chart and the draft release succeeded, and never
  for pre-releases; release tarballs and `SHA256SUMS` are signed with cosign
  (Sigstore bundles plus `.sig`/`.pem`) and carry SLSA provenance
  (`.intoto.jsonl`); the source tarball pins `VEGADUTA_VERSION` to its release.
- A `DCO sign-off` check, dependency review, the static gates and a docs link
  check are part of the single required `CI OK` check; CodeQL runs on every
  pull request and reports as `CodeQL OK`.
- `NOTICE` and `LICENSES/` cover the bundled fonts (OFL-1.1), vendored
  JavaScript and third-party logos.
- Agents: import from OpenAI Assistants, the OpenAI Agents SDK and Flowise
  (preview, then draft); declarative lifecycle hooks; per-agent tool argument
  policies; lethal-trifecta enforcement (`off|warn|require-approval|block`);
  context budget planner and compaction; on-demand tool loading
  (`search_tools` / `call_tool`); scheduled recurring prompts with a
  `/scheduled` page; eight creative built-in skills; ten audience templates.
- Chat: resumable streams, edit / regenerate / branch switching, an artifacts
  side pane, import of ChatGPT and Claude chats and export as md/json/txt.
- Browser agent v2: domain allow/deny, encrypted saved logins, server-side
  TOTP, recorded and replayable recipes, and human takeover of a session.
- MCP: one-click provider OAuth and generic remote-MCP OAuth (discovery,
  DCR/CIMD, PKCE, refresh); MCP Apps (`ui://` resources); agents and
  workflows exposed as MCP tools; Activepieces community pieces as MCP tools
  through the new `services/piece-host` and an opt-in Compose overlay.
- Knowledge: parent-child chunking, tunable RRF/MMR fusion, query rewriting
  and a retrieval eval endpoint. Web search providers (SearXNG, Tavily, Exa,
  Serper) with a fallback chain, plus an optional self-hosted SearXNG overlay
  (`deploy/compose/docker-compose.search.yml`).
- Workflows: Data Tables (service, REST API, agent tools, `data_table` node,
  `/data-tables` console with CSV import), AI nodes (question classifier,
  parameter extractor, variable aggregator, document extractor), hosted form
  trigger, Wait node, error strategies, and workflow import/export.
- Evals and quality: stored judge-calibration sets, trajectory evals scored
  with pass^k, and repeated CI gate runs.
- Audit: signed, hash-chained audit seals with verify and export endpoints,
  and an offline verifier (`scripts/verify-audit-export.py`).
- Operations: multi-replica mode with a startup safety check, advisory-locked
  scheduled sweeps and agent-job ownership leases; in-house circuit breakers,
  bulkheads and a graceful-shutdown drain; dependency health and SLO
  endpoints with a Reliability page and a North Star metrics page in the
  admin console; opt-in tail sampling and reasoning-token accounting.
- API: a generated OpenAPI 3.1 contract (`docs/api/openapi.json`) and
  generated TypeScript and Python SDKs (`sdk/`); Open Responses
  `POST /v1/responses` on the OpenAI-compatible endpoint.
- `scripts/ci/boot-check.sh`: boots the real core jar against a throwaway
  Postgres (pgvector + AGE) and Redis and probes readiness.
- New features ship behind flags that default to off; see
  docs/configuration.md.

### Changed

- **Audit outcomes shared with the Agent SDKs**: core's `AuditOutcome` gains `DENIED`, `EXPIRED`, `REPLAYED`, `BUDGET_EXCEEDED` and `WARNING` (the SDKs' vocabulary), so audit records from an SDK-governed app validate against `contracts/outbound/audit-event.schema.json`; the Python Agent SDK gains `WARNING`, which the Java SDK already had. `/api/audit-log/counts` reports the new outcomes (zero until something records them), and `AuditOutcomeAlignmentTest` pins core, both SDKs and the schema together.
- **File-size cap of 700 lines**: a source file over 665 lines (within 5% of the cap) fails `make gates` unless baselined, and a PR may not add paths to the baseline. Wave 1 split 176 files; the baseline is down from 220 to 42 entries.
- **core/agents decomposition (ADR-0023)**: agent jobs, schedules, scheduled tasks and research runs moved to the new module `core/agents-jobs`; per-user budgets and spend caps to `core/agents-budget` (root-package budget classes are now in `com.agenticai.core.agents.budget`); chat import/export to `core/agents-chatport`. No behaviour, bean name, REST or configuration change.
- **Agent tool layer moved to `core/agents-tools` (ADR-0023 step 6, first half)**: `AgentToolProvider`, the tool resolvers and sources, the tool guard and governance chain, MCP connections, the MCP registry, MCP Apps and review, the built-in tools and data tables (207 files) now build in their own module; packages are unchanged. The kernel and the turn path call tools through the new `AgentToolPort` and `ToolReplaySafety` ports in `agents`. Bean names, entity names, tables, REST paths and configuration keys are unchanged.
- **Chat turn path moved to `core/agents-turn` (ADR-0023 step 6, second half)**: `AgentChatService` and the turn pipeline, memory and user memory, context compaction, hooks, the turn journal, resumable streams, branching, decisions, assist, skills (with the built-in `SKILL.md` resources), experiments, vision sessions and public agent shares (284 files) now build in their own module; packages are unchanged and the turn still runs in-process. `core/agents-tools` depends on it; the turn reaches tools only through `AgentToolPort`. `POST /api/agents/{agentId}/chat` is served by the new `AgentChatStreamController` (published in the API spec as `AgentController`, so the operation id and SDK method are unchanged). The `core/agents` kernel is down to 255 main files and off the module-size baseline. Bean names, entity names, tables, REST paths and configuration keys are unchanged.
- **Root package of `core/agents` emptied (ADR-0023 step 5)**: the 163 classes of `com.agenticai.core.agents` moved to named packages (`agents.core` for the `Agent` entity and its services, `agents.turn` for the chat turn path, plus `modality`, `hosted`, `quota`, `guardrail`, `runbudget`, `edge`, `usermemory`, `turn.quality`, `turn.cache`). Code that imports these classes needs the new package; bean names, entity names, tables, REST paths and configuration keys are unchanged.
- **ADR-0011 vendor extraction**: vendor agent tools (ElevenLabs, Sarvam, Google Workspace, image/video generation, UPI mandate payments) moved into their vendor modules; `core/agents` no longer depends on any vendor module.
- **Tenant-aware executors (ADR-0024)**: every thread, pool and scheduler in core comes from `TenantAwareExecutors` / `TenantAwareSchedulers`, which carry the tenant, agent-version pin and MDC to the worker. `@Async` methods now run with the caller's tenant. `RawExecutorBanRulesTest` fails the build on raw `Executors.*`, `new Thread`, pool constructors and similar.
- **Explicit turn context (ADR-0022, proposed)**: stage 1 adds the `TurnContext` record, built at the chat turn entry; the ThreadLocal stays for now.
- **Spring wiring checks run in `mvn test`**: `InjectableConstructorTest` and `UniqueBeanNamesTest` (core/app) replace `scripts/ci/spring-constructor-lint.sh` and `scripts/ci/bean-name-collision.sh`.

- **Large files split by design pattern** (behaviour unchanged): `WorkflowNodeExecutor` is a strategy registry of `NodeHandler`s (28 -> 3 constructor parameters); `WorkflowExecutionEngine` and the Temporal workflow share pure traversal rules and container steps (replay tests over six recorded histories); `AgentService` is a facade over focused services and `Agent` groups fields into embeddable value objects; `WorkflowService` is a facade over six services; `AgentToolProvider` is a composite of `ToolSource`s; `AgentChatService` takes a `ChatTurnRequest` and constructor-injected facades; `HttpRequestExecutor` is a pipeline of stages; `LlmMetricsAdvisor` publishes one observation to independent sinks; `DynamicMcpConnectionManager` is split into lifecycle, transport factory, guard chain, tool cache and health monitor; plus the data-ops, AI node, eval, brief, browser, coding, voice, erasure, memory and sidecar classes. On the web side `AgentWorkbench` (4,894 -> 86 lines), `AgentPlayground`, `WorkflowCanvas`, `NodeConfigDrawer`, `AppShell`, `CallPanel`, `StudioSurface`, `ChannelsPanel`, the edge libraries, templates and API types, and the tour were split into hooks, registries and data modules. Files over 1,000 lines: 78 -> about 25 ([docs/engineering/BIG-FILES-SPLIT-PLAN.md](docs/engineering/BIG-FILES-SPLIT-PLAN.md)).
- **Tenant config APIs reject bad input with a clear 4xx (stage CORE-4).** `PUT /api/tenant/ai-model-preferences/{featureKey}` returns 400 listing the allowed keys instead of storing an orphan row (clearing still accepts any key); knowledge collection names are capped at 255 characters, chunk size/overlap and search `topK` are range-checked; a duplicate-content upload returns 409 (`knowledge-duplicate-document`) naming the file only, and the non-empty-collection 409 no longer quotes an endpoint path; rotating a secret keeps the new description; purging an unknown or already purged secret is a 404; an unknown provider name on `/api/providers` lists the allowed values. Unit-tested; not run on a stack.
