- **Stage E2E batch CORE-5 (small core fixes).** `GET /api/tutor/status` and `GET /api/browser/takeover/capabilities` answer 200 `{"enabled":false}` instead of a 404 when the feature is off; the "RL policy ... has no selectable arms" warning is logged at most once an hour per agent instead of once per turn; a background job whose model server URL is refused by the SSRF guard fails at once with code `MODEL_URL_NOT_ALLOWED` and is never retried; Telegram Bot Token validation gives up after 4 s with a clear message; `DELETE /api/agents/{id}/schedule` is documented as a pause and `?remove=true` deletes the schedule and its run history (204); JSON responses are gzip-compressed (`APP_SERVER_COMPRESSION_ENABLED`, SSE excluded); `GET /api/skills/registry/search` answers are cached for 60 s; the edge manifest's `rife-video-interpolation` entry carries its `sha256`; an unauthenticated request now gets a problem+json 401 body instead of an empty one. Unit-tested; not run on a stack.
- **Console shell: calmer first load (stage E2E SE-01, 18, 36, 37, 72).** The welcome modal, product tour and two-factor catch-up no longer arm while the person is typing, a turn is in flight, or on a workflow/build/playground/agent editing surface (`web/app/lib/overlayGate.ts`; tests can pre-seed `vd_tour_off=1` or the per-user `vd_tour_status:<sub>` / `vd_tour_seen_security:<sub>` keys); the two-factor note is a non-modal card at the bottom right; Escape closes the command palette before the tour. A sign-in that never finishes now ends in an error with Retry and the Keycloak endpoint instead of an endless "Signing you in" splash. `/api/public/status` is coalesced and backed off after failures; `/api/system/status` and the pending approvals are requested once per page load via a shared poll; sidebar links no longer prefetch; the 359 KB nav logo is a 24 KB WebP. Unit-tested; not run on the stage.
- **Deploy defaults no longer bake `localhost` into the browser bundle.** Compose defaults `PUBLIC_CODE_INTEL_URL` to empty (the web app then uses the core proxy), `NEXT_PUBLIC_ACADEMY_URL` and `NEXT_PUBLIC_CONTACT_EMAIL` are configurable (`WEB_ACADEMY_URL`, `WEB_CONTACT_EMAIL`, `web.env.*` in Helm), and the core chart has `core.resumableStreamsEnabled` for `APP_CHAT_RESUMABLE_STREAMS_ENABLED` (the redis store alone never turned resumable streams on). The kind guide documents the stale-realm sign-out patch. Rendered and linted; not run on a stack.
- **Java Agent SDK hardening (review pass over the governance gaps batch).** Tamper-evident audit: attributes are hashed under `attr.<key>` so none can shadow a field, a throwing signer leaves a contiguous chain (`chain.sigError`), the chain can resume from a stored head (`tamperEvidentAudit(signer, lastSeq, lastHash)`), the delegate is called outside the chain lock, `EvidenceBundle` verifies the whole chain before the period filter and counts unchained events, and `UnderwritingReport` no longer calls an unsigned chain "verified". Fail-closed fixes: a malformed `vegaduta.autonomy` attribute resolves to L1 and is audited; CRITICAL tools always need approval; a promotion the audit sink rejects is rolled back; an approval granted after a halt does not run; a fail-closed `ApprovalNotifier` really stops the request; concurrent approvals are compare-and-set; `MandateGate` checks a root digest and the mandate's subject; `ProvenanceGate` denies on supplier/store errors, notices a pin written later, and `Governance.provenance(...)` no longer trusts on first use in ENFORCE; a reconciler now covers non-idempotent side effects and the JDBC journal answers interrupted keys after a restart; governed memory reserves write slots atomically, binds promotion to the reviewed entry and governs deletes. `HaltStore` SPI for multi-replica kill switches, `Governance.provenanceGate()` / `reconcilingJournal()`, `GovernedTool` audit events carry a `stage` attribute (replay ignores guardrail/hook denials and folds approved executions), and the scanner refuses to score a jar in which it found no tools (Spring Boot `BOOT-INF/classes/` is now read). Reports escape names and bound free text; showback CSV neutralises formulas.
- The local-LLM bootstrap's default model is `qwen3.5:4b` everywhere (Java default, Compose pass-through, `.env.example`); it was `llama3.2` in those three places while the CLI pulled `qwen3.5:4b`.
- **Java Agent SDK governance gaps closed (all `@Experimental`).** Tamper-evident audit (`HashChainedAuditSink`, HMAC-SHA256/Ed25519 signers) with EU AI Act, OWASP ASI and underwriting evidence exporters (`io.vegaduta.agent.core.evidence`); tiered autonomy (`AutonomyLevel`, `AutonomyRegistry`, `AutonomyGate`); policy replay and shadow evaluation (`PolicyReplay`, `ShadowGate`, `ShadowReport`, `PolicyDiff`); delegated mandates (`Mandate`, `Mandates`, `MandateGate`); kill switch and drill (`HaltSwitch`, `HaltGate`, `HaltDrill`); tool supply-chain pinning and drift detection (`ToolManifest`, `ProvenanceGate`, `FileManifestPinStore`); cost attribution and showback (`CostModel`, `CostLedger`, `CostAwareBudgetProvider`, `ShowbackReport`); governed long-term memory (`GovernedMemoryStore`, `MemoryToolHook`, `PoisonProbe`); interrupted-effect reconciliation (`ReconcilingEffectJournal`, `EffectReconciler`; `GovernedTool` fails closed with `effect_unresolved` under `UnknownEffectPolicy.HOLD`); approval notifications to Slack/Teams and signed decision receipts (`ApprovalNotifier`, `ChatApprovalHandler`, `ReceiptAuditSink`, `ApprovalReceipt`); record/replay traces in the test kit (`io.vegaduta.agent.testkit.trace`: `TraceRecorder`, `ReplayTool`, `TraceAssert`, `TraceDiff`). `Governance.Builder` gains one-call wiring for each (`haltSwitch`, `mandates`, `autonomy`, `provenance`, `shadow`, `costModel`, `reconciler`, `tamperEvidentAudit`, `approvalNotifier`, `receipts`, `memory`); safety gates compose through the new `CompositeToolDispatchGate` in the order halt, mandate, autonomy, provenance, policy.
- **`vegaduta-agent-scanner`**, a zero-integration risk-map scanner and CLI for the Java Agent SDK (tools, classification, approval coverage, lethal trifecta, OWASP ASI exposure, readiness score, `--fail-under` CI gate).
- **Refund-agent Java example** (`examples/sdk/refund-agent/java`, `WithSdk`) now demotes the support agent on an `AutonomyRegistry` before the run, writes the audit trail through a signed `HashChainedAuditSink`, and ends by verifying the chain and printing the OWASP ASI coverage report.
- **Consolidated security scan reporting.** Every scanner's findings now land in GitHub code scanning (Security → Code scanning) and, optionally, a DefectDojo dashboard: new `trivy.yml` workflow (fs scan: dependency CVEs + IaC misconfig as SARIF/JSON), new `zap.yml` workflow (weekly OWASP ZAP baseline against the Lite compose stack, with `scripts/ci/zap-to-sarif.py` converting the report for code scanning), new `license-scan.yml` workflow (ScanCode on the vendored trees, gated by `scripts/ci/license-drift-check.py` + `license-allowlist.txt`), and the release grype scan now also emits SARIF per image. `scripts/ci/defectdojo-import.sh` pushes each report to a DefectDojo API when `vars.DEFECTDOJO_URL` + `secrets.DEFECTDOJO_API_KEY` are set (skipped otherwise), and `deploy/compose/defectdojo.yml` runs a local DefectDojo stack. See [Scan reports and dashboards](docs/security/scanning.md).
- **ADR-0018 Ad Studio (proposed) and its research.** A design for extending Studio into a complete ad-creative platform as separate services: real-person photos with consent, masking, virtual try-on, photoreal characters, ad images and video, a provider-agnostic `MediaProvider` SPI, a tiered browser, CPU, serverless GPU and premium API router, a likeness and consent gate, fidelity gates, and two-layer provenance. Design only; nothing is built or measured. See `docs/architecture/ADR-0018-ad-studio.md` and `docs/architecture/ad-studio-research-2026-10.md`.
- **Auth0 user-admin adapter** (`Auth0UserAdminClient`, selected by `app.identity.provider=auth0` / `IDENTITY_PROVIDER`) implementing `IdentityUserAdminPort` over the Auth0 Management API: cached client_credentials token, Lucene `app_metadata.tenant_id` tenant scoping, role diffing via role ids, `blocked` for enable/disable, `DELETE .../sessions` + `.../refresh-tokens` for logout, and emailed password resets (`/dbconnections/change_password`) for invites and resets. `app.identity.auth0.*` config (`AUTH0_DOMAIN`, `AUTH0_CLIENT_ID`, `AUTH0_CLIENT_SECRET`, `AUTH0_CONNECTION`, `AUTH0_AUDIENCE`). Keycloak stays the default (`app.identity.provider=keycloak` or unset); per-tenant SSO and social-IdP surfaces (`IdentityProviderAdminPort`) remain Keycloak-only and 404 under Auth0. See [Extending](docs/extending.md#7-add-an-identity-provider-adapter).
- **Python Agent SDK (`sdk/python-agent`, package `vegaduta-agent`).** The governance layer of the Java Agent SDK for Python applications: `@governed` tool classification (`RiskLabel`, OWASP `AsiRisk`), a dispatch gate, human approval gate with resumptions, per-run budgets, an idempotent effect journal (in-memory or SQLite, stdlib-only), secret-redaction and prompt-injection guardrails on tool arguments and output, lifecycle hooks, `key=value` audit records on the `vegaduta.agent.audit` logger, and `GovernanceMode.OBSERVE` for a measure-first rollout, and `governance_report`, which renders one run's audit events and approval records as a Markdown/JSON governance summary (per-tool risk and outcomes, approver, OWASP ASI coverage) — the artifact a security review asks for. Zero runtime dependencies; optional `[langchain]` and `[otel]` extras. 32 unit tests.
- **Configurable OIDC claim mapping** (`app.security.oidc.roles-claim-path`, `tenant-claim`, `scopes-claim`) so the resource server works with Auth0, Microsoft Entra, Okta and other issuers, not only Keycloak-shaped tokens. Defaults keep the current Keycloak claim names exactly; a missing tenant claim still fails closed. `JwtClaimPaths` resolves dot-paths (`realm_access.roles`) and literal namespaced claims (`https://example.com/roles`). See [Configuration](docs/configuration.md#authentication-oidc-and-keycloak) and the updated adapter checklist in [Extending](docs/extending.md#7-add-an-identity-provider-adapter).
- **Standards conformance table generated from source** (`scripts/ci/conformance-report.py` → [docs/api/conformance.md](docs/api/conformance.md)): MCP client and server, A2A, AG-UI and OpenTelemetry capabilities, each row backed by a grep-checked code location, with `--check` in the static gates so the table cannot drift from the code.
- **Billing gateway documentation and tests.** `deploy/compose/core.env.example` documents the core-passthrough variables (Stripe, Razorpay, PhonePe keys, `DEFAULT_PAYMENT_GATEWAY`, Google OAuth, Vertex, Temporal); `docs/configuration.md` gained a "Billing gateways" section; `StripeGatewayClientTest` covers the HMAC signature scheme (valid, wrong, tampered, replay-window, empty-secret fail-closed), the invoice/subscription event mapping and the configured-off fail-fast.
- **`invoke_agent` and `execute_tool` OpenTelemetry spans** were already implemented (`AgentInvocationObservation`, `GenAiToolObservationConvention`); README and ROADMAP now say so.
- **Refund-agent example for the Agent SDKs** (`examples/sdk/refund-agent/`, Python and Java): in each language two programs replay the same scripted support-agent turns — without the SDK (plain tool dispatch) and with it (`@governed`/`@Governed` tools through the `Governance` pipeline) — over a fake shop backend, so a demo needs no API keys or model. The run shows a prompt-injected $9,999 refund rejected at the approval gate, a retried store credit replayed once from the effect journal, a pasted token redacted from tool output, a drifting-args loop stopped by the run budget, and the `key=value` audit trail a review would ask for.
- **HTTP polling triggers for workflows** (`PUT/GET/DELETE /api/workflows/{id}/nodes/{nodeId}/poll-trigger`, canvas "HTTP polling" section): a Trigger node now polls an http(s) endpoint on an interval, extracts the item list via a dot path (`itemsPath`, blank = the response itself), dedupes per item id (`idPath`, default `id`, content-hash fallback) on a persistent seen-id ring, and starts a run per new item (capped at `app.workflow.poll.max-items-per-poll`, default 25). First poll seeds the backlog without firing (`seedOnEnable`, default true). URLs pass the SSRF guard at save time and at connect time, redirects are never followed so configured headers cannot leak, and failures back off linearly (`app.workflow.poll.*`, see [Configuration](docs/configuration.md#workflows)). Same governance as schedules: workflow must be ACTIVE and the tenant plan must allow unattended automation.
- **Webhook sender-signature verification** (`PUT/GET/DELETE .../webhook/signature`, canvas "Sender signature" section): a second factor on the shared-secret webhook URL — GitHub (`X-Hub-Signature-256`), Stripe (`Stripe-Signature` with 5-minute replay window), Slack (`X-Slack-Signature` + timestamp) and a generic `X-Signature-256` HMAC-SHA256 preset are verified against a per-node secret stored AES-256-GCM and never readable back. Off by default (`NONE`); unsigned/forged deliveries get a 401 before a run starts.
- **`WorkflowTriggerConfigService`** now owns all per-node trigger configuration (webhook, channel binding, signature, polling); `WorkflowService` keeps delegating methods so existing callers are unchanged.
- **Tenant REST API keys for headless automation** (`/api/tenant/api-keys`, console **Administration → API keys**): a tenant admin can mint a `vdak_` bearer key a CI pipeline, partner system or script uses instead of an OIDC login — `Authorization: Bearer vdak_...` authenticates through the normal security chain (`ApiKeyAuthFilter` → `PERM_*` authorities), so every existing permission check applies unchanged. Scopes are the permission catalog and are validated as a subset of the issuer's own effective permissions — a key can never carry a scope its issuer didn't hold. Keys support an optional expiry and rotation; every call is audit-attributable to the synthetic `apikey:<name>` subject, never the minting user. Secrets are SHA-256-hashed at rest (never stored readable) and a key with no scopes can still execute (RUNNER-level), which is the "trigger this workflow from CI" case. All routes require `PERM_MANAGE_USERS`; issuance additionally requires the developer-API plan entitlement.
- **`Idempotency-Key` on `POST /api/workflows/{id}/run`**: a headless caller retrying after a timeout can send a stable key; a replay within the dedup window gets a 409 instead of starting a second run. Scoped to (workflow, caller, key).
- **CI runs automatically again.** `ci.yml` (pull requests and pushes to `main`), `codeql.yml` (PRs, pushes, weekly), `docs.yml` (doc-affecting paths), `e2e.yml` (nightly), `labeler.yml` (on PR events), `scorecard.yml` (weekly + pushes) and `stale.yml` (daily) had been switched to manual-only; triggers are restored. The heavyweight suites stay off the per-PR path: e2e is nightly, release and sdk-release remain tag-dispatched.
- **The two consoles are one app.** `web-admin/` merged into `web/` under `app/platform-admin/` (was ~150 files); there is now a single Next.js console and a single `web` image/deployment. The `admin.<domain>` host still works: the web ingress binds it (`ingress.extraHostKeys`, new in vegaduta-lib) and `proxy.js` redirects its paths under `/platform-admin` (`PLATFORM_ADMIN_HOST`), and the `agentic-ai-web` Keycloak client gained the admin host's redirect URIs. `global.components.webAdmin` is now inert (kept for values compatibility), the web-admin chart/service/job/image matrix entries are gone, and `scripts/ci/console-dup-check.sh` is retired. Local stack: the platform-admin console moves from `http://localhost:3001` to `http://localhost:3000/platform-admin`; `PUBLIC_ADMIN_URL` still exists for the admin host in domain mode.
- **Console CSS de-branded**: `rilo-*.css` files renamed to `tokens.css`/`public.css`/`console.css`/`auth.css` and the `rilo-` class prefix renamed to `vd-` across `web/app` (54 files); no functional change.
- Merged the standalone core/eval module into core/quality (package com.agenticai.core.quality.eval); there is now one eval surface.
- **No Kafka configured means no Kafka client.** With the `workflow` profile on and no broker every core pod logged connection errors to `localhost:9092` from the topic admin, the outbox relay and three consumer threads. Core now decides at start-up: when `WORKFLOW_KAFKA_ENABLED` is not set, Kafka is on exactly when a bootstrap server resolved to a non-blank value from any source (`WORKFLOW_KAFKA_BOOTSTRAP_SERVERS`, `SPRING_KAFKA_BOOTSTRAP_SERVERS`, a key in the app Secret, `extraEnvFromSecrets`, `envFrom`); with none it creates no topic admin, relay or consumers, writes no workflow event to the outbox, and logs one INFO line (`WORKFLOW_KAFKA_ENABLED=false` forces it off, `true` forces it on). **Behaviour change:** the profile no longer defaults the bootstrap to `localhost:9092`, so a local run that relied on that must set `WORKFLOW_KAFKA_BOOTSTRAP_SERVERS` (Compose and `core/.env.example` already do). The Helm chart emits no Kafka switch at all (it cannot read Secrets), only `WORKFLOW_KAFKA_BOOTSTRAP_SERVERS` from `global.kafka.bootstrapServers`. The one-shot migrate process no longer starts the Kafka pieces (it skips Kafka's 30 s topic-creation timeout, observed in a local run, not on a cluster). Verified by Spring property/context tests and Helm render tests; no image was built or run against a broker or a cluster here. See [Configuration](docs/configuration.md).
- Tenant deletion no longer uses the blocking Redis `KEYS` command: the purge makes one cursor-based `SCAN` pass over
  `agentic:*` (batched deletes, cursor always closed), visits every master node when the connection is a Redis Cluster
  (unit-tested with mocks only), and reports a mid-scan failure as a partial purge after deleting what it had
  collected. Integration-tested against Valkey on a single node. Docs: new "Redis Cluster / Sentinel readiness" audit
  in the [cloud portability ADR](docs/architecture/ADR-cloud-portability.md). Redis Cluster is still not supported: 2
  known blockers (two cross-slot Lua scripts) and 5 template `SCAN` sites with unverified cluster behaviour are listed there.
- Identity administration now goes through `IdentityUserAdminPort` and `IdentityProviderAdminPort`; `KeycloakAdminClient`
  is the only implementation and no other class depends on it (ArchUnit rule). No behaviour change. See
  [extending](docs/extending.md#7-add-an-identity-provider-adapter).
- Apache AGE is now optional on the database server, so core runs on managed PostgreSQL services that do
  not offer it (AWS RDS/Aurora, Cloud SQL, AlloyDB). When the server refuses the `age` extension, migration
  V44 (graph memory) is recorded as applied without running, at startup and for every tenant schema, and a
  warning says graph memory is unavailable; any other failure still stops the migration. Helm:
  `global.postgresql.age=false` also drops the `graph-memory` profile.
- `AgentChatService.chat()` (one 1,150-line method) is now a short
  orchestration over per-stage collaborators (pre-flight and refusals, RL arm
  selection, LLM client selection, prompt assembly, dispatch and stream
  assembly). Moved verbatim, no behaviour change; the public API and
  constructor are unchanged.
- Core refactors: the chat-session endpoints moved from `AgentController` to
  `AgentChatSessionController` and `GlobalExceptionHandler` became an ordered
  chain of advice classes (paths, status codes and bodies unchanged);
  `OpenApiSpecGenerator` was split and the generated `docs/api/openapi.json`
  now carries descriptions for the transcript, export, rewind, cancel and
  branching operations (who may read a session, which flag each needs).
- No localhost defaults remain for the token issuer (`KEYCLOAK_ISSUER_URI`),
  `app.web.public-url` (`PUBLIC_APP_URL`) or the OAuth console redirects:
  under the `security` profile a missing value fails startup with the
  variable name instead of silently pointing emails and redirects at
  localhost.
- Consoles: `web/app/lib/auth.ts` split under the file-size ceiling; the
  duplicated web/web-admin lib files are reconciled and a gate
  (`scripts/ci/console-dup-check.sh`) fails on drift; hardcoded colours in
  the brand modal, channels, contacts, conversion and admin error pages use
  design tokens, with a lint warning for new literals; type-aware lint covers
  test files; `npm run test:coverage` in both apps; one Playwright config.
- CI: release images are scanned (grype) before signing, release builds no
  longer use the shared Actions cache, and a job checks the Python
  `requirements.lock` files against their sources.
- Inbound Slack, Telegram, Discord, Teams, Messenger and Instagram messages
  are acknowledged immediately and answered asynchronously, like WhatsApp
  and Twilio already were; the webhook no longer holds a request thread for
  the whole agent turn (Slack retried after 3 s because of it).
- Major version bumps in the Compose stack: Prometheus 3.15, Grafana 13.2,
  Temporal 1.29.7 (UI 2.55), Redpanda 26.2, Docker-in-Docker 29.9, SeaweedFS
  4.48 (Compose and Helm), Langfuse 4.56 with ClickHouse 26.8, and the Azure
  phase-1 Keycloak image to 26.7. Operator notes: **Langfuse** - a fresh
  install needs nothing; an existing v3 install must first upgrade ClickHouse
  to >= 25.12 while still on Langfuse v3 (`docker compose up -d
  langfuse-clickhouse` with the new tag), then start v4, which migrates its
  schema automatically and backfills history in the background (the worker's
  new healthcheck reports 503 while event propagation is stuck). Core's OTLP
  export is the v4-native ingestion path. **Prometheus 3** keeps the flags and
  config this stack uses; `.` in PromQL regexes now matches newlines.
  **Redpanda 26.2** turns on schema-registry qualified subjects (subjects
  named `:.…` are reinterpreted); this stack registers none. **Grafana 13**
  no longer runs AngularJS panels; the shipped dashboards use none.
- Every base image in the Dockerfiles is pinned by digest (Maven, Temurin
  JRE/JDK, Python 3.12.15, Node 22.23.3 / 24.21.0, the e2b template, the
  CloudNativePG base used by the release build). The core-full image copies
  node/npm from the pinned Node image and uv/uvx from astral's pinned image
  instead of `curl | sh` from NodeSource and astral.sh; the mcp-stdio-runner
  does the same for uv. Playwright's browser installer is pinned to the
  version `@playwright/mcp@0.0.79` depends on.
- Third-party versions bumped: Keycloak 26.7.5, Valkey 8.1.10, Postgres
  16.15 / 17.11, PgBouncer 1.26.0, Caddy 2.11.7, Loki 3.6.17, Promtail 3.6.11,
  Alertmanager 0.28.1, Grafana 11.6.16, redis_exporter 1.93.0, LiveKit
  1.13.9, ClickHouse 24.3.18.7 (full patch pin), docker-socket-proxy 0.5.0,
  SearXNG 2026.10.9; Helm: Valkey and SeaweedFS aligned with Compose, curl
  8.22.0, and the example helmfile moves to ingress-nginx 4.13.9 (the 4.11
  line has CVE-2025-1974), cert-manager 1.20.4 and CloudNativePG 0.29.1.
  Not bumped (majors that need a stack run first): Temporal, Redpanda,
  Langfuse 4, Prometheus 3, Docker-in-Docker 29, Grafana 12+, SeaweedFS 4,
  the Azure phase-1 Keycloak image.
- `make gates` (and the static-gates CI job) now fail when the generated
  core module graph or the SDKs under `sdk/` drift from their sources.
- Compose passes `APP_SECURITY_OUTBOUND_ALLOWED_PRIVATE_HOSTS` to core, so a
  self-hosted Ollama on the Compose network can be allow-listed from `.env`.
- Dependabot also covers `services/piece-host` (npm, docker), `sdk/python`
  and `sdk/typescript`; service `.dockerignore` files exclude `.env*`, `*.pem`
  and `*.key`.
- The web and web-admin builds no longer download fonts from Google: the
  fonts are committed (OFL, subset to the scripts the apps use), so the
  images also build without internet access to fonts.gstatic.com.
- The web build no longer downloads `@babel/standalone` from jsDelivr: it is
  a pinned dev dependency, verified by the npm lockfile's integrity hash.
- Billing: a gateway-neutral checkout API (Razorpay, Stripe); plan-limit
  messages name the plan; signup trial length and plan are platform settings.
- `infra/terraform/modules/cloudflare-dns`: SPF senders, DMARC policy and
  report address are inputs (`spf_includes`, `dmarc_policy`, `dmarc_rua`);
  adds CAA and TLS-RPT records. Defaults stay strict (`v=spf1 -all`,
  `p=reject`).
- Upstream failures answer 503 instead of 502.

### Fixed

- `docs/api/openapi.json` documents the success status a handler really sends when it builds its own `ResponseEntity` (`created`, `accepted`, `noContent`, `status(HttpStatus.X)`), read from the controller bytecode, instead of 200; the generated TypeScript and Python SDKs follow.
- Web console versus API drift (ADR-0025 `contracts/consumers/web/drift.json` is now empty): the custom code tool "Test" button sent its sample as `sampleInput`, which the server ignored, so every dry run ran with no input; it now sends `input`. `POST /api/knowledge/upload` now also returns `documentId` (additive), so a face enrollment made from an uploaded photo is linked to its knowledge document (the console read a non-existent `id` and sent none before). The console's response types for share revoke, DTMF menu PUT/DELETE, version restore, research run list and session LLM usage now match what the server sends.
- The 12 A2A REST mutating routes now have explicit authorization rules (`AuthorizationCoverageTest` is green again); Compose passes `APP_SECURITY_OUTBOUND_EGRESS_ALLOW_LIST` to core; the seeded demo's ROI is plausible; the workflow access-level probe returns VIEWER for a VIEWER grant; golden JSON for agent and workflow-run responses updated (additive); `BriefReadinessServiceTest` and `AgentGenerationServiceTest` no longer assert behaviour the SMS channel and the WhatsApp policy guard changed; the `safeLink` test is fixed.
- Resolution meter: the `/api/tenant/resolutions` endpoints and the hourly resolution sweep ran their `JdbcTemplate` statements outside a transaction, so they read and wrote the default schema instead of the tenant's (lists empty, recorded events in the wrong schema, the sweep finding no sessions). They now run in a transaction. Also fixes a core startup failure from `WhatsAppSendGuard` having two constructors and no `@Autowired`.
- **Workflow module batch (stage e2e CORE-2).** A workflow schedule tighter than `app.workflow.schedule.min-interval-seconds` (default 60; `* * * * * *` fired every second) is now a 400. A 400-character workflow name is a 400 instead of a 500 (`name` is capped at 200). The Run modal's "Run comment" has its own `comment` field on `POST /api/workflows/{id}/run`, stored on the run (migration V490) and returned in run history, instead of being appended to the node input; the console still concatenates until its call site is changed. Run responses carry `warningCount` (nodes that degraded to SKIPPED while the run completed) and, on an accepted cancel of a RUNNING run, `cancelRequested: true`. Cancel, pause and resume on a COMPLETED, FAILED or CANCELLED run answer 409 with the status instead of 200. `PATCH /execution-limits` accepts any subset of its fields. The max-steps ERROR trace row names the node about to run, an auto-approving approval node names the action in its summary, and a loop or fan-out fed a non-list logs a warning. A cancelled LLM stream is recorded with the thread's interrupt flag cleared, so the outcome row is no longer lost to `Could not open JPA EntityManager`.
- **A platform-admin account with no organization gets a 403, not a 500, from `/api/tenant/**`.** Such an account (for example one that also holds `tenant-admin`) opening the tenant console made `GET /api/tenant/settings` and `/api/tenant/usage-quota` fail with "No TenantContext ... SecurityConfig should have blocked this" on every poll. `TenantContextFilter` still lets tenant-less platform admins through everywhere else (`/api/platform/**`, `/api/me`), but now answers 403 on the tenant-scoped `/api/tenant/**` surface.
- **`/sitemap.xml`, `/robots.txt` and `/llms.txt` no longer advertise `http://localhost:3000` on a runtime-configured image.** They were prerendered at build time, freezing `SITE_URL`; they are now rendered per request. In `oss` site mode the sitemap also drops commercial routes that redirect to `/`. The Academy link has no `http://localhost:4100` default any more (hidden on `/docs` and the footer when `NEXT_PUBLIC_ACADEMY_URL` is unset, `/learn` elsewhere), and the Compose `NEXT_PUBLIC_CODE_INTEL_URL` build default is empty instead of `http://localhost:8090`. The prod E2E specs gain `E2E_SITE_MODE=oss` and nip.io-stage awareness, and use the shared `agentic-ai-web` client for the admin host. Unit-tested and typechecked; takes effect on a stage only after the web image is rebuilt and redeployed.
- **Workflow canvas: Run works on plain-http origins, Validate shows its findings, fewer empty versions.** Run no longer hangs on "Running..." where `crypto.randomUUID` does not exist (plain-http LAN installs): the run id comes from a shared `randomUuid()` with a `getRandomValues` fallback and is created inside the guarded block, so a failure resets the button and toasts. A failed Validate outlines the nodes its messages name. A finished branching run reads "4 of 5 steps (1 on branches not taken)" in the run inspector, and the inspector remembers open/closed per workflow. Validate and Run skip the draft save (and the new workflow version it created) when the canvas is unchanged. Unit-tested; not run on a stack.
- **Annotated and header errors are 4xx, not 500.** Exceptions carrying `@ResponseStatus` (eval run conflict 409, eval dataset/run, simulation and experiment not-found 404, code-intel 403) are answered with their declared status by the catch-all handler instead of "Something went wrong"; a missing required request header (`POST /api/devices/poll` without `X-Device-Token`) is a 400; and an unhandled exception on a request with no tenant is no longer followed by a failed `platform_issue` write (that table exists only in tenant schemas). Unit-tested; not run on a stack.
- **Share abuse reports and viewer counts are now saved, and every dead share link answers alike.** Three reports on an agent or workflow share left `reportCount` and `viewerCount` at 0 and the link live, because the counters were changed on a detached row. They are now applied to the row re-loaded under a lock, so the third report revokes the link. An unknown, revoked, expired or deleted share (agent, workflow or demo) now returns the same `410 {"error":"expired"}`; unknown agent and demo ids used to return 404. Unit-tested; not run on a stack.
- **Console lists show a new collection or file at once, and knowledge files can be deleted.** The web API layer cached or shared a list read that was on the wire while a write landed, so a created collection or uploaded file stayed hidden until reload (a write now drops in-flight reads, discards any read that started before it, and flushes again when it completes). Knowledge: a trash button with a second-click confirm on every file row; deleting a collection that still holds files says "Delete the N files in this collection first" instead of a raw 409 with ids and an API path (no duplicate toast); re-uploading identical content says "already uploaded as <name>". Fewer repeat requests: `POST /api/model-pricing` and the workflow import preview no longer empty the read cache, concurrent model-pricing and health probes share one call, capability/config reads stay fresh for two minutes, and the workflow-portability and judge-calibration 404 probes run once per browser session. Unit-tested; not run on a stack.
- **Playground and Quick Task composer limits.** The Playground composer now counts characters near the server cap, disables Send above 8,000 characters and says why, instead of sending and getting a 400. Quick Task starts on Log only when no phone number is on file, and a disabled "Get it done" says to add a number or choose Log only; the plan card states how an agent is auto-selected. Unit-tested; not run on the stage.
- **Console accessibility and small-page fixes (stage review WEB-4).** Every console route now sets its own `<title>` ("Page - VegaDuta"); `/usage`, `/admin/approvals` and `/admin/input-requests` have exactly one h1; the Sentinels, RAG parent-child and MCP exposure switches, the `/tools` search, the contacts import textarea, the logo file input, the `/quick-task` textarea, persona checkboxes, the workflow node Label input and the per-dataset Delete/Export buttons have accessible names; the theme popover is a labelled dialog; `/tools` no longer overflows at 768px and its "enabled / available" header matches the list total; `/capabilities` redirects to `/capabilities/decision`; Insights says "1 call"; the Channels header counts only channels that have a tile, `?channel=` stays in the URL and follows tile clicks, and the duplicate error/success alert is no longer announced twice; the Schedules "Writing requires a tenant admin." hint shows only to non-admins. A loopback `NEXT_PUBLIC_CODE_INTEL_URL` is ignored when the console is served from another host (the core proxy is used), the agent editor skips the code-intel repo call when code-intel is not enabled, and `/code-intel` says so instead of printing a localhost URL. Unit-tested and linted; not run on a stack.
- **Core starts again.** The HTTP poll trigger release (1cfa407da) stopped every core shape at startup: a `workflowService` / `workflowTriggerConfigService` constructor cycle and two poll beans with two constructors and no `@Autowired` ("No default constructor found"). The MCP `app.mcp.stdio-launcher-allowlist` setting is now actually applied; Spring had been building `McpStdioCommandGuard` with its no-arg constructor, so extra launchers were silently ignored. `InjectableConstructorTest` now also fails on that silent-fallback shape. Verified with `scripts/ci/boot-check.sh` and on the kind stage.
- **SonarQube remediation pass.** `TenantSchemaMigrator`/`PlatformMigrationService` now refuse schema names outside `^[a-z_][a-z0-9_]{0,62}$` before they are quoted into SQL (the dynamic-identifier path Sonar flags); the dev-only `local` coding-sandbox runner creates workspaces under an owner-only directory (`CODING_HARNESS_LOCAL_WORKSPACE_DIR`, default `~/.vegaduta/coding-sandbox`) instead of the shared system temp dir (java:S5443); `data-tables` column-name validation is aligned with the server grammar; and Sonar-visible naming/constant collisions (`ALLOW`/`catalog`/`total`) and a zero-width-space test literal were cleaned up, plus `@SuppressWarnings("java:S2187")` where tests live in `@Nested` classes.
- **SonarQube follow-ups (security).** Stdio MCP package caches (npm/uv) move from predictable `/tmp/mcp-*` roots to an owner-only directory, `MCP_PACKAGE_CACHE_DIR` (`app.mcp.package-cache-dir`, default `~/.vegaduta/mcp-package-cache`; Compose/Helm now mount `/home/agentic/.vegaduta` writable for it and for the coding sandbox); every E2B sandbox is created with public traffic to its URLs restricted and reached with the `e2b-traffic-access-token` header (`E2B_RESTRICT_PUBLIC_TRAFFIC`, default `true`; a create that returns no token fails closed); the tenant-schema identifier rule now lives in one place (`TenantSchemaGuard.requirePlainIdentifier`, and `quoteIdentifier` refuses anything else); the e2b template docs describe the V2 `e2b template create` flow and why the sandbox user keeps passwordless sudo (E2B re-grants it at build time; isolation is the disposable microVM).
- **An API pod no longer stops answering for a minute under a sudden burst of requests.** `PlatformBroadcastService.current()` (the maintenance-mode gate that runs on every `/api/**` request) reloaded its 10-second snapshot inside `synchronized (this)`; the reload is a database read, and on JDK 21 a virtual thread that blocks inside a monitor pins its carrier thread. Requests that arrived while one thread reloaded queued on the monitor and pinned carriers too, a burst pinned all of them, the reloading thread waited for a connection held by a request that could not get a carrier, and the pod stopped answering (probes included) for 40 to 130 s, once until its liveness probe killed it. On the kind stage a step from 2 pods to 40 long streams failed 0.6 to 24% of requests in 5 of 6 runs (no first byte for about a minute; the ingress logged 499); with this change 0 of 4. The reload now uses a `ReentrantLock` and a caller that finds a stale snapshot while another thread reloads serves the stale one at once. This was found while testing the "cold pods" hypothesis for the scale-out stall: the stuck requests were always on a pod that was already serving, never on a pod the HPA had just added, and the measured cold-pod penalty is about 1.5 s on the first wave. `tests/load/kind/step.sh` reproduces the step and `tests/load/kind/pinned-threads.py` reads `-Djdk.tracePinnedThreads=full` output. See `docs/architecture/HOT-PATH-PERFORMANCE.md` section 4d.
- **Async hops that lost the tenant, found by an audit of every executor, `CompletableFuture`, Reactor scheduler and raw thread in `core/`.** Three hops ran tenant work off the request thread without the tenant. The A2A task's terminal audit row (`a2a.task.send`) was written from the reply stream's completion callback on a thread with no `TenantContext`, so the write failed against the fail-closed schema and every A2A task went unaudited. The live-call voice turn ran each sentence's text-to-speech (voice lookup, credential and quota reads) and its audio-ledger write on `boundedElastic` and the stream-completion thread without the tenant, so a tenant's replies degraded to text-only. `VoiceRelayWebSocketHandler` set the tenant inside `Flux.defer` and cleared it in `doFinally`, which runs on another thread, so the pooled `boundedElastic` thread kept the tenant after the subscribe and ran unrelated work under it. `EvalEngine.evaluateAsync` (no production caller yet) ran the judge evaluators on a thread without the tenant. Each now captures a `TenantScope` on the originating thread and re-applies it on the worker. New `TenantScopedFlux.subscribeInScope` for Reactor subscriptions. Every other site was traced and is documented in `core/app/src/test/resources/archrules/async-hop-allowlist.txt` with the reason it is safe.
- **Webhook and billing job pools lose egress to the tool executors.** `core-webhooks` and `core-billing` run no agent turn but their NetworkPolicy allowed egress to `sidecar`, `code-intel` and `tool-sandbox`. It is now limited to postgres, valkey, keycloak, seaweedfs, core and mcp-stdio-runner, DNS, the configured integrations and the external ports. `core-intake`, which runs agent turns, is unchanged. The chart READMEs give the reasoning (including why `mcp-stdio-runner` stays) and `test-guards.sh` covers it.
- **A busy core pod is no longer killed by its liveness probe.** A load test saw a core pod exit 143 after `Liveness probe failed: context deadline exceeded` while it was only CPU-saturated. The liveness probe of every core-family chart (core, core-jobs, core-worker, core-ingest, core-webhooks, core-intake, core-billing) is now period 10 s, timeout 10 s, 12 failures (about two minutes of continuous silence; it was 15 s, 5 s, 5, and a restart still follows a dead or wedged JVM). Readiness stays at 5 s and 3 failures so a busy pod leaves rotation first, and the startup probe is unchanged. `deploy/helm/test-guards.sh` fails if the liveness tolerance or the readiness speed regress.
- **Knowledge ingestion heartbeats fail fast.** One heartbeat thread serves every in-flight claimed job of a pod, so a hung JDBC call (for example a row lock held by a stuck writer) stopped all heartbeats and led to a mass reclaim. The heartbeat statement now has a query timeout (`app.knowledge.ingestion.claiming.heartbeat-timeout-seconds`, default 5); `docs/configuration.md` states that mixing claim-mode and legacy replicas is unsupported beyond a single rollout.
- **The agent-version pin no longer leaks across pooled threads.** `AgentVersionPin` is a ThreadLocal cleared only by `TenantContextFilter`, so a pin set or left on a pooled/scheduler thread could stamp a wrong `agent_version_id` on later work. `TenantScope.capture()` now carries the capturing thread's pin (or none) to the worker, and every non-empty `TenantScope` restores the worker's previous pin afterwards.
- **A disabled or edited user-script tool stops at once.** `UserToolScriptCallback` evaluated the expression held in the cached per-pod snapshot, so a disabled or edited script kept running for up to `app.chat.hotpath.config-lookup-ttl`. The snapshot now carries the id and `call()` re-reads the row (enabled and expression), failing closed with an error JSON, like the custom-code and OpenAPI callbacks.
- **A tenant schema without `llm_spend_month` no longer fails every BYOK turn.** The budget check read the counter through a repository call that marked the shared transaction rollback-only before the check's own fail-open `catch` ran, so the commit threw `UnexpectedRollbackException` out of `resolveClient` (HTTP 500 for every BYOK turn of that tenant when V481 had not been applied). The counter and the SUM fallback are now read by `LlmSpendReader` behind a JDBC savepoint on the caller's connection; an unreadable counter falls back to `SUM(llm_call_log)`, logs at ERROR and increments `llm.spend.counter.unavailable`. At startup `LlmSpendCounterSchemaCheck` logs at ERROR and sets the `llm.spend.counter.missing.schemas` gauge when an ACTIVE tenant schema lacks the table.
- **Tenant webhooks for streamed replies, and the sampled quality judge, no longer run in the wrong schema.** `WorkflowEventPublisher.publish(..., tenantId)` is the overload for callers on a thread with no `TenantContext` (the stream-completion thread), but it fanned the event out on that same context-less thread, so `WebhookDispatchListener` queried the default schema (`relation "webhook_subscription" does not exist`) and no `agent.message.completed` webhook was recorded for a streamed reply. It now applies the given tenant for the in-JVM fan-out and the outbox write and restores the thread's previous context afterwards. The same completion callback also started `LlmQualityJudgeService.sampleAsync` without the tenant, so the judge's `llm_call_log` rows failed with `relation "llm_call_log" does not exist`; the context captured on the request thread is now re-applied around that call. `AgentMessageEvents` also binds the tenant around the publish. `WebhookDispatchListener` now refuses (rate-limited WARN, event type and tenants only) when the thread's tenant is missing or differs from the event's, so a wrong-tenant thread can never write a delivery row carrying another tenant's envelope; its failure log no longer prints the signal (reply excerpt) or a stack trace. `StreamEndExecutor` also clears `AgentVersionPin` after each task. The session-history summariser task now uses `TenantScope` instead of a bare `set`/`clear`. Both original failures were closed (no cross-tenant read). Covered by unit tests and `TenantContextOnAsyncHopsIT` (two tenant schemas, real Postgres).
- **The dev-only local coding runner no longer creates its workspaces in the shared system temp dir.** `LocalCodingSandbox` (runner `local`, opt-in via `CODING_HARNESS_ALLOW_LOCAL_RUNNER`) runs agent-generated code, so its workspace and scratch HOME now live under an owner-only (`rwx------`) parent that must belong to the process user: `CODING_HARNESS_LOCAL_WORKSPACE_DIR` (`app.coding-harness.local-workspace-dir`), default `~/.vegaduta/coding-sandbox`, instead of `java.io.tmpdir` where another local user could pre-plant, read or swap them (SonarQube java:S5443). Paths returned and cleanup on close are unchanged.
- **Chat turns no longer stall under concurrency (regression from the once-per-turn query embedding).** `QueryEmbedding.vector()` made the sidecar call inside `synchronized`; on virtual threads that pins the carrier thread, so at about 32 concurrent turns on one tenant the carriers were all pinned, the database connection pool showed 12 of 12 in use with an idle database, and turns waited 20-30 s or failed. It now uses a `ReentrantLock`. Measured on the kind stage at 32 concurrent users: 99% failed turns before, 0% after.
- The Keycloak reconcile (`scripts/vegaduta up`) now merges the web clients' redirect URIs and web origins instead of
  replacing them: entries added by hand are kept and the derived one is added when missing (entries for a previous
  domain are not removed). `deploy/README.md` described the old behaviour.
- `scripts/vegaduta doctor`/`up` (and `vegaduta.ps1`) reset `CADDY_AUTO_HTTPS=on` in an existing `.env` to empty:
  older `.env.example` files shipped it and Caddy refuses to start with it.
- Helm: the core NetworkPolicy now opens the port of `global.objectStorage.azure.endpoint` for `type: azure-blob`
  (a Private Endpoint was blocked); a literal `value` for a secret name (`REDIS_PASSWORD`, the Kafka JAAS line, the
  Azure connection string, ...) in the core, core-jobs or core-worker `extraEnv` list is now refused like it is in
  `env`; `values.schema.json` accepts and validates `global.valkey.tls`/`username`, `global.postgresql.age` and the
  `global.kafka` SASL keys, and the chart rejects `valkey.tls` with the bundled Valkey, a Kafka SASL mechanism
  without a SASL protocol, and SASL without a JAAS source.
- `deploy/helm/charts/*/VALUES.md` regenerated (it was missing the Azure, Valkey TLS and Kafka SASL keys), with a
  gate (`deploy/helm/scripts/check-values-docs.py`) that fails when the umbrella `values.yaml` has a key the table
  lacks.
- Streamed responses (SSE) of delegation-token callers now get a `TenantContext` on the ASYNC dispatch too: the
  delegated authentication is saved on the request like the JWT one is. `vmcp_` grant-key callers of `/v1/**` are
  still not covered (their filter also rate-limits and reads the body, so it is not re-run on that dispatch). If
  the tenant is stopped while a stream is in flight, the already committed response is no longer written to (it
  threw `IllegalStateException` from the filter); the dispatch just ends.
- Azure Blob storage: a missing container is now a backend error, as a missing S3 bucket is, instead of a
  "not found" that looked like an ordinary 404. There is still no `demo-images/` lifecycle expiry on Azure (it is an
  account-level ARM policy the application cannot set); [configuration](docs/configuration.md) says how to create it.
- LiveKit call recording with `STORAGE_CREDENTIALS=default` or `azure-blob` now fails at the request with a clear
  message instead of being accepted by LiveKit and failing at upload with the MinIO default keys. The MinIO default
  credential WARN is no longer logged for `azure-blob` or `local` storage.
- Recording the skipped Apache AGE migration ran outside Flyway's lock, so replicas starting at once on a database
  without AGE could in principle clash (primary key on `installed_rank`) or rethrow the AGE error. Recording is now
  idempotent and a replica that finds it already recorded retries. Hardening only: three concurrent starts against
  a real pgvector-only server, four rounds, did not reproduce a failure on the old code.
- The tenant migration Job no longer starves for connections: the parallel schema migrations (default 4) are capped
  to what the Hikari pool carries (2 connections per Flyway run plus the lock connection), and the Helm migrate Job's
  pool is 10 instead of 8.
- A hosted-pool provider row with an undecryptable key or a blocked base URL is reported once per row, not on every
  FREE-tier turn.
- AWS S3 with `STORAGE_CREDENTIALS=default` now works with EKS IRSA: the AWS SDK loads its STS credentials factory by
  reflection and answered "the 'sts' service module must be on the class path"; the `sts` module is now a
  dependency of core/storage, with a classpath test.
- The Apache AGE skip no longer hides a fixable problem: V44 is recorded as skipped only when the server's own
  catalog (`pg_available_extensions`) says `age` does not exist (and the failure is SQLSTATE 0A000), not on any
  message that mentions the extension. An extension that is offered but refused (not allow-listed, permission
  denied) still fails the migration.
- `TenantScopedFileStorage` recognises another tenant's `tenant/<id>/...` key even when it contains a line break
  (the ownership pattern now matches across line terminators).
- Compose `voice` profile: the LiveKit media range (UDP 50000-50100) was hardcoded, so a host process on any of
  those ports (for example `adb`) made `up` abort and leave core, Caddy and web unstarted. The range is now
  `LIVEKIT_RTC_PORT_RANGE_START` / `_END` (same defaults), applied to both the server and the published ports.
- Compose `tracing` profile: `langfuse-s3` was OOM-killed by its own healthcheck (`weed shell`) at the 256 MB limit
  and stayed unhealthy on a busy host, which made `scripts/vegaduta up --wait` abort and leave core, Caddy and web
  unstarted. Its limit is 512 MB and the healthcheck timeout 20 s.
- Platform issues raised while a streamed (SSE) chat response finishes, such as an upstream provider error, no
  longer fail with `relation ... does not exist`: the tenant context filter now also runs on the async dispatch,
  where the finishing thread previously had no tenant.
- The hosted LLM pool no longer fails every FREE-tier chat because one row's API key cannot be decrypted (a
  plaintext placeholder, or a key encrypted under an encryption key this deployment no longer has): that row is
  logged and skipped, like a row that fails the SSRF guard. The local LLM seed's plaintext placeholder is
  documented as the limitation it is.
- `EncryptionService.decrypt` answers a value that is not ciphertext (a plaintext placeholder, bad base64,
  too short to hold an IV) with the usual `Failed to decrypt secret` error instead of leaking a raw
  `IllegalArgumentException` / `NegativeArraySizeException`.
- `core-worker` failed to start without an `applicationTaskExecutor` bean, and `core-jobs`/`core-worker`
  failed health-group validation because the `tenantMigration` readiness contributor only existed in the
  API process. The worker defines the same executor as the API and jobs, and the readiness indicator moved
  to `core/tenancy`, which every process includes.
- Compose `tracing` profile: Langfuse web crash-looped on "JavaScript heap out of memory"; its memory limit
  is now 1536m with a 1280 MB Node heap.
- The background-jobs and Temporal-worker processes (`core-jobs`, `core-worker`) crash-looped at startup:
  two services in modules those shapes include injected REST controllers (`MemoryImportService` in `agents`,
  `ChannelControlPortImpl` in `omnichannel`), and those shapes do not load controllers. The shared logic now lives
  in `UserMemoryFactService` and `ChannelStatusService`. `PluginArtifactService` was moved onto a new
  `PluginCatalogService` in the same change, but it lives in `app`, which the jobs and worker shapes do not
  include, so it was a layering cleanup and not a cause of those crashes. An architecture test
  (`ControllerLayeringRulesTest`) fails the build if a non-controller injects a controller or controller advice
  again (fields, type arguments such as `ObjectProvider<XController>`, constructors, `@Bean` and `@Autowired`
  methods).
- Compose `observability` profile: Grafana and Promtail never started, because they waited for a Loki
  healthcheck that cannot run in the distroless Loki image (no shell). The healthcheck is gone and they wait
  for Loki to start. `tracing` profile: the Langfuse worker stayed unhealthy because it listened only on the
  container IP; it now binds all interfaces (`HOSTNAME=0.0.0.0`).
- `/api/system/status` no longer logs `relation "workflow_event_outbox" does not exist` on every
  call: the Kafka section's outbox counts ran on probe threads with no tenant and queried the
  public schema. The tenant is now re-applied there, and the cached reading is kept per tenant.
- The domain overlay's default `CADDY_AUTO_HTTPS=on` made Caddy refuse to start (`on` is not a Caddy
  `auto_https` value), so Let's Encrypt mode never came up. The setting is now empty by default (automatic
  HTTPS) and only `off`, `disable_redirects`, `disable_certs` or `ignore_loaded_certs` are written out. The bare
  domain and `www.` now redirect to the console.
- Caddy no longer crash-loops when `ACME_EMAIL` is empty (the `email` directive is now emitted only when
  set), and `CADDY_AUTO_HTTPS=off` now works behind a tunnel or TLS proxy: set `CADDY_SITE_SCHEME=http://`
  too so the sites listen on :80 (without it nothing listened on :80 at all).
- `scripts/vegaduta up` re-asserts the web and platform-admin client redirect URIs from `PUBLIC_APP_URL` and
  `PUBLIC_ADMIN_URL`, so changing the domain or ports after the first start no longer ends in
  "Invalid parameter: redirect_uri" at sign-in.
- Helm: the schema-migration hook Job no longer fails on a fresh install. Its database pool was 2
  connections, which the core's scheduled tasks used up while the migrator waited (60 s timeout, exit 1, so
  `helm install --wait` reported failure). The migrate pool is now 8 (`core.hikari.maxPoolSize` overrides it).
- Admin Issues now records "platform-hosted LLM pool exhausted" and the
  provider-fallback issues (fallback triggered / fallback also failed). They
  were written from reactive threads that carry no tenant context, so with
  `app.tenancy.fail-on-missing-context=true` the insert hit a schema that does
  not exist and was swallowed as a warning: operators never saw them.
- A chat turn that failed without producing text left the question with no
  answer in the reloaded transcript: the "failed reply" entry was written
  under the raw client session id instead of the server-derived one the turn's
  other entries use, so no reader ever saw it (and it landed in the unscoped
  keyspace session scoping exists to avoid). It is now stored with the rest of
  the conversation.
- Reloading a chat transcript, exporting a chat session and chat branching
  (edit, regenerate, switch) work again: they looked the session up by the
  raw client session id instead of the caller-scoped id chat stores it under,
  so every call answered 404. Another member who knows the session id still
  sees nothing of it, and a branch tree is now bound to the person whose turns
  it records.
- The voice-agent image starts again: its lock had resolved `openai` 3.x,
  which no longer brings in `httpx`, so `import livekit.agents` failed.
  `openai` is held at 2.x until livekit-agents supports 3.x.
- Agent payment tools (create payment link, UPI collect) work again: every
  call failed because the cap reservation ran outside a transaction, and a
  gateway failure could not release the reserved cap.

### Security

- Request bodies on nine endpoints that declared validation constraints but
  lacked `@Valid` are now validated; payment checkout and the developer API
  no longer echo raw exception text; private-address detection uses real
  address ranges (172.16/12, not all of 172/8); the remote MCP pre-probe goes
  through the address-pinned HTTP client, closing a DNS-rebinding window.
- Knowledge-base writes (document ingest, upload, delete, re-embed, collection
  create/update/delete, RAG eval) and face enrollments now require the
  "manage agents" permission; listing or enrolling faces also requires a
  grant on the agent. Commerce catalog writes and payment checkout require
  "manage billing". Before, any tenant member could change the shared
  knowledge base every agent retrieves from, pull every face template, or
  reprice what the tenant sells. The authorization coverage test now also
  checks controllers that are only loaded under a Spring profile or property
  flag, which is how these were missed.
- Compose: SeaweedFS leaves the `observability` network. Its master, volume
  and filer APIs have no authentication, and that network also carries
  Grafana, Loki and Langfuse; Prometheus now scrapes it over a dedicated
  two-member network. The code-intel Docker daemon (runs tenant code) gets a
  memory ceiling and a process cap (`CODE_INTEL_DOCKER_MEM_LIMIT`,
  `CODE_INTEL_DOCKER_PIDS_LIMIT`). The search profile requires
  `SEARXNG_SECRET` (now generated by `scripts/vegaduta secrets`), mounts its
  config read-only and runs with `no-new-privileges`, a memory limit and a
  healthcheck.
- code-intel, app-builder and rl-trainer accept `KEYCLOAK_ADDITIONAL_ISSUERS`
  (also `<SERVICE>_KEYCLOAK_ADDITIONAL_ISSUERS`) like core does, so a domain
  migration no longer answers 401 from those services while core accepts the
  same token. Their `*_KEYCLOAK_ISSUER` no longer defaults to localhost: with
  `*_AUTH_MODE=keycloak` and no issuer set, token requests answer 503.
- The CI job that boots core mints a throwaway `APP_ENCRYPTION_KEY` per run
  instead of carrying a literal key in the workflow.
- Web console: the plain-HTTP-to-HTTPS redirect now targets the request's own
  URL instead of the `Host` header, which anyone reaching the origin directly
  could set to another site (open redirect). `/api/eval/*` is gated to
  development like `/eval`. The chat stream parser caps what it buffers for
  one line or one event (4 MB) instead of growing without bound. The legal
  page escapes the configured contact address before rendering it as markup.
- Platform-admin console: the Content-Security-Policy no longer allows
  connections to any `wss:` host (it opens no WebSockets).
- Both consoles: a production build with `NEXT_PUBLIC_API_BASE_URL` or
  `NEXT_PUBLIC_KEYCLOAK_URL` unset no longer falls back to `localhost`
  silently (the fallback was copied into ~20 files); `publicApiBase()` /
  `publicKeycloakUrl()` keep the dev default outside production and report
  a missing value once.
- Granting or revoking an agent payment mandate requires the billing
  permission (tenant-admin by default). Any signed-in member could do it.
- Closed authorization gaps any signed-in member could use: answering
  another user's pending workflow form (now needs run access to that
  workflow), commanding or revoking any paired phone, publishing or taking
  down any studio app, spending LLM budget on or accepting suggestions for
  any agent's improvements, and re-routing email, Telegram and voice
  channels or unpairing live conversations.
- Users whose only role is end-customer can no longer make console changes:
  they can chat with agents (text and voice) and manage their own account and
  notifications, nothing else. A user with any other role is unaffected.
- More console writes now require the matching permission instead of any
  signed-in member: creating agents and workflows (agent permission; a
  delegated user may still create agents and edit its own), duplicating agents
  and importing templates, script tools (tools permission), ad-hoc channel
  sends and device pairing (channels permission). Skills, human handoff and
  moderation decisions already checked these in the handler; the security
  configuration now records them too.
- Changing an agent's content guardrails needs the guardrails permission;
  sharing or submitting a community template needs the templates permission;
  cloning or deleting ElevenLabs voices needs the channels permission, like
  the ElevenLabs key; running the decision playground needs the integrations
  permission. The console shows these controls as read-only, hidden or
  disabled, with the reason, for members without the permission.
- Ownership checks inside handlers: stopping a chat turn works only on the
  caller's own session (and now also stops turns routed into an A/B
  experiment); cancelling a job needs to be its requester or an editor of the
  agent; reading or deleting what an agent remembers about someone else needs
  editor access; a vision session needs chat access to start and can only be
  ended by whoever started it; a collaborator agent can only be added by
  someone who can run it; a chat session's prompts and costs are visible to
  the person who had the session and to the agent's editors (anyone who knew
  the session id could read them); pointing an agent at code-intel repos or
  giving it a code workspace needs the tenant-admin or agent-builder role, like
  the rest of code intelligence.
- In-app call recording can only be started or stopped by the person the call
  was issued to. Anyone who knew a room name could record that call.
- Rating an agent's reply (thumbs, rating, edit) needs chat access to the
  agent and only works on your own turns; feedback can no longer skew another
  user's reward or plant answers in the preference export. Importing a chat
  turn into an eval dataset copies the recorded turn only when it was yours.
- Approving or rejecting a workflow step's approval needs run access to that
  workflow (any member except the requester could decide it).
- Request body caps and password length limits; rate limits on previously
  unthrottled public endpoints (limiters never fail open).
- The page Content-Security-Policy reports violations to `/api/csp-report`.
- Skill supply-chain scanning, content-hash pinning and a review gate;
  `http_request` safe mode (https only, allow-lists, named credentials).
- Binary GET responses are no longer decoded as text, which corrupted
  downloaded generated documents.
- New configuration endpoints need the matching tenant permission: agent
  hooks (`MANAGE_GUARDRAILS` or tenant admin), tool argument policies
  (`MANAGE_GUARDRAILS`), MCP tool exposure (`MANAGE_INTEGRATIONS`), agent
  import, judge-calibration sets and trajectory expectations
  (`MANAGE_AGENTS`), workflow import (`MANAGE_WORKFLOWS_ADMIN`). Chat
  edit / regenerate / branch switching only works on the caller's own session.

### Performance

- **Tenant sweep cost measured, idle-skip decision recorded** ([Tenant sweep cost](docs/architecture/TENANT-SWEEP-COST.md)). One jobs pod with one idle tenant makes about 1.0 tenant-scoped database calls per second, which grows linearly with tenants (extrapolated, not run); idle-skip covers about 22% of them and adds up to 30 s to agent-job and webhook pick-up, so it stays off by default. New counter `vegaduta_tenant_sweep_tenants_total{job,outcome}` and a test that proves the staleness bound of idle-skip at every position of its back-off. The on/off comparison and a many-tenant run were not done.
- **Post-merge benchmark numbers** for the whole turn (two replicas, 1/8/32 users, caches on and off) in `docs/architecture/HOT-PATH-PERFORMANCE.md` section 4b, with the raw results next to the harness.
- **Hot-path performance, third step: each per-turn fact is looked up once.** A chat turn read the tenant's plan up to a dozen times (preflight, the inbound guardrail check, the gateway's budget and rate-limit checks for every client it built, and one per RL dimension on a Playground turn), evaluated the tenant's feature flags three times (version pin, effective-agent overlay, experiment variants), resolved the MCP tools a second time just to learn whether any are offered, and embedded the user message twice (agent memory, then tenant-shared memory). The plan and the flags are now resolved once in preflight and handed down, the tool resolution returns its MCP answer with the tool list, and the two memory recalls share one embedding (`QueryEmbedding`). Defaults are unchanged and every path that does not hold the value still looks it up as before. `ChatTurnLookupCountTest` locks the per-turn counts on a real `chat()` turn; `HotPathQueryBudgetIT` measures the plan and flag lookups on a real Postgres. `app.chat.hotpath.plan-cache-ttl` stays `0s`: with the plan passed down it could save at most one lookup per turn. The outbound guardrail check and the delegated-call, specialist and workflow clients still read the plan (listed in `docs/architecture/HOT-PATH-PERFORMANCE.md`). Milliseconds were not measured.
- **Per-turn latency harness and first measured numbers.** `tests/load/scripts/turn-overhead.js`, a mock Ollama-compatible model server (`tests/load/mock-llm`) and `tests/load/kind/bench.sh` measure platform overhead per chat turn in milliseconds on a local kind stage, with no model cost. First results (one laptop, mock model): about 50 to 60 ms of platform overhead per idle turn; the hot-path caches save about 20 ms per turn at idle and about 40% throughput at 8 users. Method, table and limits in `docs/architecture/HOT-PATH-PERFORMANCE.md`.
- **Hot-path performance, third step: tool resolution reads nothing when warm.** The last eight transactions of tool resolution (Google connection status, the hosted image pool check, skills metadata and settings, user scripts, custom code tools, OpenAPI tools) now go through the same `HotpathLookups` cache as immutable snapshot records (never a detached JPA entity), evicted on a write to the underlying entity on the same pod (new `@PlatformConfigEntity` marker for the platform-wide hosted pool rows). The tool callbacks of custom code and OpenAPI tools re-read their row when the model calls them, so an edit or a disable applies to the next call and no source code or secret sits in the cache. Warm tool resolution for one turn of a default agent: 8 to 0 connection checkouts, 11 to 0 statements. `HotPathQueryBudgetIT` now also counts a whole turn from the pre-flight checks to the first token against a mock model (warm: 15 connections, 14 statements; cold: 44 and 46) and runs with `app.chat.hotpath.config-lookup-ttl=0s` to prove the old behaviour. Same TTL setting as before (`0s` = off).
- **Hot-path performance, second step.** The "is this configured" checks the tool resolvers make on every turn (any MCP server enabled, ElevenLabs, Sarvam and E2B credentials, image and video providers, payment mandate, specialist tags, platform tool credentials) now go through a small tenant-keyed read-through cache (`HotpathLookups`), evicted on a write to the underlying entity on the same pod. `app.chat.hotpath.config-lookup-ttl` (default `3s`, max `30s`, `0s` = off) bounds staleness on other replicas. Warm tool resolution for one turn of a default agent: 23 to 8 connection checkouts, 26 to 11 statements, measured by `HotPathQueryBudgetIT` (now with a cold and a warm number). The scheduling-role skip no longer logs a line for every bean when a pool skips a broad package; it logs only beans with scheduled methods.
- **Hot-path performance** ([Hot-path performance](docs/architecture/HOT-PATH-PERFORMANCE.md)). A chat turn's 16 read-only agent lookups (tool allowlist, autonomy tier, capability flags, knowledge scope, run budget) were each their own database transaction, and every transaction costs three round trips because of the per-tenant `SET search_path`. They now read an immutable per-pod snapshot, evicted on every agent write on that pod. Measured on a real Postgres for the tool-resolution step of one turn of a default agent: 52 to 23 connection checkouts, 55 to 26 statements. `app.chat.hotpath.agent-projection-ttl` (default `3s`, max `30s`; `0s` restores the old behaviour) bounds how stale another replica can be. Authorization and edits never read the cache. New `HotPathQueryBudgetIT` records the budget so a new per-turn query has to be justified.
- Opt-in `service.trafficDistribution` (`PreferClose`) on the Helm Services and `examples/values-low-latency.yaml`, to keep pod-to-pod calls in the caller's zone (Kubernetes 1.31 or later). Not measured on a multi-zone cluster.

### Docs

- **Java Agent SDK docs:** compliance mapping, security review pack, adoption playbook and positioning pages under `docs/sdk/`; `sdk/java/README.md` gains a "What's new" section and a "governance you can prove" walkthrough (kill switch, autonomy, shadow policy, tamper-evident audit, evidence export).
- `docs/platform-documentation/` (the `agentic-ai`-era HTML handbook) is now labelled legacy: the Markdown `docs/` tree is the maintained source and wins on disagreement.
- `docs/repository-structure.md` now marks module maturity (core path vs deeper vs early/niche) and documents the `eval`/`quality` split.
- Docs: [repository structure](docs/repository-structure.md) (layout, audiences, how the platform, public pages, SDKs, plugins and docs are linked, current hosting state). `docs.yml` runs on doc-affecting changes; `sdk-release.yml` stays tag-dispatched by design.
- **Documentation site**: `docs/` is built with MkDocs Material (`mkdocs.yml`, `make docs-serve`) and published to GitHub Pages by `.github/workflows/docs.yml` (strict build on every pull request). New pages: SDKs and dependencies, plugins/apps/mobile, site modes, SDK release procedure. A hook (`scripts/docs/mkdocs_hooks.py`) embeds the SDK READMEs and rewrites links that leave `docs/` to GitHub URLs.
- Docs: README "Deploy anywhere" and "Status and honesty" sections, corrected skill (23) and workflow node type (35)
  counts, a "Good first contributions" list and the quick path, formatting and integration-test notes in CONTRIBUTING.md,
  and a documentation issue template.
- `scripts/ci/docs-link-check.sh`: offline check of relative links and anchors in `README.md`, `CONTRIBUTING.md` and
  `docs/`, part of `make gates` and the static-gates workflow.
- Docs: [cloud portability ADR](docs/architecture/ADR-cloud-portability.md): the open-protocol decision, a per-cloud
  dependency matrix with what is verified, and the ordered list of gaps.
- Docs: [run it locally and see the infrastructure](docs/operations/local-stack-guide.md): tiers, profiles and
  ports, Docker commands, and a kind walkthrough with `kubectl` and `k9s`.
- Docs: [Kubernetes architecture](docs/architecture/kubernetes.md) (namespaces, isolation, workload security,
  scaling, verification status) and [hosting behind a Cloudflare Tunnel](docs/operations/tunnel-hosting.md);
  a namespace baseline example (`deploy/helm/examples/namespace.yaml`: Pod Security `restricted`, quota, limits).
- Documentation: an authorization model page (`docs/security/authorization.md`),
  an operations index with an `APP_ENCRYPTION_KEY` rotation runbook, an ADR
  index, the security-hardening settings and Compose-only variables in the
  configuration reference, corrected MCP registration guide, rewritten
  `web-admin/README.md`, and the conversation-ownership model in the handbook.
- Docs: the DNS the default Let's Encrypt mode needs now includes the apex and `www.`; `PUBLIC_BIND_ADDR` is in
  `.env.example` and the tunnel notes; stale image versions and the LiveKit port range in the appendix pages.
- `deploy/seed` README and scripts no longer say the raw-SQL mock-provider seed makes FREE-tier chat work; it
  inserts a key core cannot decrypt, so the mock has to be registered through the platform-admin API.
- Helm README: which cloud identities reach object storage under the default NetworkPolicy (IRSA and Azure workload
  identity yes; IMDS and Pod Identity need an `extraEgress` rule), and that `core`, `core-jobs` and `core-worker`
  each need the ServiceAccount annotation.
